mr president, it is never easy being a small country, especially one whose territory has historically been marched through back and forth numerous times simply as a result of being surrounded by more influential and larger neighbours. nonetheless, luxembourg has managed to overcome these obstacles, emerging as a major actor in the process of european integration with much greater weight than its size might otherwise suggest. the previous semester has undoubtedly been a difficult one, especially due to the severe worsening of the migrant crisis and the terror attacks in paris. i hope that the recently approved measures aimed at strengthening external borders and combating internal radicalisation will very soon produce viable long-term results. the data protection package, the setting up of the european pnr system and the reform of the court of justice of the eu are also among the more positive outcomes. i can only regret that my own country, latvia, was not able or willing enough to emerge with results of the same scale and long-term impact during its presidency of the council of the eu just before luxembourg.
the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the following areas: the digital single market. that's a lot of words. we probably understand that in this room, but what do those words really mean? let's say we could go to, say, munich and buy a white painting from munich. i can go to riga, another can go to london, stockholm, lisbon and eat it there. it seems perfectly normal. at the same time, from riga, with a paid subscription, say, on television or in the electronic environment of the internet, i cannot enjoy it in munich, london or dublin. why would you do that? with goods, we have largely dismantled barriers, borders between member states, but in the digital field, which will become increasingly important in our economy, we still have these barriers, which are largely based on copyright issues, including patents. so what do we do? we must remove these barriers, we must ensure that copyright and patents are valid throughout europe, not just in one member state. otherwise, we'll only be able to go to munich to eat the minhenes. we think that's ridiculous. it's just as absurd that to enjoy content on the internet, we have to be in one country and we can't be in another country. thank you for your attention. (the president agreed to reply to the question on the blue card (rule 162 (8)).)
answer to the blue card question. thank you for your question, colleagues. when we talk about a single market, we are talking about a digital single market. it is already the case, and on behalf of the european people's party we argue that we must remove barriers in all areas that restrict trade. so in this case, we're debating today about the digital realm, which is very important. of course, it's also very important in education. and we still have barriers in the market for goods and services. so, on behalf of our group, we argue that we must remove the obstacles.
in writing. ‒ this vote to waive the immunity of a member of the european parliament concerns the infringement of the law by polish member of parliament, mr czeslaw adam szekirska, in connection with a speeding offence committed on 10 january 2015 in a built-up area. mr šekirska himself acknowledged that this infringement had taken place a year earlier, but chose to exercise the immunity legally granted to his meps so that the prosecutor general of the republic of poland could not legally investigate this speeding by bringing the criminal proceedings in question. while there is no need to abolish the practice of granting immunity to members, any person - especially an important official such as a member of the european parliament - who has not only committed an illegal act but has also admitted it himself, needs to be held accountable for his actions on the basis of the principles of the rule of law. it was therefore logical to vote for the waiver of immunity.
in writing. ‒ this vote to waive the immunity of a member of the european parliament concerns the infringement of the law by polish member of parliament, mr czeslaw adam szekirska, in respect of a speeding offence committed on 19 april 2015 at a place where the legal speed limit is 50 km/h, compared to 79 km/h registered for the car driven by mr szekirska. in other words, this case is in full agreement with the previous precedent of speeding that occurred three months earlier. as in the previous vote, mr šekirska acknowledged the fact of this infringement but chose to use his parliamentary immunity to prevent the criminal proceedings against him. i consider that, at least from a moral point of view, such conduct was not entirely acceptable in this case, and i therefore voted to waive the member's immunity until all the relevant criminal proceedings have been completed and the case has been closed following a formal settlement.
mr president, i would like to thank you for your time. the main objective of this draft legislative resolution was to promote a more effective common fisheries policy for bluefin tuna stocks in the atlantic and mediterranean. this is to be achieved by supporting traditional fishermen using various types of artisanal fishing gear, such as the almadraba hook, whose main advantage is their low impact on ecosystems. given that bluefin tuna is classified as an endangered species, a series of measures will be implemented in order to avoid any deterioration of its rather fragile balance, thus making it even weaker. recreational and sport fishing will inevitably have to take account of different rules, such as the mandatory release and return of juveniles. the amendments introduced in general, alongside the repeal of the regulation, should become another mechanism for standardising the rationalisation of the european union's fisheries policy, and this is welcome. thank you.
mr president, i would like to thank you for your time. the competition policy of the european union is to some extent one of the most crucial community-wide sectors. it affects the eu's economic development, including its promotion of progress in the social sphere and international relations. in business, it plays one of the most important roles. but striking a more effective balance between defending consumers' interests and improving the business climate in the internal and external markets is what this report seeks to achieve in the future through the recommendations it contains, which are addressed primarily to the european commission. reforming the digital economy, fostering social cohesion, fostering closer cooperation through national competition authorities, streamlining the role of the banking sector and taxation policies - not only all of these, but also more effective antitrust patenting and much more was mentioned in this report. that is commendable. all these cases must contribute to the implementation of our union's competition policy, so that we can guarantee both the interests of our internal consumers and internal competition. thank you.
in writing. ‒ the european union's competition law is one of the cornerstones of the internal market. it aims to provide a fair environment for business, whereby consumers receive goods and services at market-conform prices and quality, and entrepreneurs receive clear rules of the game. unfortunately, in practice, things don't work out as they should. we still have monopolies and cartels in the european union, which have caused between 181 and 320 billion euros in economic losses. the work of the european commission, which monitors compliance with competition law and restrains violators by bringing charges against them and imposing penalties, is therefore important. for example, the us company microsoft has been fined €1.68 billion by the european commission. the european commission has also indicted the russian state-owned company gazprom for abusing a dominant market position in eight eu countries. i therefore believe that the european commission's capacity to investigate and monitor such serious infringements must be further strengthened to ensure that the laws are respected and to prevent unfair competition which harms not only consumers but also many honest businesses. in the light of the above arguments, i vote in favour of the resolution.
in writing. the existence of cartels causes losses of eur 181320 billion each year, i.e. about 3% of the european union's gdp. tax evasion, tax fraud and tax havens, in the form of uncollected revenues, cost eu taxpayers around eur 1 trillion a year. i believe that every effort should be made to eliminate distortions of competition between taxpaying and non-taxpaying companies. nor are distortive national tax models that create unequal competition between companies, in particular large multinational companies and smes, and between member states where such tax models are not applied. the implementation of a fair eu competition policy will not only help to overcome these problems, but will also increase consumer welfare and raise the standard of living of every citizen of the european union. i therefore hope that the resolution adopted today will help to establish a competition policy which will ensure the successful completion of a single european market in which consumers, wherever they live, will be able to obtain the same quality of goods or services without having to pay for them because of unfair competition and unjustified market restrictions.

in writing. ‒ at the european parliament plenary session in strasbourg, i voted for a resolution highlighting the impact of external factors and the obstacles to women's entrepreneurship in europe. i support the promotion of gender equality and believe that important steps have already been taken to achieve equal rights and development opportunities for women and men. i voted against the resolution's paragraph on the introduction of mandatory gender quotas in the member states because i believe that the demographic situation in each member state is very different and therefore i do not believe that the introduction of mandatory gender quotas is the solution to promoting female entrepreneurship in europe.
mr president, i would like to thank you for your time. i am also very pleased to vote for this report by mr plura, because it is extremely important. youth unemployment is one of the main problems in the european union economy as a whole, and as far as my country, latvia, is concerned, youth unemployment has become a real tragedy in my country. consequently, any measure, any method, any approach, any policy and any policy-making to reduce youth unemployment must be welcomed. the unfortunate thing is that the main thing employers now want is for an employee to be 25 years old and have the same seniority. obviously, that's not possible. and if you don't have experience, you don't get hired, and you don't get hired because you don't have experience -- that's a loophole. we have to seriously pursue this particular vector, this vector, to reduce youth unemployment not only in latvia, not only in eastern europe, but also throughout the european union, because in my view this is the number one problem.
in writing. ‒ youth unemployment is a very serious problem in the european union and effective solutions must be found to keep it from rising. the figures speak for themselves in 2014, the eu youth unemployment rate was 21.4%, which was twice the overall unemployment rate in the eu. the crisis has affected employment sufficiently and therefore the eu needs funds to improve the situation in the field of youth employment in the european union. young people are the future of the european union and for this future to succeed, the european union needs policies that promote the entrepreneurship and skills of young people. on the basis of all the above arguments, i supported this resolution of the european parliament.
in writing. ‒ i supported the european parliament's resolution on moving towards a digital single market because i believe that digitalisation has a huge potential to boost the european union's economic growth by boosting gdp growth, creating a good investment environment and creating thousands of new jobs. while the single market in its traditional form is already functioning in the european union, fragmentation still exists in the digital market, with 28 different laws in each member state. this is unacceptable and not in the interests of the eu's citizens. uniform rules will facilitate cross-border trade, give businesses greater choice in cross-border transactions and reduce administrative burdens, especially for small and medium-sized enterprises. the resolution also stressed the need to find a solution to ending geo-blocking and price discrimination based on location. when a resident buys a service or a good online, he or she should be entitled to enjoy that service regardless of his or her location, credit card issuing country or nationality.
in writing. - e-video has become an integral part of our lives. it is therefore only logical that the opportunities and potential that have been uncovered should be used to have a positive impact on the daily lives of europeans. this applies to all sectors affected, from business and education to culture and security. at present, even with the gradual development of the transnational eu-wide system i.e. the digital single market , there are still 28 separate relevant policy-making and implementation systems, leading to certain obstacles such as restricted consumer options, as well as more active development of security systems due to the existence of several, often completely separate systems. maximising digitalisation, more standardisation of vat, encouraging efforts to boost safety and the implementation of the remaining steps would not only have a huge impact on the eu's economic development, but also on other sectors such as information exchange, education or ecology, through the use of less wood due to the higher degree of digitalisation of more materials. the main thing, of course, is to guarantee a constant and maximum stable supply of electricity in parallel.
in writing. at a time of declining economic growth rates in the world and a tendency for manufacturing to move away from europe, existing and new resources must be used to accelerate development. i believe that the time is right to use the experience and knowledge in the digital economy for development, especially in the new member states, such as latvia, which is already emerging as a leader in the digital economy. the digital economy needs a digital single market based on common european union rules to flourish. a number of barriers preventing smes, start-ups and growth companies from taking advantage of the opportunities offered by new technologies such as big data, cloud computing, the web of things, 3d printing and others, need to be removed. it is important for consumers to end geo-blocking, or restricting online content based on the location of the customer, and to strengthen consumer protection online. unfortunately, today, not only does the availability of audiovisual material vary from country to country, but also the rights of buyers are not protected when buying digital content online, such as mobile applications. it will also make it cheaper to deliver parcels across borders, which are currently often inadequately priced or not available at all. it is also essential to promote public confidence in online business, or e-commerce, by ensuring the protection of personal data and by creating common rules for businesses and consumers across europe.

in writing. ‒ at the european parliament plenary session in strasbourg, i voted on the draft council decision for latvia to start the automated exchange of vehicle registration data (trd). i believe that this is a welcome decision which will help the latvian law enforcement and other security authorities to work much more efficiently, will ensure an even better functioning of the data protection system and will increase the security of the citizens of the european union. data protection and the automated exchange of information between the competent authorities of the eu member states is a very topical issue in the light of the events of the last year and can be seen as a positive step towards a safer europe. in order to improve the fight against terrorism and organised crime, it is necessary to ensure the effective exchange of data and information at least within the union, while respecting the protection of citizens' data and their right to privacy.
in writing. ‒ strengthening crossborder cooperation is one of the cornerstones of the european union's integration policy and covers several areas, including transport and, in particular, vehicle policy. as a member of the european parliament representing the republic of latvia, it was a great honour for me to become the shadow rapporteur for this draft council implementing decision, especially in view of the potential benefits that this practice could bring to both my home country and the partner countries. the automated exchange of vehicle registration data will not only increase the effectiveness of this area in latvia itself, but will also contribute to the development of relations with other eu member states, and - which has recently become much more relevant and important than before - will boost the presence of safety within our union. each country must contribute to the advancement of its art in this area.
in writing. i am pleased that latvia has successfully implemented the pilot with the netherlands and has introduced all the necessary data protection rules and will thus be able to participate in the eu's automatic exchange of vehicle registration data system together with other eu member states such as belgium, sweden, germany, the netherlands, lithuania, estonia, poland. the accession of latvia to the single automated vehicle registration system is of particular importance in several respects. it will ensure cross-border cooperation and help to prevent serious crime or terrorist threats, if any. it is of the utmost importance that latvia now has the possibility to call on other national police officers 24 hours a day, seven days a week, when necessary, to ensure public order and security or to prevent crime, for example during mass events. i have always stressed that the security of the country and its citizens must be one of the priorities, and i hope that latvia's accession to the car registration system will increase the security of latvian citizens and help prevent and investigate criminal offences and terrorism in other european countries.
the presumption of innocence is one of the most important cornerstones of jurisprudence. it is legal in the bible, and it is, in fact, a principle that works worldwide. the idea is the main one and it must be implemented, and i support and vote in favour of today's decision. it should be said that the inadmissibility of the use of force during the execution of criminal proceedings, the inadmissibility of describing a person as a criminal in public or in the mass media, and that it is the prosecution that must prove its guilt in court. it is the absolute right of the accused to be present in the courtroom during the trial. further general implementation of these principles is needed by our community. i repeat, i voted in favor. it will help to realise and strengthen our values, because the rule of law is the property of any healthy state and society.
the community-wide legal framework for the placing of personal protective equipment (ppe) on the single market has been in place for more than a quarter of a century. however, it is clear that, given the progress made since 1989 both in union legislation in this area and in the technical development of iuds, it is clearly time to modernise the council directive which also regulates the placing of iuds on the single market of the european union. i support this approach because personal protective equipment, which is actively used not only by professionals such as firefighters or soldiers but also by other people who for some reason have become dependent on it, must meet a number of different requirements, firstly in terms of quality and use. i therefore welcome this proposal for a regulation of the european parliament and of the council, which aims to promote these aspects of the use of ida, and i repeat that i voted in favour.
in writing. i am pleased that this regulation has led to the creation of harmonised rules aimed at better protecting users while reducing the amount of bureaucratic requirements that manufacturers have to meet. the use of personal protective equipment - crash helmets, safety harnesses, life jackets - is a matter of public health and even public life. these devices, which are used every day in many workplaces and sports, must be absolutely safe and perform their functions. the use and manufacture of these products has changed a great deal over the last 25 years since the development of the existing eu safety standards for personal protective equipment. the working environment in which these products are used has changed, as have new and often more extreme sports activities, which are impossible without these products. i am therefore pleased that we have adopted rules that will significantly modernise existing legislation by setting higher safety standards. at the same time, it is important that the bureaucratic burden is not imposed on manufacturers whose products, such as decorative tarpaulins and dishwashing detergents, raincoats or clothing with decorative reflective elements, are not designed for extreme conditions.
while some eu citizens use wood, coal, petroleum or specially processed, organic and other residues for heating purposes, gas fuels remain the most popular fuel. home heating, water heating, transport fuel given the frequent daily proximity of consumers to gas-fired appliances, i naturally welcomed this desire to improve the safety situation. the exposure to gas, firstly, flue gas, or any other substance, can cause serious harm to human and domestic health. therefore, greater unification of transnational legislation with the aim of standardising quality across all 28 member states is necessary and, of course, welcome.
in writing. in latvia, an average of 60 to 70 patients with smoke inhalation are admitted to hospital each year at the beginning of the heating season. the most common cause of these life-threatening poisonings is a gas boiler leak. i am pleased that we in the european parliament have developed stricter rules on gas installations. as required by the committee on the internal market and consumer protection, manufacturers will be required to design gas installations so as not to cause the accumulation of carbon monoxide or soot or other substances harmful to health. this regulation will make boilers, gas stoves, grills, toaster ovens, outdoor heaters and heating boilers much safer. the law also stipulates that only professionals may install and operate any type of gas equipment. it should be noted that there are currently around 470 million gas-fired boilers in the eu, and this number is growing more than 30 million are sold each year. in latvia, there is also growing interest in autonomous gas heating in homes.
the european union is the world's leading region in the use of gravel road equipment. some 17 500 local installations account for 60% of the world total. the european union is also a leader in the production of such equipment. it is therefore entirely logical and correct that the new regulation should thus aim to replace the old directive on rolling stock for the transport of persons. the renewal of the relevant legislation, in parallel with efforts to take account of the latest standards in passenger safety and technical regulations, forms the basis of the new regulation. this is important, and it is important that the new regulation does not affect historic structures, agricultural and forestry equipment, and other equipment used for recreational purposes only and not for the transport of persons. i'm positive about this!
in writing. ‒ the european union often operates a system of generalised tariff preferences in its relations with developing countries, under which exporters from these partner countries are able to pay lower tariffs on goods exported within the eu or not to pay any at all. the commission has already adopted a number of measures to ensure that the union's financial interests are protected. this vote was largely due to the fact that kyrgyzstan, which applied for the gsp+ scheme for its exporters, has still not made changes in its domestic policy on human rights, inter-community relations, labour legislation and other issues. it has historically been the case that eu foreign policy, including trade policy, is often dependent on value considerations. and here the problem is that the kyrgyz authorities' actions over a long period of time have not been in line with these values, although some progress was certainly made a few years ago. therefore, as long as the situation remains unchanged, it is not appropriate to include kyrgyzstan in the list of gsp+ countries, nor to approve the abovementioned commission delegated regulation. it is clear that there is a need to support our partner countries, but only if there is also some active movement against it.
in writing. ‒ while every human being needs to eat to stay alive, perhaps the most vulnerable group in this respect are children, especially infants, for whom food quality and maximum safety are of paramount importance. i therefore welcomed the motion for a resolution adopted by the european parliament in response to the commission's delegated regulation of 25 september 2015. the protection of infants and young children against obesity, the absolute minimisation of sugar levels in line with the standards of the world health organisation, the total prohibition of pesticides, the most transparent labelling system possible and, of course, the maximum transparency of the whole system - if we want our future generations to be healthy, then safety must be guaranteed in an area with which every person is in regular contact throughout his or her life and in which there can be no gaps in principle.

madam president, i would like to speak about the eu-ukraine agreement. its entry into force was postponed until 1 january 2016 due to russian objections. it is an irony of history that now its future is challenged not by mr putin but by dutch citizens. however, i want to believe that one of the world’s most advanced nations, among the first in europe to embrace modern freedoms, a trade and technology powerhouse and leader on human rights, will support the freedom aspirations of another european nation and will let the freedom of trade and human exchange advance in the relations between the european union and ukraine. we should never forget that the eu shares over 1 300 kilometres of border with ukraine. that is why a secure and prosperous ukraine is in our own strategic interests. stability and trade across this border means more secure borders for the union. ukraine is an important trade partner for the european union. it is the largest european country, with fairly well developed industrial, agricultural and technology sectors. the agreement allows european businesses free access to this huge market. we have to be aware that a ukraine closely associated with the eu is the best safeguard against the revisionist powers who want to redraw european borders.
madam president, the last decade has undoubtedly proven difficult for georgia. the august 2008 war severely worsened relations with russia and these have only recently started to recover from economic difficulties. yet despite all this georgians have achieved the almost impossible: numerous successful reforms, especially of the judiciary, the strengthening of democracy, economic development – all of which contributed to the eventual conclusion of the eu-georgia association agreement and will soon result in the finalisation of the visa liberalisation dialogue. the fact that some of my colleagues regularly criticise georgia, often on issues which have actually long been resolved, is most regrettable. the same applies for our resolutions, colleagues. what in the world has happened to quality writing? we purport to be the citadel of democracy, yet whom are we trying to lecture here? georgia, which is the cradle of european civilisation, with its millennia-old traditions and statehood? if we really are who we purport to be, a standard of proper conduct has to be upheld in everything we do here in parliament.
mr president, our debate today is a stark reminder that, six years after the entry into force of the lisbon treaty, there are still no implementing arrangements for the mutual defence clause and its invocation has largely been symbolic and political rather than practical. unlike for article 5 of the north atlantic treaty, there is no contingency planning and no structures at the european level for the implementation of the clause. the response to attacks in paris and the whole developments of the last few years have proven that we do need a european defence union, which would include countries that wish to cooperate closer on defence matters, to move ahead on the legal basis of permanent structured cooperation. we also need a permanent european civil military headquarters tasked with contingency planning, including for collective defence, in close cooperation with nato. in fact, closer eu-nato cooperation is key to defending our states, societies and infrastructures in the future. we do not need to reinvent the wheel, but jointly prepare for all kinds of attacks, be they traditional military or highly sophisticated non-military. this is exactly where the eu has a key role to play.
mr president, just several decades ago different parts of europe were plagued by internal conflicts with various nationalistic groups. those times are over. the advent of the globalisation age has removed any meaningful boundaries. terrorism is no longer linked to any borders; it is both global and much more radical than even before. like in northern ireland or spain, there can be no peaceful resolution. the only reasonable way to combat the current wave of radicalisation in europe, including active propaganda and isis membership, is a complete overhaul of the existing policies on integration, judiciary and prosecution, and security of financial transactions, as well as counter-propaganda in the case of those susceptible to such radicalisation, not to mention a more assertive foreign policy in relation to those who openly or allegedly contribute to these processes. i mean saudi arabia, turkey, qatar or anywhere else. i am not proposing to forsake and abolish our core values. our first and foremost objective is to guarantee the safety of our citizens, not endanger them even more.

in writing. ‒ on 17 december 2015, the european parliament voted on the establishment, competence, membership and term of office of the committee of inquiry into emission measurements in the automotive sector. the basis for this step was the recent scandal revealed by the us environmental protection agency, in which the german carmaker vw admitted that for years it had effectively left its cars with both diesel and petrol engines in the testing phase for co2 measurements (i.e. some 8 million cars are currently registered in europe). the indicators in these tests were several times smaller than in road tests because the specially installed hardware diverted the test to automatically edited emission measurement results. one of the main objectives of the committee will be to understand why it was the us authority that exposed this breach and the long-term deception to millions of consumers, and not some authority in the european union. it is hoped that the members appointed to the committee of inquiry will not only be able to analyse the facts and evidence available with sufficient speed and scrupulousness, but will also be as independent as possible from any outside influence. any fact-finding exercise must be entirely objective, rational and fair, at least insofar as it concerns both ecology and consumer rights and health.

mr president, i would like to thank you for your time. following the horrific terrorist attacks in paris on 13 november last year, france became the first country in the history of the european union to invoke article 42 (7) of the treaty on european union (teu) as a 'mutual defence clause' rather than article 222, the solidarity clause. it is to be welcomed that, in principle, all the other member states have in one way or another withdrawn and have given france various forms of support. it could not be otherwise, because the fight against terrorism cannot be fought in one country alone, and solidarity must be shown not only in the form of solidarity but also in the form of concrete assistance. since no country has invoked this clause before france, it will be necessary to pay attention to its invoking system and to possible improvements in the near future, because if something similar happens, god forbid, this mechanism must be operated quickly and legally. so i'm with you. thank you.

mr president, ethiopia represents one of the most ancient and culturally rich civilisations. its diverse ethnic and religious community, which has managed to live through millennia side by side, was something to admire not so long ago. in the last few decades, however, the situation has strongly deteriorated, with numerous human rights violations severely hampering social, economic and political progress in the country. the recent events surrounding the expansion of the ethiopian capital, addis ababa, have become just another example of large-scale civil rights violations. harassment of opposition, crackdowns on protesters, limitations on civil society and the media, the political under-representation of most ethnic groups and internet censorship – this is, most regrettably, the current reality in ethiopia. the eu is a major financial donor and provider of assistance to ethiopia and must step up its efforts, effectively addressing this issue by using every available instrument at its disposal. ethiopia has the potential to become considerably more than it is today and our task is to lend a helping hand. the current situation, however, is no longer beneficial and it has to change as soon as possible.

in writing. ‒ about 10 years ago in march 2006 regulation (ec) no 562/2006 of the european parliament and of the council was adopted, the aim of which was to introduce a community code on the rules governing the movement of persons across borders. in february and april last year, the working party of legal experts of the european parliament, the council and the commission concluded that the commission's initial proposal for a schengen borders code was fully acceptable, as it did not in any way amend the existing texts, but merely consolidated them and thus codified them. in other words, today's vote was intended to raise the legal force of existing legislation by further integrating it into the new regulation. it is hoped that this will increase the efficiency of the schengen area, which has become more of a challenge in principle than ever in the past year.
in writing. ‒ the convention on long-range transboundary air pollution, signed in 1979 and entered into force in 1983, became the first international agreement of its kind. the 1998 aarhus protocol specifically noted the need to reduce and control more closely the levels of lead, cadmium and mercury discharges. today's vote was geared towards adopting the 2012 amendments, which provide for stricter emission limits for dust emissions from certain large stationary sources. this also applies to flexible transitional arrangements which will help to some extent the parties that will accede to the amended protocol by the end of 2019 including the countries of eastern and south-eastern europe. the new amendments also provide, inter alia, for an annual national emission report, which will allow for more effective control of the emission levels in each country. it should be noted, however, that while the adoption of these amendments is undoubtedly an important step, it is imperative that as many countries as possible not only join in putting these principles into practice, but also implement them in the most equitable way possible. otherwise, there will be no real positive effect on the environment.
in writing. ‒ while the previous vote referred to heavy metals as environmental pollutants, this one was about persistent organic pollutants, which are often still used in agriculture (as pesticides, insecticides), electrical engineering (as insulators), motor vehicle manufacturing, the chemical industry, oil refining and many other places. all these elements, while serving and serving various kinds of desired outcomes, are, in general, highly polluting and harmful to health. as with metals, the agreement is to reduce emissions to 1990 levels or equivalent levels from any year in the decade 1985-95. the adoption of the amendments is a crucial step towards more efficient ecological management and, of course, public health, especially in view of the high prevalence of respiratory diseases worldwide - and not only in europe.

in writing. ‒ this particular request for waiver of the immunity of a member of the european parliament arises from the fact that polish member of parliament, mr roberts jaroslavs ivaškevičs, allegedly drove at a speed of 79 km/h in the city on 7 september 2014, whereas the speed limit in urban areas in poland is only 50 km/h. mr ivashkevich himself confirmed that he was indeed driving a speeding car on that date. on 13 august 2015, following a request from the polish road traffic authority sent to the president of the european parliament via the prosecutor general of the republic of poland and the conclusion of the european parliament's legal committee, a decision was taken to waive the immunity of mr ivaškević. i agree with this decision because it is necessary to respect all the principles mentioned in the legislation of the republic of poland and appropriate to situations of this kind and mr ivaškeviča should not be in conflict with the laws of his native country.
in writing. ‒ as in the previous vote on the waiver of the immunity of mep robert jaroslav ivashkevich, this case also concerns speeding. however, unlike in the first case, mr ivaškeviča did not in any way confirm that he was driving at 94 km/h in a 60 km/h zone, although his face is clearly visible in the photograph taken during the exceeding of the speed limit. on the basis of the same as in the first case, namely the request of the polish road traffic directorate sent to the president of the european parliament via the prosecutor general of the republic of poland on 13 august 2015 and the conclusion reached by the legal committee of the european parliament, a decision was taken to waive the immunity of mr ivaškević. i would agree with the decision because no member of the european parliament can have any unresolved conflict with the judicial system of any country.

in writing. biodiversity is seriously threatened both globally and in europe. it is increasingly affected by climate change, increased consumption, and the increasing use of transport and energy resources. human activities are increasing pollution, increasing co2 levels and causing species to become extinct. surveys show that more than 80% of europeans are concerned about the loss of biodiversity, which calls for a responsible environmental policy. preserving biodiversity is important for our health, well-being and well-being, as well as for the health, well-being and well-being of our children and grandchildren. often, in our day-to-day lives, we do not realize how much we depend on such precious ecosystem services as fresh air, clean water, and the raw materials we use to build our homes, for example. i believe that in the future, sustainable financing mechanisms for the protection of biodiversity should be provided. of course, investing in nature and biodiversity requires money, but if we do not act responsibly, the losses in the future will be much greater. we must therefore do everything we can to tackle the root causes of biodiversity loss and to integrate biodiversity into sectoral policies, including agriculture, forestry, fisheries, energy and transport. i believe that the report adopted today will help to achieve the objectives of the eu biodiversity strategy and will prevent biodiversity loss, thereby preserving the planet as clean as possible for future generations.
in writing. ‒ this vote does not in any way concern the temporary suspension of any specific tariff preferences for the republic of moldova it is simply the addition of a specification within the framework of the eu-moldova association agreement, which has already become a standard addition to documents of this type. the commission proposal was, inter alia, supplemented by the need to organise consultations with moldovan representatives, which must take place within 30 days, before any suspension. if no compromise is reached within this period, only then will the commission be able to implement any temporary suspension of tariff preferences. as this vote concerns a perfectly logical amendment to the text, rather than the temporary withdrawal of moldova's trade preferences, there was no reason to vote against.
in writing. ‒ the eu-georgia association agreement from the outset included a circumvention mechanism, which allows georgia to re-introduce the most-favoured-nation tariff rate of duty if imports of certain agricultural products from georgia exceed a certain volume without a proper justification of their precise origin. the commission should be able to verify that the conditions for the granting of the union financial assistance are met. the commission proposal also provides that it will submit a public annual report on the application and implementation of the regulation each year. the commission has also proposed that the council should adopt a directive on the protection of the environment against the risks of chemical pollution caused by certain dangerous substances. in other words, the purpose of this vote was not to introduce any kind of restrictions on eu-georgia trade merely to clarify existing principles and rules, and there was therefore no reason to vote against this proposal and the amendment it contained.
in writing. ‒ the marrakesh agreement, signed on 28 june 2013, the full title of which is the marrakesh agreement to facilitate access to printed works for persons who are blind, visually impaired or print disabled for other reasons. the idea was to make it easier to obtain the permission of copyright holders to print texts in specialised formats that could be read by the visually impaired and print disabled. in the case of the eu in particular, the problem is that while the eu and its member states signed the treaty in april 2014, there are still a total of seven countries that effectively block full ratification of the treaty. currently, only 5% of all books published in developed countries and less than 1% in developing countries are accessible to people with visual impairments. in 2010, there were 2.55 million blind and 23.8 million visually impaired people in europe a total of 26.35 million. in other words, the fact that we still do not fully ratify the marrakesh treaty and start implementing its principles in a meaningful way is simply absurd and totally illogical, given the long-standing efforts of the eu and its member states to guarantee the maximum rights for all its citizens.
in writing. ‒ as i supported resolutions b8-0135/2016 and b8-0134/2016, i also supported and voted for the european parliament resolution calling for the withdrawal of the commission implementing decision authorising the placing on the market of products containing, consisting of, or produced from genetically modified soybean fg72 (mst-fgø72-2). the european commission has to come up with a better proposal on licensing procedures. the current proposal does not address the concerns often raised about the potential health and environmental harm of gmos. the impact of products containing gmos on humans and the environment is not yet sufficiently understood and there is too much uncertainty about gm products.
in writing. ‒ this objection relates to the commission's wish to authorise the placing on the market of the genetically modified soybean mst-fgø72-2 developed by bayer cropscience ag as a food, food ingredient and feed ingredient. one of the reasons why the european parliament voted against was that these beans synthesise the protein 2mepsps, which confers resistance to glyphosate herbicides, and on 20 march 2015 the international agency for research on cancer classified glyphosate as a possible human carcinogen. in view of the above, and also of the fact that the commission proposal is not in line with union law, such as regulation (ec) no 1829/2003 of the european parliament and of the council, the amendments to which the european parliament refused to approve following the commission proposal in october 2015, it was not possible to vote on the commission proposal again, and therefore i voted for the ep's rejection decision.
in writing. i have always advocated that latvia and the european union as a whole should be free of genetically modified organisms (gmos). we cannot allow the health of our citizens to be jeopardised in the name of economic interests. it is regrettable that this is the latest occasion on which the european commission, contrary to the member states' position, wishes to give the green light to the placing on the market of new gmo products, using the powers conferred on it by law. gmo soybeans are resistant to glyphosate, which the international agency for research on cancer has classified as a possible human carcinogen. this time, the ec not only ignored national concerns about the harmfulness of gmos, but also ignored studies by the world health organization agency on glyphosate as a possible carcinogen. in the light of the studies carried out by the world health organisation agency on glyphosate and the negative attitude of the latvian public towards gmos, i voted against the placing on the market of these three gmo soybeans. furthermore, i believe that the existing legislative procedure should be amended to give greater decision-making powers to both the european parliament and the states when granting authorisations for the placing on the market of new gmo food and feed products, thus ensuring more effective democratic control and respect for the public interest.

in writing. ‒ about 4,5 years ago, monsanto europe s.a. submitted an application to the relevant dutch official authorities for authorisation to place genetically modified soybean mon-877ø5-6 × mon-89788-1 on the market. in view of the existence of a single market, this submission was forwarded to the commission, which would like to approve it. similar to the vote earlier today, these beans synthesize the protein cp4 epsps, which confers resistance to glyphosate herbicides, while the international agency for research on cancer has classified glyphosate as a possible human carcinogen. the fact that the existence of various genetically modified products and their components has already become a daily reality in our single market makes it categorically unacceptable to place products on the market without having gone through all the legally required consultations which the commission has not yet carried out. this is largely why the european parliament voted against the existing proposal. my vote was therefore returned for the ep's rejection.

in writing. ‒ in the wake of the recent car emissions scandal, the european commission has come up with legislation to test new car models for compliance with eu environmental standards. this legislation is a major step towards restoring consumer confidence, as the tests will be carried out in real driving conditions on the road, rather than in a laboratory as is the case now. if the european commission's proposal were rejected, we would lose many months or even years before a new regulation was drawn up. the current offer also gives a clear signal and rules for the automotive industry. for the above reasons, i voted against the european parliament's request to withdraw the legislation proposed by the european commission.
in writing. ‒ even if freight and other vehicles are excluded, large passenger and commercial vehicles represent one of the most important emission groups in terms of percentage, alongside industry, agriculture and various other sectors. therefore, the strictest possible compliance with and non-compliance with the emission levels (mg/km) set is of fundamental importance, especially in the european union, given our overall ecology and environmental protection policy, not to mention emission quotas. the vote concerned the recent draft commission implementing decision on which the technical committee on motor vehicles (tcmv) published its opinion on 28 october 2015. the commission notes that the new standard is based on the principle of the 'zero emission' principle, which is the principle that all new vehicles must be equipped with a vehicle with a low emission limit. as a result, the european parliament concluded that the draft implementing decision supported by the commission was not in line with regulation (eu) no 715/2007, let alone that the commission had in fact decided to go against several principles that had been largely defended throughout the years. i therefore voted unanimously in favour of the ep's decision to reject the commission's proposal.

madam president, after kosovo’s unilateral declaration of independence in early 2008, the eu almost immediately initiated kosovo’s european integration process. the same applies to assuming the mantle of chief intermediary between belgrade and pristina, and i think there are two key points. first, if kosovans truly want to become part of our family of nations, european values of respect towards democracy, freedom of speech, good governance and others have to be fully respected. this is not conditional – it is obligatory. second, kosovo and serbia have to reach a mutually-satisfactory compromise, paving the way to long-term peace and cooperation. serbians’ attitude towards kosovo is that of grieving, disgruntled people who have lost their historic homeland. this mutual understanding has to resolve this serious injury. and, of course, there can be no eu membership perspective for kosovo unless all our member states recognise it as independent. cyprus, greece, romania, as you know, slovakia and spain – 5 out of 28 – have not yet done it, each for their own reasons.

mr president, transportation is one of the key areas in both the european and adriatic macro-regional strategies. for example, the recent danube transnational programme, with a budget of eur 222 million, should not only contribute to more effective water-management and flood-risk prevention policies, innovation or cross-border institutional capacity but also the creation of a modern regional railway system accessible to both numerous urban and rural dwellers, as well as flight transport. the same applies to the adriatic macro-region. with its growing seaports and traffic, investment is necessary to maximise the region’s economic potential. the european structural and investment funds are just one of the possible sources. moreover, in parallel to upgrading cross-border transportation capacity, not only telecommunications, postal services, law enforcement and other public services would improve, but there would also be benefits for people-to-people contact, which, aside from the pure economy, is the most important goal of all.
madam president, revision of the pet travel scheme has, regrettably, not made the system more effective, due to frequent abuses. the introduction of a compatible system for the registration of pet animals across member states would make cross-border supervision of the animal trade, travel or pet vaccination much easier. as a result, these long-overdue changes, ideally combined with appropriate data exchange, would contribute to dealing with various disease outbreak threats, amongst other things. many people have to leave behind, sell, cast out or even euthanise their pets simply due to being unable to take them along with them when going abroad, precisely due to pet registration system differences in our countries, and for no other reason. i have to say that the cause of mutual integration within the eu has contributed to the gradual alignment of an identity documentation system for its inhabitants – passports, ids, diplomas and the like. the same should undoubtedly apply to our pet animals.
in writing. ‒ the hague convention on the service of judicial and extrajudicial documents in civil or commercial matters was signed in 1965 and entered into force in 1969, replacing the previously active convention on civil procedure adopted in 1905. the main objective is to guarantee the possibility for official bodies of all kinds and ordinary people to transmit official documents between countries without using diplomatic channels, thus issuing them in other signatory states. malta has already ratified this convention a few years ago, while austria has yet to do so after the relevant consent process has been completed in the institutions of the european union, including the european parliament. while i am somewhat surprised that these countries are the last in the european union not to have fully acceded to this convention, i welcome this step. the cross-border service of documents is an integral part of international jurisprudence and other fields, the effective implementation of which should be promoted in all possible ways, and today another very important contribution was made in this regard.
in writing. ‒ as in the case of liechtenstein two and a half months ago, today's vote concerned the european union-san marino agreement on the exchange of financial information on bank account holders. this agreement has the potential to foster more effective international cooperation in the field of tax policy and to promote the legitimation of the income of eu citizens directly through the full payment of these taxes. compared to other countries, san marino has never been a major player among low-tax havens such as liechtenstein itself or switzerland. however, the signing of this agreement is clearly of great importance, as it will not only increase tax transparency, but in principle stimulate further improvement of relations between our community of nations and san marino, and of course, eliminate another tax paradise, actual or potential.
in writing. ‒ more than four years ago, croatia became the 28th member state of the european union. since then, a series of different agreements, protocols and conventions have had to be brought into line with the nature of the enlarged community. the 2011 act of accession of croatia introduced a simplified framework that helps croatia and the eu to continue adapting the remaining agreements to the new digital reality. in this particular case, a positive vote will help to harmonise eu financial mechanisms and related policies ever more closely. croatia's accession to the conventions on the protection of the european community's financial interests is precisely of this nature and therefore it was logical to fully support it.
chairman! employment policy is one of the primary aspects of the european union's nature and therefore the rationalisation of the mobility system in parallel with the further integration of labour markets is entirely to be welcomed. i support that. it is logical that citizens of our countries, when working in another member state, should receive similar remuneration and, in general, the same working conditions as all other employees performing such tasks, irrespective of their nationality, that is, irrespective of their citizenship. if we really live in a single union and the well-being of each country and individual depends to a large extent on the others, there can be no discrimination in this respect at all, and it is commendable that this is included in this report. the eures portal has become increasingly active in recent years in helping hundreds of thousands of europeans to find work both in their own member states and in other member states. however, i agree that the current system needs a comprehensive reform.
chairman! since the change of regime in tunisia more than five years ago, its economy has embarked on a long-term path of reform, a process of restructuring. however, there are some sectors which remain as the primary national industries. before the revolution, it was the seventh largest city in the world. as in several other similar cases, a decision was taken today to support the tunisian economy by introducing a temporary quota for olive oil imports to the european union of 35000 tonnes (that's a lot!) for two years until the end of 2017. and this is welcome, at least because not only is tunisia one of the eu's most important partners in the south, but also because its assistance is in some ways boosting internal stability, growth, which indirectly affects us as well.
in writing. ‒ the european globalisation adjustment fund was set up almost a decade ago to provide financial support to european companies that have made redundancies or otherwise lost employees as a result of trade liberalisation. in the present case, this concerned 412 employees of two belgian glass companies, together with 100 young people who were not in employment. the total cost will be eur 1095544. human capital is the most important resource of any country and everything must be done to avoid its loss and to continue to use it productively. a positive vote today was needed to enable more than two thousand people to integrate or reintegrate into the labour market as quickly as possible and to continue to apply (and receive) and share with colleagues the experience gained over many years, thus positively affecting themselves and their surroundings and the european union economy as a whole.
chairman! the growth and development of the european union's economy is the cornerstone of the community's existence, and this report, which follows the commission's annual growth survey, is particularly welcome. promoting economic reforms, facilitating the exchange of views and debate among all stakeholders (including national parliaments, social partners, civil society) is essential for the successful and productive long-term recovery and further development of the union economy. i joined the rapporteur in saying that investment, fiscal policy and structural reforms should also be complemented by close monitoring of domestic demand. while the idea of a free market is in itself largely logical, it must be said that the specificity of the existing demand, which clearly affects all other areas, cannot be ignored. i'll back you up.
chairman! support for housing, continuing renovation, health protection, child and youth policy, elderly policy... although only partially mentioned in this report, this set of measures of fundamental importance is absolutely vital for the whole of the european union, and in particular for my country, latvia. i would like to emphasise once again the special emphasis on children and youth policy. because, to put it figuratively, if you don't pay attention to the development of a fruit tree or, say, a strawberry plant, there's a chance that nothing good will grow or grow but it won't be particularly edible. but children are the jewels of our lives, and we must do everything we can to ensure that they grow up happy, socially, spiritually, morally. this is very important. that's task number one. and that's why i supported this decision.
chairman! what's important about this report? there has to be transparency, there has to be openness. it's the same thing. the european standardisation system should have been updated and its use in the field of information and communication technologies increased. the principle of mutual recognition and its maximum respect and a fairer administration of public procurement are of fundamental importance, as the full functioning of the single market is simply not possible without effective reforms in these areas. the same, it should be said, applies to a clearer division of competences between the different levels of management in order to avoid duplication of functions. i'm all for it.
chairman! historically, relations between europe and tunisia have always been a very active regime, and in recent years this has only intensified due to the change of regime in this north african country. and our european union is tunisia's primary economic partner, especially in trade in agricultural products, and this cooperation is only getting stronger every year. it is therefore time to take the next step in our relationship, to intensify the negotiations on a potential free trade agreement. this is particularly logical given that the eu-tunisia association council met on 17 march last year and adopted an action plan for the establishment of a privileged partnership. and our community's efforts to develop relations with the eu-mediterranean partnership region will, i hope, be successful. so i'm with you.
chairman! in general, the ombudsman's primary task is to promote the protection of citizens' rights. if every member state of the european union has such a post, such an ombudsman or such a law-enforcement officer, then the european ombudsman has much broader responsibilities. for example, in 2014 specifically, issues such as fundamental rights, lobbying systems, transparency of public authorities, european union institutions, etc. were the focus of the ombudsman's attention, and this is very good, as, for example, 20% to 30% of all complaints were directly related to the principle of transparency and its breaches. for example, it is understandable to some extent that access to documents is restricted, but our citizens still have a right to know what is happening in the european union. for example, the eu-us transatlantic trade and investment partnership, which has been mentioned several times. why is the conversation confidential? it is imperative that we be as open as possible to our citizens.
chairman! the financial crisis of 2008-2009 was a crisis whose effects are still being felt, but the new, ever-evolving international reality often leaves the european union's economy in a periodic state of fever. the european central bank's policy in this respect is generally helping to overcome the consequences of this crisis in a sufficiently effective way. of course, there are distinct shortcomings of various kinds in its policies, such as different financial and credit policies for different member states. however, the european central bank's efforts to regulate inflation and stimulate economic growth cannot be ignored. i have to say that in my country, latvia, a prime minister called the pre-crisis years the lean years, and in order for us to return to the lean years, we have to stimulate investment in entrepreneurship.
chairman! although australia and new zealand are geographically very far away from the european union, there has been quite intense contact between the two sides since the beginning of the 20th century, and both australia and new zealand have been included in the western civilization, in the western countries. i would say that both australia and new zealand are now trying to diversify their trade links as much as possible, and in that respect they have a lot in common with us, the european union. given the production structures, the diversity of goods and the principles of economic policy management in both countries, a rapprochement with the european union is entirely possible. this is not fantastic or unrealistic, especially when it comes to regulating the law. the opening of negotiations on free trade agreements with these countries is therefore absolutely welcome, and i voted in favour.
chairman! pets are an integral part of our lives. they are our pets, sometimes our friends, helpers, allies. for lonely people, it may be the only friend they have. and of course, some people have not only cats and birds in their homes, but also camels, rats, guinea pigs. there are cases where people also keep crocodiles and rabbits, but anyway, if we are talking about the most endangered pet variants - dogs and cats - it is long past time to harmonise the maximum, to standardise the maximum existing registration systems in all our member states, because this would not only help to facilitate the possibility of transport across borders, but also help to fight, including, against animal smuggling, because we know that this problem exists in the european union, and it is extremely important.
in writing. unfortunately, there are cases in europe where pets are kept in poor conditions and illegally traded. illegally sold pets are not vaccinated, do not receive the necessary medicines in case of viral infection, and do not receive veterinary and sanitary care, which often results in treatment with antibiotics that can increase microbial resistance. these unvaccinated animals are dangerous to humans they carry more than 100 different diseases, including rabies. seventy percent of the new diseases affecting humans that have appeared in recent decades are of animal origin. the eu national databases in which each member state registers pet data are not yet compatible. consequently, there are limited possibilities for tracing animals that are moved within the eu. i therefore support compatible eu requirements for the identification and registration of pet animals. they will improve animal welfare, public and animal health protection and reduce the falsification of animal documents and illegal trade.

chairman! the health and effective upbringing of our children, their education in school - that is the cornerstone of our union, that is our future. no children, no future. you know that. i therefore support the view that the provision of fresh fruit, vegetables and milk - both regular milk and lactose-free milk - in schools is a very important and meaningful set of measures. the positive thing is that if we do that, we can also support our european union farming sector in a parallel way, particularly in my country, latvia, where the sector has suffered greatly from the introduction of russian counter-sanctions, and in general cheese, bananas, yoghurt, nuts and fresh food are much healthier than lemonades, chips and everything else that is sold in our schools that contains chemicals. in total, the annual funding will be eur 250 million - eur 150 for fruit, eur 100 for milk - and i hope we will also ensure a fair distribution of funding between member states. help, thank you!
in writing. ‒ at the european parliament's plenary session in strasbourg, i voted on a proposal for a support scheme for the supply of fruit and vegetables, bananas and milk to educational establishments. i consider that promoting children's health is a very important task in the european union and therefore this initiative is to be supported. the rules adopted in plenary provide for the merger of the eu school milk and fruit programmes and an increase of their annual budget by eur 20 million. member states will be able to choose or participate in this eu programme on a voluntary basis, provided that healthy eating habits and training on healthy eating are also significantly promoted at national level. this issue has also recently become relevant in latvia, and i believe that additional funding will help many member states to both raise awareness of healthy eating and introduce healthy eating habits.
in writing. school milk and fruit programmes are a great way to teach healthy eating habits that children unfortunately do not always learn at home. statistics also show that children eat unhealthily and are too sedentary more than 20 million eu children are overweight and teenagers on average eat only 30% to 50% of the recommended daily intake of fruit and vegetables. the situation in latvia is not good either. studies show that one in four primary school pupils is overweight. i am therefore pleased that we have been able to allocate an additional 20 million from the eu's annual budget to the school milk and school cereal programmes. given the increased funding and the revised criteria for allocating funds, including the level of development of the regions, we can expect latvian schools to be beneficiaries. i believe that this programme should not only provide products in schools, but also promote healthier eating habits, stimulate local food chains, organic farming, sustainable production and the fight against food waste. schools should also ensure that children are kept in touch with farming, for example by visiting farms. i believe that this programme will be both an immediate and a long-term support for our farmers, who are currently facing difficulties in realising their production, especially in the dairy sector.
chairman! of course, historically and today, ports play a huge, enormous role in the european union's economy, and it is clear that a more transparent, fairer system of governance is entirely welcome. for example, as we know, the commission has recently launched an investigation into the cases of financial support for ports in france, the netherlands and belgium, and of course we need new rules, but we cannot ignore national specificities and we cannot fail to point out that we cannot make a comparison, for example, between the port of rotterdam in the netherlands and the tiny, micro-small port of skultė in my country, latvia. and of course, national specificities also play a huge role in how ports operate. and we can't under any circumstances forget about the rights of workers, because the unions tell us that unification is absolutely impossible. that's why i voted to postpone.
in writing. ‒ i voted in favour of the proposal for a regulation of the european parliament and of the council establishing a framework for market access to port services and financial transparency of ports, as i believe that the regulation will help to attract additional investment and also ensure the development of latvian ports. when we go to the store to buy something, we can find out, for example, what the product is made of, where it comes from, and other information that may be useful. similarly, investors before they make an investment, they want to know the rules of the game. investors must be clear about how prices for the use of infrastructure and other services are set. the port authorities are therefore required to provide transparent service charges. the port authorities will also have to ensure financial transparency in the use of public funding. i believe that the latvian people will benefit from the transparency of our port activities, and that such improvements will attract investors who will develop them and create new jobs, which is why i supported the proposal.
in writing. i believe that the third step has been taken by the european parliament to prepare a proposal for a regulation on market access to port services and financial transparency systems for ports, which is acceptable to all parties. it is clear that there is a need to improve transparency in the european union, including latvia, in relation to the setting of port charges and the public funding received. where the port authority receives public funding, it must be transparent and separate accounts must be kept for the activities financed from public funds. the establishment of a single port system in the eu would not be appropriate, as port systems in the member states are highly diverse, with many different models for the organisation of port services and port management. nor do i think that the introduction of free competition between eu ports is right. such a system would put shipping companies in a privileged position, putting particularly small ports, including latvia, at risk. it should be noted that a large number of shipping companies are not eu companies at all. for example, the setting of charges for the use of port infrastructure is certainly left to the port administration. the commission notes that the port authorities have not provided any evidence that the port is in a state of disrepair.
in writing. ‒ in the vote on improving the efficiency of port operations, i supported the european parliament's position on the port services regulation and its further development. although this is the third attempt to regulate port services at european level and there are still a number of contradictions, the commission's proposal has been considerably improved by the european parliament, and i thank the rapporteur and the shadow rapporteurs for the long search for compromise. in terms of content, i would like to emphasise that important improvements have been made in parliament's position, such as the absence of the principle of free competition in the regulation of port services, thus recognising the diversity of ports and allowing the different models of port management and organisation of services currently established in the member states at national level to be maintained. i also appreciated the fact that the port authority could set the charges for the use of the port infrastructure autonomously. in the case of port charges, on the other hand, a number of fundamental principles of transparency and non-discrimination will have to be respected. in the case of public funding, i consider that the transparency and the determination of the transparency of the use of funds, by separating the activities carried out with public funds, is proportionate and will not be a significant obstacle to the development of ports. the european parliament's position adopted today provides a good basis for negotiations with the council with a view to adopting the final version of the regulation.
in writing. ‒ the current system for calculating the hicp is based on regulation (ec) no 2494/95. the new regulation thus aims to replace the old one by modernising it to incorporate the innovations in union decision-making introduced by the treaty of lisbon, which will allow the european commission to adopt implementing and delegated acts through eurostat. the latter will, inter alia, help to define appropriate methodologies for the compilation of harmonised indices among member states and to modernise the weights of the sub-indices of the harmonised index of consumer prices. due to the specificities of the member states, sub-indices will not be necessary in cases such as drugs, instant lotteries, prostitution, life insurance and indirectly measurable financial intermediation services. on the other hand, it will be necessary for sampling and representativeness, price collection and analysis, substitution and quality adjustment, index preparation, revision, special index and in the case of products processed in specific areas. in a slightly simpler way, one of the main tasks of the new regulation, by effectively adapting its principles to the practice of the member states, will be to guarantee a fair pricing policy as far as possible, so as to disadvantage consumers, particularly the most vulnerable, such as pensioners or needy families.
chairman! the overall impact of non-fraud related irregularities increased by 36% in 2014 compared to 2013. and, in turn, the number of known infringements rose by 48% to over 16,000. add to this the fact that, according to a recently published report by the european court of auditors, the level of error spread on the expenditure side of the community budget for 2014 amounts to 4,4% or more than 6,3 billion of the total expenditure. it's a huge amount of money, and it was spent very inefficiently. the losses due to corruption at union level now amount to €120 billion, and these are only official figures. we don't know anything about unofficials, colleagues. the commission's proposal to make it compulsory to publish all documents is therefore welcome and i support it. thank you.

in writing. ‒ at the european parliament plenary session in strasbourg, i abstained when voting on the report on an integrated approach to gender equality in the work of the european parliament. i support the promotion of equality between women and men and believe that important steps are being taken more and more often and that there is progress. however, as regards the disproportionate requirements for the provision of a quota for women at all levels of the european parliament, i consider that binding rules only hamper efficiency and are disproportionate. i also believe that the rights of women and men are already respected and taken into account in parliament. there is equality between women and men in parliament, and everyone has the same rights and opportunities. i support an integrated approach to gender equality, but i believe that the report places too much pressure on parliament and could harm the efficiency of its work.

madam president, there are numerous cases known where children, even below the age of criminal responsibility, commit terrible crimes going far beyond theft or vandalism: murder, rape, assault, kidnapping. nonetheless, they have to be treated as underage which, however, applies to proceedings and not necessarily to verdicts. in the case of charges brought against a person over the age of 18 for crimes allegedly committed when they were still under age, specific rules have to be followed, amongst them the right to mandatory assistance by a lawyer, to medical examination, to specific treatment, to individual assessment, and rules on questioning, are especially important. moreover, those dealing with such persons have to be properly trained in order to effectively assess the situation at every stage of the proceedings and beyond. in the end, however, it usually comes down to upbringing, education, relations with family members, a sense of belonging and many other things as well as a positive outside influence amongst other important factors. without the quality of this there can regretfully be no happy childhood or youth, which creates a most fertile ground for unlawful behaviour during these periods of life.
mr president, i would like to thank you for your time. according to official statistics, about one million children are prosecuted in various forms of criminal proceedings in the european union each year, representing about 12% of all persons involved in criminal proceedings in the european union. the treatment and legal framework of child criminal proceedings vary widely between member states. nor is there a statutory definition of the concept of 'fair trial for children'. the draft proposal for this directive was thus intended to set minimum rules, to define the package clearly, to create an appropriate set of rights for children. this is very good, it is welcome and it is important to note that the directive will also apply to those who have reached the age of 18 but have not yet reached the age of 21. however, the logical perspective would be to extend this directive or to develop a new one that would directly apply to children aged 14 and over.
