madam president, i will speak now in my capacity as vice—chair of the central asia delegation. in this capacity, i prefer mutually respectful, equal dialogue with kazakhstan. the eu has a vital interest in stepping up strategic cooperation with such an important international actor as kazakhstan. therefore, i would like to pose the following question to my colleagues. unless it decently calls into question kazakhstan’s ambition to be a candidate for a non-permanent seat on the un security council in 2017-2018, do we have the moral right, as cavalierly as is made out in the resolution, to teach kazakhstan on the questions concerning freedom of expression while we ourselves behave badly in the very same area? last december our parliament accepted the resolution saying that measures taken over the past few years in hungary have led to a serious systematic deterioration in the situation as regards the rule of law and fundamental rights, inter alia freedom of expression. this january we hotly debated the rule of law and media independence in poland. in my own country, latvia, the parliament has just adopted at first reading new provisions in criminal law which are aimed at sentencing to imprisonment for even non—violent actions against the state, that is independence, sovereignty, territorial integrity, and also for cooperation with foreign organisations against the security interests of the state. i myself sharply condemn attacks on freedom of expression elsewhere in the world. therefore, i am sure that we have to fight for these freedoms together, but not against each other.
chairman! commissioner, mr hana, colleagues, i would like to thank you for your work. of course, the situation with freedom of speech in kazakhstan is far from ideal. there is much to be done here by both the government of kazakhstan and civil society. i know several bloggers and journalists personally, including vladimir kozlov's wife, because before i became a member of the european parliament here, as a journalist, i gave them the opportunity to speak and express their views, because they didn't have that opportunity in kazakhstan. but the reality is quite different. how can we teach kazakhstan in light of what is happening in our own homes? recently in my country latvia a man named maksims koptelovs was sentenced to a real six-month prison term just for writing on the internet about latvia's possible accession to russia, and that was a joke, he added. we socialists condemn this attitude, but it is also a huge violation of freedom of speech.
chairman! this proposal marks the latest effort by the european union to further develop community legislation on animals living next to us, since in principle veterinary medicines are often used not to treat or improve animal health but also to guarantee health for us humans, i.e. consumers of animal products. the main objectives of this draft legislative resolution are to improve access to the necessary medicines and to reduce the administrative burden in the field concerned, to promote innovation, competitiveness and to ensure the efficient functioning of the veterinary medicines market within the community, and to combat antibiotic resistance. the last point was, incidentally, already discussed during the plenary session on monday. this is a very important document, and that is why i voted in favour.
chairman! the way in which the potential source of funding for the european medicines agency is specified in this document is commendable. i also agree with the rapporteur that the role of the agency in the registration and supervision of veterinary medicinal products should be clearly defined, using a highly centralised procedure. that's very good, that's a big step forward. at present, each member state has its own system and needs a common denominator. i would also like to welcome the changes that have been made, which have reduced the role of the commission to a certain extent. and i would also like to welcome the idea of publishing reports every five years, analysing in detail the experience gained in implementing the provisions of the regulation. i therefore voted in favour.
chairman! by 2020, the total amount of data will reach 16 zettabytes, trillions of gigabytes, so that's a 236 percent increase, which is a big year-on-year increase. other forecasts include 40 and 44 zettabytes. for 40 zettabytes, that's a whopping 5,200 gigabytes of data for every person on the planet. in other words, the potential for economic development is huge and must be exploited. i support this document and, from the perspective of consumers, businesses, the education system and other areas, there is a clear need for further, i would say, efforts to inform and disseminate the information to the public, which is a must for the european union, for the european commission, and the same applies to innovation and investment not only at the transnational level of the european union but also at all other levels.
in writing. ‒ i supported the european parliament resolution on moving towards a data-driven, emerging economy. this resolution goes hand in hand with the eu's drive towards a digital single market, which is being implemented to unlock wider opportunities for economic development. the data-driven economy, or digital economy, in which information technologies are widely used in all social, economic, cultural and educational fields, offers enormous development opportunities. however, investments in research, infrastructure and development are needed to exploit these opportunities and this resolution accordingly stresses the need for the eu to develop a single, modern and future-proof legal framework that stimulates and encourages investment in both network infrastructure and research. i believe that the data-driven economy, using information technology, has enormous potential to drive economic growth in the european union.

mr president, i would like to thank you for your time. as is known, in 2012, a new union-wide banking policy was launched in response to the eurozone crisis, which resulted in the transfer of responsibility for the control of this sector from the member state level to the transnational level. this is very good, and this is the vector we need to keep going. i joined the call for the speedy implementation of structural reforms in the banking system that would minimise, at least in part, the likelihood of recurrence of crises and the effectiveness of their management. incidentally, the same applies to the supervisory system, firstly, in the case of large banks. but overall investors and bank customers will benefit, because one of the most important points of appreciation is the call to guarantee the maximum possible security of deposits - both in principle and in the case of compensation - irrespective of the geographical location of the member states, and i therefore support this document.
the president, the commissioner! the reasons for the fall in pigmeat prices are more than clear - it is the sanctions war with russia, which several of my colleagues have already mentioned here, but as we know, before the sanctions war started, trade between the european union and russia amounted to more than one billion euros a day. at the moment, latvian farmers are suffering very big losses, and in fact it happens that the agricultural sector is the most regulated in the european union, but what can we do with our own regulations? they're powerless. so we have to open the russian market back to eastern europe, to my country, latvia, because russia is a traditional market for the european union. it is welcome that the sanctions against belarus have been lifted and that european pig producers may be able to enter the belarusian market, but it is important to know that this will not happen tomorrow or the next day. it takes time. in conclusion, i would like to say that latvian pork was once very famous throughout europe. soon, if we do not take urgent action, the latvian pig will be a memory.
madam president, every state has the right to defend its inhabitants, independence, territorial integrity and interests by whatever means deemed necessary, without, however, breaking the law or violating the inherent rights of its own people. of these rights, freedom of speech and self-expression rank among the most important. two years ago, the latvian maxim koptelov published a survey on the possibility of latvia joining the russian federation. he himself described the whole idea as a joke. nonetheless, in february of this year he was sentenced to six months in prison. interestingly, the authorities had ignored similar surveys on the subject of latvia joining the united states of america. what is more, a month ago the latvian parliament introduced an amendment to its criminal law, according to which essentially any statement, even a joke, deemed to have improper content could now lead to actual imprisonment. my home country has disgraced itself in a shameful manner.
mr president, firstly i wish to thank rapporteur metsola for doing this very difficult job. one of the best examples of a holistic approach in our history and literature is sherlock holmes. here is an example to take when looking at the migration crisis. applying the method of deduction to the migration crisis shows the weaknesses of our current system and politics, which arose as a result of delaying foreign and security policy. it also shows the current weaknesses in our value system, our border-security system and in internal cooperation between different institutions, as well as weaknesses in the institutions themselves. if we really want to regain the trust of our citizens, we must be successful in solving the migration crisis, and to do this we have to proceed step by step. this step-by-step approach entails, for example, trying to fix these problems one after another. i see the upcoming file at parliament on our border agency and more secure european external borders as a contribution to this holistic approach. this is because we cannot survive with our current values and schengen system of open internal borders without fixing our external borders. let us do it step by step and we will also succeed with the migration crisis. (the speaker agreed to take a blue-card question under rule 162(8))
blue-card answer.– i have no reason to believe that the commission is not, just like ourselves, doing its job to the best of its capability. we must understand that none of us is a god here. the commission, the european parliament, the council and the world in general have restricted capabilities, so the only way we can proceed is not just to paint ideals on the wall and think about a wished-for world in the future, but to fix every issue step by step. that is the only practical solution in politics that i can suggest to you.
in writing. ‒ the purpose of this vote was to support the commission's proposal for the codification and recasting of council regulation (ec) no 1528/2007. this regulation governs the import of certain products originating in the african, caribbean and pacific group of states and applies to those goods produced in the least developed acp states, by ensuring consistent duty-free and quota-free access to the european union market. the scheme currently covers 27 acp countries. in other words, the idea is to continue to support developing countries by this kind of indirect economic assistance, giving them the opportunity to export their production to the union. as this scheme does not really harm the interests of local producers and consumers, and at the same time contributes to the eu's growing role on the world economic and political stage, it was logical to support it.
in writing. ‒ like iceland or the faroe islands, greenland's primary economic sector and export commodity is fisheries and fish respectively, accounting for around 90% of all exported goods, of which around 90% goes to denmark. in recent years, the union has signed a number of fisheries partnership agreements and protocols with third countries. the eu provides financial and technical support in exchange for fishing rights in the exclusive economic zone of the partner countries. as greenland is contractually part of the kingdom of denmark and the fisheries in its territorial waters are managed by nuuk and outside of them by copenhagen, it was necessary to conclude a contract with both parties. the original fisheries agreement was signed in 1985, extended several times and finally replaced by the fisheries partnership agreement. the aim of this protocol is thus to strengthen existing cooperation, since it is the third most important eu agreement in terms of economic value in terms of its scope. given the mutual benefits to the parties involved, it was, of course, logical to vote for the agreement. it is important to note, however, that in the future the agreement should pay greater attention to the sustainability of fisheries.
in writing. ‒ the commission has been negotiating with the macao special administrative region of the people's republic of china for some time now an agreement to replace certain provisions in the existing 15 bilateral air services agreements concluded between eu member states and this territorial component of the prc, which has autonomy in several areas, including air transport policy. the main idea of the new agreement is to legally replace any currently active bilateral air transport agreements concluded between certain eu member states and macao. this is done in order to completely eliminate contractually any possibility of discrimination against airlines operating in the eu territory or owned by nationals of its member states. the primary aspects of the agreement are safety, taxation, compliance with the competition rules, and the carrier selection clause.
in writing. ‒ when it became clear that the introduction of the single market would not make it possible to implement the commission's proposals for the establishment of a definitive system of tax harmonisation, directive 92/77/ec was introduced, according to which the basic vat rate in all member states could not be set below a level of 15%. the provisions of the directive have been extended five times, the last of which until 31 december 2015, which means that it is time to do so again this time until the end of 2018. given that the vat rate in all eu member states is above 15%, no distortion of national tax policy will occur in this respect. the existing system will therefore continue to operate until the day when a single tax policy for all eu member states could possibly be implemented. it is also worth noting that the commission initially proposed to extend the validity of the existing rules until the end of 2017, but this proposal was later changed to the above mentioned date.
in writing. ‒ as has happened several times in recent months, a vote was held today on the conclusion of a europol strategic cooperation agreement with a third country, this time with the federative republic of brazil. the fact that the summer olympic games will soon be held in brazil is particularly important, as it always intensifies the various types of criminal activity. outside these, it will be possible to contribute to a more active and effective fight against illegal immigration, trafficking in human beings, drug-related crime and credit card fraud, as well as other cyber-related activities. counter-terrorism activities also play an important role in cooperation with brazil. given that this agreement will help to combat organised crime more effectively and will significantly strengthen cooperation between international law enforcement agencies, it is hard to think of any reason why the council's proposal for an agreement should not be supported.
in writing. ‒ the role of the european parliament as a branch of the road police and csdd authorities of the member states has been considerably strengthened in recent months, which is of course very welcome. if previous votes on the waiver of immunity for speeding were to be held, this time it was on the preliminary investigation into a traffic accident involving german mep herman winkler. since this case is in no way connected with mr winkler's work in the european parliament and since all members must be clear that there must be no problem with national or international courts, it was logical to vote for the waiver of the member's immunity in order to allow the german court to examine the case in its entirety and reach a final conclusion, which in turn would determine mr winkler's role and, consequently, the possible consequences of this traffic accident.
chairman! the un plans to develop an international legally binding instrument by the end of 2017 to promote the conservation and sustainable use of marine biodiversity beyond the territorial jurisdiction of states. this already means a modern concept of sustainability, the development of marine ecosystem management and so on. i think this is extremely important because animal selection... i apologize for that. i join the call on the commission to cooperate as actively as possible with all the other parties involved in the development of the new regulations and, in principle, to regularly bring to the attention of both its own and other partners the issues of the sustainable use of marine resources. i voted in favour of course, because it is clear that the european union's involvement in this matter cannot be limited to the common fisheries policy, just as unilateral action on the part of the eca will never produce effective results. thank you.
in writing. ‒ the purpose of this directive is to combine several directives on zootechnical regulations for different species into a single single legal act, thus contributing to the creation of a single market in this field. this would facilitate the free movement of breeding animals and their genetic material within the territory of the member states. the same applies to more efficient import and export procedures. the system of animal breeding directly affects not only the agricultural sector but also consumer rights, economic development, health and much more, so it is perfectly understandable why so many amendments had to be tabled there simply cannot be any misunderstanding here. this is not to mention the need to maximise the synchronisation of the characteristics and historical specificities of the systems existing in the member states. the proposal also focuses on the protection of the rights and interests of breeding societies, rules on the registration of work results, cross-border cooperation and, of course, the protection of rare, endangered and also 'indigenous' species. overall, given the importance of the proposal, it was necessary to support it. it is hoped that in the future, adapting its performance to the realities of my country latvia will bring maximum benefits to our farmers and consumers.
chairman! it is clear that the events of the past year have shown that the current european union policy on migration is not working. it may be effective enough for small numbers of migrants, but it simply cannot function effectively or cope with such large numbers. it is clear that different countries have different attitudes to the current situation, especially after the events in paris and brussels, and therefore coordination between national, transnational, regional and municipal levels of government is vital. clearly, the principle of solidarity is of paramount importance, which is why i support this document and the reporters who worked so hard on it. but we must say that we do not yet have the ideal system, and we - the european parliament and the commission - and the european union as a whole have a long way to go to put in place an effective system to deal with the migrant crisis. but i will vote in favour again. thank you.
in writing. ‒ europe is currently experiencing the largest influx of refugees since the second world war. war in the middle east and poverty in africa have driven thousands of people to seek refuge and a better life in europe. we cannot turn our backs on people fleeing war, but we must not accept those who want to reach europe in search of a better life. first, we must secure the external borders and allow them to return safely to third countries. no uncontrolled movement of people is allowed. unfortunately, i do not think that the european parliament's resolution emphasises enough the need to regulate the external borders. i do not think it is fair to propose that refugees be distributed among the member states in a forced way. such a mechanism has already shown itself to be unable to function. the proposal to create a european union refugee assessment office to determine which refugees and how many to accept, thus imposing criteria on member states, is also not to be supported. member states should have the right to determine this themselves. given that the resolution does not offer a solution to the situation but only complicates it, i voted against it.
in writing. ‒ there is no simple solution to the current migration crisis in europe. i very much respect the difficult work done by the co-rapporteurs, however, this report includes too many elements which are not acceptable to my country; it mixes asylum, labour market needs, demography and stateless persons. while coordinated approach is needed, and latvia takes part in relocations from italy and greece; solidarity cannot be enforced. a permanent, mandatory resettlement programme for all eu ms on the basis of criteria such as the size of the country and the population density are unfair conditions. we need to finally implement existing rules, including the dublin regulation, instead of writing new. asylum decisions are a national competence and i cannot support idea that people would apply to asylum in the eu and brussels would decide to which ms and how many applicants are sent. labour migration is a national competence; it is not brussels which can determine the labour market needs of eu ms. furthermore, the request to ms to reduce the number of stateless persons is unacceptable given that it goes beyond the scope of this report. therefore i could not support the report on a holistic eu approach to migration.
in writing. ‒ i voted against the european parliament resolution on the need for a holistic eu approach to migration, as it stresses that a centralised asylum system will effectively address the influx of migrants and asylum seekers. populism though it has been in its various forms is proving stronger in the european parliament, at least for the time being. it is also being carried out by the left-wing and centrist colleagues of the parliament, most of whom do not want to gain political power in the member states. for example, the eu council's position on this issue is different, and there is a shift towards a sensible and realistic decision-making process. it must be understood that the compulsory voluntary quotas, this time wrapped in a cloak of solidarity, do not work because, in the end, this is not in the interests of most member states, nor of the migrants themselves, who, on the basis of the proposed plan, want to be sent to different member states with different standards of living and social guarantees. moreover, it does not solve the secondary movement problem, but on the contrary, the fact that the vast majority of migrants and asylum seekers are forcibly in countries they would rather not be in poses serious risks to the existence of the schengen area. the basic principle of the dublin regulation must remain in place. it is also necessary to ensure that any migrant who has paid smugglers is sent back; and that only genuine refugees from the threat of war obtain their refugee status in third country camps where they apply to a member state for a review of their status.
chairman! on the one hand, it is welcome that the principles of subsidiarity and proportionality were effectively applied in practice in the european union's governance in 2012 and 2013. and i therefore join the call for the national parliaments in the european union to be more actively involved in these processes, both in general and in the light of developments in general. but the same is true of regional and municipal authorities, since they are the main institutions implementing the principle of subsidiarity and, i would say, the de facto closest authorities to the citizens of the european union. but it is unfortunate that in recent years the implementation of the principle of subsidiarity has suffered more and more, i would say, has also withered and is not fully implemented in our lives. but i agree with the need to simplify the procedure for requesting eu funding as much as possible and i repeat that i voted in favour.
chairman! the compliance and enforcement programme is arguably one of the most logical innovations ever implemented in the european union, but of course its main objective is to simplify legislation, reduce regulatory costs and so on. but when our citizens sometimes ask us, the mps, and in general... when asked why they are against or partially sceptical of the european union, the clear answer is that bureaucracy and over-bureaucratisation are the reason why eu citizens sometimes lack confidence in the european union and its institutions. because maximum transparency, stakeholder consultation and dialogue with citizens and social partners in general - this is the number one task we need to do, and it doesn't take as much time and effort to make it happen. but overall, i support this document. i think that makes a lot of sense. thank you.
chairman! the single market is to a large extent the cornerstone of the european union. we should therefore all strive to maximise the principles on which the relevant regulations operate. today we have already discussed the principles of subsidiarity and proportionality, and it is clear that they are also of paramount importance in the case of the single market, since its functioning depends on virtually all the administrative mechanisms of the european union. it must be said that while several administrative bodies are involved in the legal mechanisms for the functioning of the single market, its main, i would say, participants are nevertheless a number of undertakings located in the territory of the european union. we must therefore always seek to maintain and intensify the dialogue with them so that they can make the most of the benefits of the single market. and in parallel, of course, to improve the contacts between decision-makers and entrepreneurs, i would agree that we need to increase the efficiency of conflict resolution methods. thank you.
chairman! two years ago, 52% of europeans felt that their voice was not heard here in parliament at all, and 44% simply did not understand how it all worked. as a result, the participation rate in european elections is well known and is decreasing and this needs to be changed. i am therefore very pleased that we voted today for this report, which is also intended to focus on the effective information of our citizens. the information starts, of course, at school, because the future electors to the european parliament and the future of the european union are sitting on the school's doorstep. and it is also very good that we can use all these men for this growth potential, and for example good mechanisms like 'erasmus+' and 'creative europe' are just one of the proofs that this process has a future.
chairman! erasmus+ is a great opportunity not only to study or work abroad, but also to qualify such periods as full-time work, which will then be taken into account throughout the european union and in each member state. in other words, there must be a clearly defined link between education, access to education and employment opportunities, which is as widely disseminated as possible throughout the eu. but there is one "but". i'm not voting for the guy. i held back for one reason, because unfortunately the majority in the european parliament did not support an amendment that would allow erasmus+ documents to be available in all the languages in which education is provided in schools in the european union. for example, in my country, latvia, there are not only schools with latvian language instruction, in which i am speaking now, but also schools traditionally in belarusian, ukrainian, russian, hebrew. what if we sent these documents to young people in their native languages? i'm afraid that doesn't make sense to me. thank you.
chairman! despite all the factors, the fact that other world markets and competitors are developing rapidly, the european union is a very strong player on a global scale. this, of course, means that our participation in a number of international organizations plays a huge role. i agree that we need to define clearly the role of each international and regional institution, the dialogues, the specifics of the dialogues, the regulation and so on, and it is clear that while the european union is represented as a single participant in many cases, there is still a lot of disagreement about the specific steps. we'd have to keep moving. it is clear that we must consolidate our positions, because at the end of the day the european union is defending the common interests of all the member states, including at international level. thank you.
chairman! coastal fishing vessels account for about 80% of the total fleet of the member states of the european union. in many regions, fishing is the primary form of employment, both de facto and historically. it thus constitutes the largest sphere in terms of the jobs it occupies. in other words, effective regulation and - and this is crucial - quality support for those working in this field must be at the forefront of both member state and union policies. unlike industrial fishing, small-scale coastal fishing is precisely that type of small-scale fishing and is often carried out by small companies or even on an individual basis. this should always be taken into account when we develop policy in this area. but i voted for it. thank you.
chairman! the european union is the world's largest market for fishery products, consuming only 11% of the world's catches, while importing 24% of the value of the catch. the latter can also be attributed to the fact that the european union and its companies are important processors of marine resources. the fisheries agreement with greenland has already been voted on today, but it is only one of a number of agreements of this type, both bilateral and multilateral. we must therefore continue our efforts to maximise the systematization of fisheries at international level, particularly in terms of sustainability and, i would say, also in terms of the protection of ecosystems. it is to be hoped that active cooperation with partners in other countries, especially regional fisheries management organisations, and more active scientific work will sooner or later bear fruit. this is therefore commendable.

the rapporteur. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the following areas: on 22 march, the parliament's foreign affairs committee began with a moment of silence to honour the victims of the terrorist attack at zaventem airport. when the foreign affairs committee was preparing to vote on my report on the strategy for a european foreign and security policy, we learned that there had just been an explosion in the parliament building at the nearest metro station. it was a strange, surreal feeling. we voted on the strategic objectives of european foreign policy, but people were dying a few hundred metres away - partly because of europe's failure to achieve the objectives set out in other strategic documents many years ago. looking at the european security strategy adopted in 2003, we see that one of the three main strategic objectives is the stabilisation of europe's neighbourhoods, including the middle east. today, 13 years later, we must recognize that this strategic objective has not been achieved and that the situation in the neighbouring regions has become one of the main causes of the major security problem at the heart of europe. i would not want our descendants to see in five or ten years' time the good papers, the weak works. the reason for this is that our union suffers from a chronic lack of political will, which affects the implementation of many european policies. therefore, one of the key recommendations in the resolution on the eu global strategy is to monitor its implementation, to regularly assess and update it, and to link objectives to resources. the european parliament, through this resolution, wishes to contribute to the formation and concentration of the political will of the institutions and the member states. e resolution calls for a substantial strengthening of europe's common defence capability and for the creation of a european defence union in the foreseeable future. we call for the european union's external action efforts to focus directly on stabilising europe's wider neighbourhood, rather than trying to be all things to all people. we call for europe to be a world-class player with a regional focus, active in shaping global rules rather than filling them we stress that this is only possible in cooperation with the union's main strategic partners. at the same time, we must rapporteur.– mr president, i thank you all very much for all the pertinent remarks you have made. i will just repeat the key words of the work we are doing together. first of all, it is important to have the political will to build the resilience of europe and europeans. there is a need for a coherent internal and external policy, and what is most important is that we implement the strategic documents. to do that, we need resources for external policy. i fully agree that we have to keep our engagement and continue with enlargement and our promises. there is the importance of strategic autonomy and the strengthening of our defence capacity. we have to cooperate with nato and we have to think about building a european intelligence agency. human security is at the centre of our strategy. because of that we need to better control arms. we have to be more skilful in conflict resolution and invest more in development, because we want to be a global player with a regional focus in this connected, contested and complex world.
mr president, central asia has an important role and potential in regional and global security, transit and trade, energy and communications. our goal should be to act as an impartial mediator in the resolution of the existing conflicts, primarily related to water supply and ethnic divisions. the same applies to development assistance provision, especially in education and the environment. tailor-made country action plans, together with the long—term experience of our eastern european member states, should indeed help on this path. my sincere advice to our leadership is to take into account and respect the centuries-old traditions and customs of societal and governance conduct in these countries. only by accepting these differences and effectively adapting any of our reform suggestions to the very local specificities will the eu be able to have a strong voice in the region. i congratulate the rapporteur on his excellent work.
madam president, i am in favour of a constructive relationship with turkey, but the current one clearly resembles bullying and extortion on the part of turkish president erdoğan, with the threat of overloading europe with refugees in the event of non-compliance. the same applies to threats of sabotaging the whole migration deal should turkey not get visa-free travel. i wholeheartedly acknowledge the fact that turkey currently houses and provides for more than 2.5 million refugees and may require assistance. however, the current actions of the turkish leadership, in relations with the eu and a majority of its members, cannot go unnoticed. in particular, there are serious indications that turkey played a major role in enabling jihadist groups, such as daesh and the al-nusra front in syria. this support has contributed to the current refugee crisis. that being said, i urge european leaders to promote the interests of european states and europeans by keeping true to our values.
in writing. ‒ almost two months ago, the european commission decided to support the redundancies of five companies in sweden, which were involved in the production of cars, trucks and semi-trailers. as part of this adequacy, sweden will receive a total of eur 1 793 710 for the retraining of 500 former workers, various forms of support and gradual reintegration into the labour market. one of the main reasons why this aid became necessary was that in recent years the european commercial vehicle industry, especially the trucks, has been affected by similar trends in the development of companies in asia, with which it is rather difficult to compete due to lower wage costs. i supported the granting of this funding in order to allow the early return of the dismissed workers to the labour market, thus contributing to the further development of the vehicle manufacturing sector.
in writing. ‒ unlike the majority of the other votes on the use of the european globalisation adjustment fund, this one concerns the allocation of funds to the european commission itself, and in this case to the relevant institutions, with the aim of financing monitoring and information measures, developing a knowledge base interface and advising the member states of the european union on the use, monitoring and evaluation of egf funding. the total amount of the grant shall be eur 380 000. the request as such follows from the fact that the egf regulation states that each year approximately 0,5% of the annual resources of the fund may be requested for this type of purpose. the ceiling for this year is thus eur 828,060. given that the fund has continued to support thousands of redundant workers every year since its creation, helping them to re-skill and re-enter the labour market, and otherwise consistently supporting the competitiveness of the eu as a whole and its economy and its further development, it was logical to vote in favour.
in writing. ‒ given the developments over the last few years, and especially last year, i fully support the setting up of this new financial instrument, which will be able to provide at least part of the support from the member states affected by the migrant/refugee crisis, first of all greece and italy. i also strongly support increasing funding for the european counter-terrorism centre and europol to enable them to deal more effectively with the logical risks that such a large influx of people of all kinds inevitably brings. while it is understood that the crisis in each country will be addressed through other sources, it is nevertheless conceivable that the allocated funding of eur 300 million in 2016, eur 200 million in 2017 and eur 200 million in 2018 may be insufficient. i therefore very much hope that the commission and the council already have, or are planning, alternative sources of funding and/or additional resources to deal with the crisis.
in writing. ‒ the european court of auditors acts as an independent external auditor, regularly auditing the management of the union budget by the european commission and consistently providing an overall view of the existing financial situation together with recommendations for its improvement. although it has no legal authority, the role of the court of auditors in financial control is undeniable. this means that the 28 members of the european court of auditors - one from each eu member state - are the most qualified financial professionals, with a long record of work in their home country, whose primary task is to oversee the union's finances in a way that safeguards the interests of its citizens, i.e. taxpayers. having considered and analysed mr jereba's candidacy and having regard to his professional experience and a number of other factors, i have voted against. however, he was elected by 404 votes to 222.

mr president, i would like to thank you for your time. one of the main problems is that glyphosate is largely non-selective in its action, killing not only unwanted herbs but also all plants, including algae, bacteria, fungi, and this has a very negative impact on biodiversity and ecosystems in the areas where it is applied. i agree, ladies and gentlemen, that the commission's draft implementing regulation still fails to ensure, to a reasonable extent, the protection of human and animal health and the protection of the environment itself. i also support the need to develop as soon as possible an independent review of the classification of glyphosate, firstly, with regard to its carcinogenicity and endocrine system. in the light of all of the above, i voted unanimously against the renewal of glyphosate and i also joined the call to the commission to publish the data on the basis of which the renewal of the approval of glyphosate has been proposed once again. thank you.
in writing. at the european parliament plenary session in strasbourg, i voted to raise an objection under rule 106 of the rules of procedure to the renewal of the approval of the active substance 'glyphosate'. the european commission's proposal provided for the renewal of the approval of the active substance glyphosate for a further period of 15 years. the european parliament resolution and the objection highlight the potential hazards of glyphosate and its potential negative effects on human health, and therefore object to the authorisation of the use of such a substance for such a long period. the european parliament resolution provides for the authorisation of the substance for a further seven years, but if new studies reveal and can reasonably be shown that the substance concerned no longer meets the approval criteria, the commission may withdraw the approval of the active substance. in addition, member states can decide at national level whether they wish to authorise or prohibit the substance. the resolution also stressed that the harmful substance should only be used at a professional level and called on the commission to recommend that member states restrict or prohibit the sale of glyphosate to non-professional users. in the light of the information available to me, i consider that it is appropriate for health reasons not to authorise the renewal of glyphosate for 15 years, but to authorise it for a much shorter period and with much stricter conditions of use.
in writing. a study by the world health organization suggests that glyphosate may cause cancer. the numbers are dramatic. in the last 40 years, the world's use of this agrochemical has increased 260-fold. glyphosate is one of the most widely used herbicides in europe. the most common is the roundup. farmers are increasingly using roundup to prepare their fields for the next season, to destroy weeds, or to speed up the death of crops. the plants leave circular traces after they are harvested. every day, we eat flour, oatmeal, bread, cakes, and other products that are part of the cycle. a few years ago, a study showed that the urine levels of the latvian population are among the highest in europe. concerns are also growing about the effects of glyphosate on the hormonal and reproductive systems, as well as its effects on pregnancy. glyphosate and its residues have also been found in water, soil, food and beverages and non-food products. this damages the environment and nature, reducing biodiversity on agricultural land and destroying food sources for birds and insects. given that glyphosate not only kills unwanted weeds but also plants, pollutes the environment in which we live and poses a serious threat to human health, i voted against the renewal of the licence for glyphosate. i do not believe that the use of plant protection products that have been identified as potentially carcinogenic by the world's leading health agency should be allowed.
chairman! i take this message with mixed feelings. on the one hand, it is clear that the european union has been a very influential player on the world stage for many years, for decades, especially in the economic field. this also applies to the protection of our values. on the military front, there is, i would say, a fairly specific division of responsibilities between the european union and nato, largely thanks to the 'berlin plus' system, and the member states' potential in this respect is not being used well. on the other hand, the report looks like a ragged dress. in fact, we have to spend 2% of gdp from the national budgets just to spend it. we need to strengthen our relations with the united states, because we are expecting some kind of threat from our eastern neighbours, which our nationalists love to talk about. i therefore voted against this report with the same sentiments, because i think it is simply not as well prepared as we would like it to be. thank you.

chairman! clearly, evolution is not something that is unique to humans. the same viruses in your mouth have been around for decades and are becoming more dangerous, more deadly. zika virus is one of the latest examples, and the principal difference is its devastating effect on unborn children, as it causes microcephaly. in adults, it leads to guillain-barre syndrome. this is very important. the virus has been detected in 28 countries, including spain. and the fact is, if it originally -- in the mid-20th century -- it was affecting equatorial areas in asia and africa, then it was actually moving across oceania and south america three years ago. in view of all this, i wholeheartedly support the commission's decision to allocate eur 10 million to zika virus research and i urge all the specialised bodies to take up this matter, especially as it is of great relevance, especially ahead of the summer olympic games in brazil.

chairman! the security of personal data has long been a matter of concern for the european parliament, and it is true that millions of people every minute, every second, buy goods online, register for events, submit documents electronically, and so on. and the fundamental problem is that given these constant developments in the market and the legal framework, there is absolutely no guarantee that this data will not be available to third parties and third parties. how often have we all received a phone call from a complete stranger who stood in front of us and already knew our name, first name, telephone number, and so forth? why would these people have access to our data in the first place? so, when we give permission to an authority to process our data in a very specific way, that's when that data is legal. i therefore voted with great pleasure in favour of this legislative resolution. thank you.

chairman! security or freedom? these two numbers are what make up one of the most fundamental equations in the governance of nations. which is more important? who should take precedence? the point is, both are vitally important. in the case of the european union, for decades we have lived as if in one of those innumerable 'safeties' that seem self-evident, permanent, but unfortunately are not. and recent events in paris and brussels and other european cities prove that we need to think about security, to compromise, to balance security and freedom to the maximum, ignoring neither. i support this document by voting today because the passenger data register presents the same dilemma, but it seems that, in the light of all that has happened recently, such a register is needed by the european union precisely to improve the safety of our citizens. the most important thing in this regard is, of course, to guarantee the fundamental freedoms of our citizens. thank you.
in writing. ‒ i support the new directive which will regulate the use of passenger name record (pdr) data for the eu's prevention, detection, investigation and investigation of terrorism and other serious crimes. the new rules will require airlines to transfer passenger data to member state authorities for all flights between the eu and third countries. by collecting passenger data, sharing it, and analyzing it, our intelligence agencies will be able to detect suspicious patterns of behavior. pdr is not a panacea, but member states with national pdr systems have repeatedly demonstrated its effectiveness. the directive also provides data protection safeguards and is proportionate to the risk to which we are currently exposed. the aim is to harmonise the rules of the eu member states on the collection and processing of pdr data.
in writing. at the european parliament plenary i voted for a directive which provides for the establishment of a passenger data register at european union level. i believe that this decision, which has been delayed in an excessive and unjustified manner, is very welcome and provides a powerful weapon for european law enforcement and other security authorities in the fight against terrorism and organised crime. law enforcement authorities will now have access to information on people arriving in the european union from third countries. the text of the directive clearly states that the information contained in the register may only be used for the purpose of combating crime and terrorism, thus also respecting the principles of data protection for citizens and preserving the right to privacy. the exchange of information will facilitate the tracing and detection of individuals who are not yet in the sights of law enforcement and other security authorities as potential terrorists or criminals. the shortcoming is that the directive does not cover intra-eu flights, but this information will be exchanged between services on a voluntary basis.
in writing. people still remember the recent terrorist attacks in brussels and paris, which took the lives of innocent people. it is therefore clear that more must be done to protect citizens across europe. the passenger data register (pdr) is an important step to ensure safety. the register will collect information on people entering or leaving europe, thus allowing the identification of persons previously detained on suspicion of involvement in terrorism or serious crime, as well as those who need to be further screened before they are allowed to leave or enter the country. the urgent need for a unified passenger data system in the eu is demonstrated by the inefficient exchange of information between eu countries, which has resulted in a significant delay in the search for suspects. the absurdity is that before the brussels terrorist acts, there were reports from foreign security services about suspicious people entering europe, yet the security services of the eu countries concerned failed to use this information properly, resulting in loss of life. it is regrettable that the establishment of the pdr system has been delayed for years by left-wing forces, who have been concerned about the protection of personal data. in fact, it is clear that honest people have nothing to hide, and if the pdr system is used to expose planned terrorist attacks, it will be a benefit to public security. in addition, the system provides for the careful storage of data, making them available only to law enforcement authorities.

in writing. ‒ the european union is one of the most developed and richest places in the world, but unfortunately not everywhere people have the opportunity to live a decent life, according to their skills and aspirations. in 2013, 122,6 million people, or 16,7% of the eu population, were at risk of poverty. in 2008, there were 117 million. given the trends of the last few years, i don't think the statistics could have been any more positive. although the europe 2020 strategy sets a target of reducing such numbers by 20 million by the end of the decade, this should not be the main objective, as the necessary efforts must be implemented on the basis of the facts, the strategy merely provides a guide. i unambiguously join the call on the commission, and in particular on the member states themselves, to do everything possible and within their power to combat poverty and social exclusion in europe as effectively as possible. in our existing social systems, which are far from being de facto liberal and government-free, the states are responsible in one way or another for social policy, health care and support for the poor. access to clean water, winter heating resources and - as a matter of principle - support for our elderly, at least through fair pensions, must be at the heart of social policy in any member state. in some countries, however, it is the pensioners who are in the worst financial position compared with the rest of society. it's impossible to effectively build the future without thinking about those who helped build the present.
in writing. ‒ as the private sector in developing countries accounts for around 84% of gdp, any eu support to such regions, along with addressing other issues, should be focused on this area, which usually - but not always - also employs the majority of all local workers. smes (micro, small and medium-sized enterprises) in developing countries are often the cornerstone of national economies and societies, providing opportunities to provide for one's family and to strengthen social ties, thus promoting social integration. the eu could thus clearly share experience that has already proven positive in a number of cases around the world, in the areas of access to finance (for example, microcredit, which has proven to be excellent in several countries, notably bangladesh), employment and, consequently, social policy reform, and the modernisation of banking and tax policies. i agree that it is also of fundamental importance to promote positive and productive private-public cooperation. it will also gradually guarantee effective change in education, health, agriculture and innovation, where real progress is not possible without such symbiotic relationships. it is of course very important to always take account of local specificities, which, if used as a kind of basis, could also contribute to qualitative development.

mr president, those of us who have, for example, watched star trek or read isaac asimov’s sally will know about the concept of autonomous driving, and even flying. the current level of technological development allows for a limited vehicle autonomy, which is bound to increase over time. one of the main issues is obviously safety. the electronic and other systems involved have to be highly advanced and cover a massive number of behavioural and traffic probabilities. therefore, testing and inspection procedures which are quite different from those for ordinary vehicles are of utmost importance. another principal issue is security. you would not want to be kidnapped while still in your own car when it suddenly rushes off course. as a result, i fully support the idea that the commission, alongside national governments, should soon start drawing up a respective strategy for this area of transportation. having said that, we should never forget about skynet/terminator, with virtual intelligence turning into artificial intelligence and robots some day receiving rights of their own in a few decades, and that soon we might have to deal with the question of how much control we ourselves have over our own lives.
mr president, the process of european integration and institution-building has always been based on the ideals of participatory democracy and transparency – at least in principle. it is therefore most unbecoming for a number of eu institutions, including parliament, that maximum public access for all unclassified documents, even nowadays, is not fully guaranteed. in the light of this, i support the idea of incorporating the european council, the european central bank, the court of justice, and all other eu bodies and agencies within the scope of the regulation being discussed. transparency for documentation related to delegated texts and negotiations on international agreements is of particular importance. the most basic reasoning for such change is quite simple. those who, through their taxes, supply the bulk of the eu’s budget deserve to know what is actually happening behind the walls of each institution. moreover, a higher level of transparency would directly and positively affect public interest in the union itself.
