fourth railway package? what will change? the technical pillar consists of three documents: the regulation on the european railway agency, the directive on the interoperability of the european rail system and the railway safety directive. these three questions are clearly closely related. i shall be speaking mainly about my report on the european railway agency. so what was accomplished? simplified procedures for the authorisation of rolling stock have been achieved, particularly when used on the european rail network, or in cross-border transport and when working for one company in several countries. in the future, era will issue all authorisations for vehicles intended for cross-border use. the era will also be the body that will issue safety certificates to railway undertakings providing cross-border services. consequently, railway undertakings will no longer have to contact each national authority dealing with the issue of these certificates and authorisations in a given country in such cases, but will be able to use this legal endorsement of the european railway agency. and what is important is that it has managed to maintain a balance between vehicles and companies involved only in domestic transport, in the transport of one country then the customer has the possibility to carry out this certification and authorisation both in the european railway agency and, of course, in the national body. what has been achieved is that the era is also given a greater role in the development of ertms control, because, despite the way this signalling system is developing in europe, this interoperability is often not good, and i think that michael cramer and izaskun bilbao are also talking about this. the era will finally be a single agency, and our document will also regulate the cooperation between the european railway agency and the national institutions. finally, a few words about the baltic peculiarity, because we are well integrated in the baltic states and finland in the large former, say, cis bloc with rail transport. we managed to find a good compromise that any railway operator or any other legal entity that wants to get an authorisation or certification can do so in one of the baltic countries, say, railway authorities, inspections, and within four years there has to be a situation where a certificate issued in one country is valid in the other countries as well, because there is none at the moment. and this situation, this fragmented system in these markets is also extremely bad. now we use it to avoid him. thank you.
the rapporteur. - mr president! first of all, i would like to thank all my colleagues for the overall positive assessment of this work on the technical pillar. so thank you again for your work and for your appraisal! two things i'd like to mention after the debate. there were very high hopes that eventually the railways would have a single market in the european union and that all monopolies would have ceased to be able to operate in their own member state in such a monopoly position. i'd be a little more careful with this. with this legislation we have given the institutions the means to do that, but at the same time we have the experience that we have just recently reviewed the legislation of the first railway package. that was only a few years ago, and i would not like to see the fourth railway package legislation reviewed by our future colleagues or perhaps even by some of us. the other thing. this is not a federalist approach, as some of my colleagues have said. there is a single market approach here in the rail sector, and most of my colleagues in the european parliament, i think, are in favour of a single market. it is not a question of a federalist or a federalist on the political stage. we will be precise in our political assessments of this particular legislation. thank you.
in writing. ‒ on 2 december 2010, georgia became the first country in the caucasus region to conclude a comprehensive air transport agreement with the european union, which forms part of the framework of our union's external aviation policy. following croatia's accession to the eu in mid-2013, it should progressively accede to all agreements which the union has with third countries in various fields. given that the only change introduced in the existing agreement involves the accession of croatia itself, thus also providing a further impetus to the development of eu-georgia relations, it was logical to vote in favour.
in writing. ‒ israel is one of the most important transport hubs in the mediterranean and middle east regions in terms of aviation, and one of the eu's main partners in a wide range of fields. the commission therefore considers that the aid is compatible with the internal market within the meaning of article 107 (3) (c) of the treaty. as croatia became the 28th member state of the eu less than a month after signing this agreement, it was time to extend it to include our newest member state in the context of the agreement. given the potential benefits of such a step under the implementation agreement, it was logical to vote in favour.
in writing. ‒ the convention on mutual assistance and cooperation between customs administrations was created with the aim of establishing mutual support mechanisms between eu member states in the field of customs administration and indirectly in the field of border protection. for example, it covers, inter alia, criminal investigations into infringements of the customs rules of the member states and of the community. with the accession of croatia to the european union almost three years ago, the total length of our land and sea borders has increased significantly, which has inevitably led to the need to intensify mutual cooperation in the field of customs administration. this is particularly important in the western balkans region, where it can also indirectly provide the further context and relevant backdrops for cooperation between existing countries in this area not only between eu member states.
in writing. ‒ this request for waiver of the immunity of a member of the european parliament concerns the possible exceeding of the speed limit by the polish member of parliament bolesław piasz in the urban area. the incident itself took place before mr pehas became a member of parliament. following the request sent to the president of parliament by the prosecutor general of the republic of poland on 29 october 2015 and the conclusion of the parliament's legal committee on the matter on 20 april 2016, it was decided to waive the immunity of mr pijas. i do not agree, since it is necessary to ensure that the provisions laid down in the legislation of all the member states and appropriate to situations of this kind are fully respected and that mr piecha does not have any conflict with the laws of the republic of poland.
in writing. ‒ ideally, within any society, there is a large number of children who grow up happy, are well-educated, have full-fledged, loving families and who study the specificities and traditions of their respective cultures, and there is a maximum degree of integration of these children in the realities of each particular society. sadly, this ideal situation does not always exist, and it is a minority in the world's children. social exclusion, violence, inadequate state or even parental attention to the quality of children's daily lives and education, high levels of risk in times of conflict, trafficking in human beings, child prostitution, even the unjustified removal of children from their families, which has become so widespread in recent years, especially in scandinavian countries, children are in fact the most vulnerable population group in any country. policies geared to the development of children must always be at the heart of all legislation, actions and actions relating to them. while parents themselves have a key role to play, countries must also play an active role in ensuring that their younger generations grow up to be happy, ready for life and able to work productively for themselves, their relatives and society as a whole. i also joined the call to the council to report on the progress made in the implementation of alarm systems used in cases of child abduction with cross-border implications.
in writing. ‒ in early september 2015, commission president jean-claude juncker announced the creation of a european pillar of social rights, which will be implemented by the european commission in the coming years. the initiative as such is part of the commission's work towards a closer and fairer economic and monetary union. as a result, it primarily includes the euro area countries, although others could of course also join. the idea behind this pillar is to overcome the effects of the recent economic and financial crisis that are still being felt in our community, especially in terms of well-being, employment and social protection in general. institutional consultation is therefore needed to promote the necessary further efforts to make an effective contribution to the guarantee of the social rights of europeans. given the specific nature of the work of both the european parliament and the european economic and social committee, their mutual consultation on this matter is logical and highly necessary.
in writing. ‒ the primary objective of this regulation is to standardise the operation of the rail system in the european union through the harmonisation of the relevant structures and operating principles in the member states. for example, the need to submit multiple applications will be reduced. the eu railway agency itself will issue licences to all operators wishing to operate in the territory of more than one member state, thus engaging in cross-border transport. however, if the potential activity of the operator were to be limited to one member state, there would be a choice of where to obtain authorisation the edps or the relevant national authority. the same applies to the enhancement of the edps' role in the development of the european rail traffic management system. such further centralisation is necessary in particular because there are two track gauge systems 1435 mm and 1520 mm in the union. the latter also covers finland and the baltic states, thus making them a territorial periphery of the railway system in russia and the ussr. it is only logical that centralisation of powers would allow any issues in this respect to be dealt with more effectively. overall, i support this agreement as it will both contribute to the further development of the eu rail sector and will also contribute to certain savings for companies and manufacturers in the eu.
in writing. ‒ the principle objective of the railway legislative package is to achieve a higher degree of harmonisation between our member states in the railway sector. in parallel to the enhanced role of the eu railway agency already mentioned today, the number of existing national rules will be minimised in cases where common ones are or will be in place. this package will also set specific deadlines to be followed when implementing the new operational rules for the railway sector. rail transport is today - compared to the past and taking into account the fuel used - by far one of the cleanest and also the most cost-effective modes of transport. in fact, the only equally profitable means of transporting cargo is a cargo ship. it is also sufficiently fast and comfortable for passenger transport. it is therefore clear that the adoption of this legislation is of the utmost importance and is necessary to guarantee the further development of our community railway sector.
in writing. ‒ similar to the issuing of operating licences, the adoption of this package of legislation will give the eu railway agency the power to also issue safety certificates to cross-border operators and, if requested, to operators operating in the same country. another important aspect of railway safety is the much more intensive and general training of staff - first of all, of course, oriented towards those who are in direct contact with rail transport both inside and outside. in view of the overall safety situation in the union, this is clearly a step in the right direction. in parallel to the introduction of safety-enhancing measures, member states' authorities will need to monitor, for example, the compliance of drivers with the new rules on working hours, driving and rest periods. it is very similar to the system that exists, for example, for bus or truck drivers. another very important aspect is the improvement of the information exchange system, which is particularly important in any type of emergency situation. the same applies to the provision of information to relatives of victims.
in writing. ‒ manipulation of interest rates on london interbank borrowing, the currency benchmark and a number of other indices can often threaten the financial stability of entire countries not just their stock markets. i therefore welcomed the legislative amendments introduced, the primary aim of which is to make benchmark manipulation illegal and to promote a more efficient and fair regulatory system. to achieve this, it is necessary to improve management systems and standards, primarily for the most widely used benchmarks. it is also important to improve transparency and make information more widely available. given that a greater diversity of benchmarks would have a positive impact on economic processes, it would be logical to maintain a wide variety of benchmarks on offer and to have access to them. the european securities and markets authority will play a key role in this process by developing draft regulatory technical standards on the management and control requirements of benchmarks.
in writing. ‒ 2014 marked the beginning of the 2014-2020 programming period, which brought some changes to the work of the european commission and its executive agencies, which can be linked to a large extent to the implementation of the horizon 2020 programme. the total amount of commitments was eur 142,69 billion and the total amount of payments was eur 139,03 billion. the commission shall be empowered to adopt delegated acts in accordance with article 290 concerning the establishment of a european data protection supervisor. although there were still unresolved problems in 2014 with the receipt of full revenue from various sources, as well as spending irregularities, in particular in the european commission's international cooperation and development policy segment, it can be said that overall the budgetary commitments were implemented in a sufficiently effective manner this year.
in writing. ‒ as already noted, one of the main problems is that the external financial assistance provided by the european commission through its agencies in particular europeaid is very rarely qualitatively assessed after such expenditure has been incurred. in fact, billions of euros are lost to the black hole every year. moreover, the commission often does not require any reporting from those receiving its assistance. in other words, the commission needs to maximise the rationalisation of its policy of allocating financial resources, especially when it comes to external assistance geared towards development support in third countries, primarily in africa and asia. however, the same is true of the eu's neighbours. while such assistance is vitally important, our taxpayers need to understand where their money, which also makes up the bulk of the eu budget, is going in principle.
in writing. ‒ since its inception in 1959, the european development funds have been and remain the main instrument of the european union for providing assistance to the countries of the african, caribbean and pacific regions, as well as to the overseas countries and territories. they are outside the eu budget, but while the member states provide the funds through the council, the commission directly controls the way the funds are spent. in particular, in 2014, the total amount of the 8th, 9th, 10th, 11th edfs and co-financing amounted to eur 9,673 billion. while the union did provide enormous support to regions such as haiti or east africa, there were a total of 917 projects, of which 428 were then delayed or at risk of not achieving their objectives. the total amount of financing for such projects in 2014 was eur 9,188 billion. in other words, i again call on the commission, and on eu delegations around the world, to pay much closer attention to how our taxpayers' money is being spent in principle and whether there is any real, tangible benefit to be gained from these not-so-small sums spent.
in writing. ‒ in 2014, the total budget of the european parliament was eur 2.168 billion, with an utilisation rate of 80.4%. 72% of all actual expenditure was related to the members, officials and temporary staff of the institution, the maintenance of buildings and parliamentary assistance. in particular, 4% or eur 71,5 million was spent on the maintenance and renovation of the konrad adenauer building in luxembourg. it can be noted that among the party groups, the socialists and democrats spent the most on their financial resources (84% of the 2014 budget) and the least on the united european greens/nordic greens (54%). the commission will continue to monitor the implementation of the budget and the implementation of the budgetary discipline and the budgetary discipline. while there are still clearly various problems - administrative, financial and other - it can be concluded that parliament worked sufficiently efficiently and qualitatively in 2014, especially in view of a number of reforms initiated. although it would be better if our citizens were much more informed about this than they are today.
in writing. ‒ the total budget of the european council and the council for 2014 was eur 534.2 million, of which 91.3% was spent. in particular, the council's budget fell by 0.2% or eur 1.3 million this year. unfortunately, the report does not contain detailed financial data on expenditure during the year. i therefore unequivocally support the need to ensure parliament's access to information on the implementation of the council's annual budget. aside from the financial data, i would also like to join the calls for the council and the european council to engage as actively as possible with the european parliament in the decision-making and prior consultation process. the same applies to the lack of transparency in the trialogue.
in writing. ‒ in 2014, the court of justice of the european union became one of the few community institutions in which the court of auditors found no significant weaknesses in audited human resources and procurement, which is very welcome. the total budget for 2014 was eur 354,88 million, which was 99% used, an increase of 96,3% compared to 2013. 75% of the cost of the task and the human resources. as regards productivity, in 2014 the courts composing the court of justice, namely the eu general court and the civil service tribunal, received 1691 cases and closed 1685 cases. overall, the court of justice delivered a high-quality performance in 2014. however, we would like our citizens to be better informed about the legal possibilities to defend their interests also in this instance, when the need arises.
in writing. ‒ the court's primary task is to ensure that european union funds are correctly collected from all possible and legally derived sources and subsequently used in accordance with existing commitments. the court thus also provides assistance aimed at improving the financial management of the union. in 2014, the court's budget was €133,468 million less than a year earlier, compared with €142,761 million. the budget was implemented by 98,8%, which was more efficient than in 2013 by 92%. during the year, 91 reports and opinions were produced and the reform of the court's operation, which was aimed at improving the efficiency of the institution's work, was completed. i was pleased to note that the results of the 2014 work became the most comprehensive in the court's existence. it is hoped that the institution will continue to fulfil its duties as effectively as possible. although its reports and other documents do not have legal force, its work is clearly very important, first and foremost in terms of the overall improvement of the level of democracy in the eu's administrative institutions and the efficiency of the use of resources.
in writing. ‒ as in the case of the court of justice, the economic and social committee did not have a material error of budget implementation in 2014. the total budget amounted to eur 128,56 million, of which the institution used 95,6%. the total compensation for travel expenses and allowances was eur 17,376 million, which may be due, firstly, to the fact that the institution's work is heavily dependent on persons not permanently resident in brussels. the eesc's specific nature of work makes it one of the most important in eu governance, and i therefore joined the call for the institution to maximise its efforts to inform the citizens of the community and other stakeholders more effectively about its work and its direct and indirect impact on the daily lives of europeans. i congratulated the fact that the number of meetings using the videoconferencing facility, which allows the direct follow-up of the institution's meetings, has doubled compared to 2013.
in writing. ‒ the european committee of the regions, as we know, is an assembly of representatives at local and regional level, within which they can express their views directly and communicate with the other eu administrations, thus progressively increasing the regions' participation in the union's governance. the 2014 budget was eur 87,6 million, which was used by 98,5%. i was very disappointed to see that the committee of the regions' rent debt had reached over eur 65 million at the end of 2014, and i therefore strongly urge the committee of the regions to find effective long-term solutions to this problem together with the european parliament. the commission notes that the commission has not provided any information on the amount of the aid granted to the beneficiary. i was pleased to note that in 2014 the european charter for multi-level governance was adopted, which aims to develop new forms of dialogue and partnership between all public authorities in the european union in order to optimise public policy orientations and public spending, as well as to improve the implementation of community policies.
in writing. ‒ the european external action service, also known as the european union's ministry of foreign affairs, is one of the most important eu administrative mechanisms responsible for the external dimension of the policy to be implemented. in 2014, the eeas budget was eur 518,6 million, while the overall error rate in the administrative budget was 0,5%, according to the court of auditors' report. however, in the case of ex ante and ex post controls, the level of error found in the commitments and payments to be verified was estimated at 18%. in total, eur 212.9 million was used to maintain the central office and a further 305.7 million for the union delegations. i strongly supported the call for the eeas to maximise its efforts to coordinate more closely the promotion of foreign policy with the member states' foreign ministries and missions at different levels abroad. the same applies to the need to develop a comprehensive strategy and vision that would contain guidelines for the implementation of the union's external policy, in particular in the light of the rapidly changing world situation.
in writing. ‒ in 2014, according to the court's report, neither the audit findings on human resources and procurement shortcomings nor the significant errors in administrative expenditure were found, which is very welcome. the total budget for 2014 was eur 9,857 million. during the year, the authority responded to 1823 requests for information, registered 2079 complaints, provided 19170 advice through an interactive guide on its website, opened 342 investigations (325 following complaints and a further 17 on its own initiative) and closed 400 investigations (387 based on complaints and a further 13 on its own initiative). the european ombudsman, together with the court of auditors, the court of justice and other similar institutions, forms a kind of institutional 'group' whose members have the task of administering the policies of the european union, with the utmost attention to the protection of the interests and rights of its citizens. we therefore hope that the person who takes up the post of european ombudsman will continue to work actively for the benefit of europeans.
in writing. ‒ the european data protection supervisor is responsible for monitoring the processing of personal data in the eu administration and ensuring compliance with data protection rules. the authority shall cooperate closely with parliament, the commission and the council, as well as with other data protection authorities, primarily in the member states. the total budget for 2014 was eur 8,013 million, of which 92% was used. the commission has also received information from the united kingdom on the implementation of the council's recommendations on the use of the financial corrections mechanism. the work of the edps is undeniably of great importance, especially in the light of the negotiations on the same transatlantic trade and investment partnership and the privacy concerns it has raised. it is hoped that, regardless of the outcome of this or any other negotiations (e.g. with google), the privacy rights of europeans will be fully preserved and respected.
in writing. ‒ in 2014, the total budget of eu agencies was around eur 1.9 billion, 5% less than the previous year. this €1.9 billion in turn represented around 1.4% of the eu's general budget. it should be noted that only 63% (eur 1.2 billion) came from eu funding the rest came from other sources. in total, these agencies employed 6578 staff at that time, or 14% of the total number of union officials. it should be noted that 93 per cent of agencies had joint services with other union agencies and institutions in 2014. hopefully, this will increase in the future. fortunately, 29 agencies (or more than 90%) had a policy of publishing cvs and declarations of interests of board members, senior staff and external and staff experts, which play an important role in promoting transparency, although only 23 agencies published them on their websites. interestingly, only half published information on their websites in all the official languages of the union, while 9% of the information was available only in english. it is understood that full linguistic availability is not possible in many cases, but it is imperative to publish information in at least three of the most widely used languages of the union english, german and french , which is the absolute minimum.

in writing. ‒ the total budget of the european centre for the development of vocational training in 2014 was eur 17.276 million, which was 3.62% less than in 2013. the budget implementation rate reached 95,68%, which was 3,29% higher than in 2013. the centre's activities in 2014 did not overlap with those of other eu, member state or international bodies, but potential synergies with the european training foundation (etf), the european foundation for the improvement of living and working conditions (eurofound) and the european agency for safety and health at work (eu-osha) were identified. in particular, there was a very close cooperation with the etf and eurofound. it is to be welcomed that the centre has actively used its website, social media, organised events in the host member state (greece) and actively cooperated with the commission on a number of publications and press releases to promote its work and to raise the visibility of vocational education and training opportunities in the union as a whole.

in writing. ‒ given its scope and specificity, the european aviation safety agency is undoubtedly one of the most important eu agencies. it is the authority which authorises foreign operators to operate in the territory of the union and ensures that safety rules are properly implemented in the community, primarily at airports and on aircraft, in close cooperation with the national authorities. in 2014, easa's budget amounted to eur 181,18 million, which is 8,47% more than the previous year. it should be noted that only 21,1% of easa's annual budget was financed from the union budget. the level of compliance decreased to 97,1%, which was 0,9% lower than in 2013. although easa was established in 2004, a comprehensive headquarters agreement with germany was still not signed in 2014 which would significantly contribute to improving the functioning of the agency on the ground. in view of the air disasters of that period alone, such as the disappearance of mh370, the dramatic crash of mh17, the air asia qz8501 accident, etc., i joined the call to easa to pay much closer attention to the operation of foreign operators in the union and, where possible, in its neighbours, and to intensify cooperation with foreign partners.
in writing. ‒ with the emergence of the migrant and refugee crisis last year, the role of the european asylum support office has already increased significantly compared to previous years. the commission also found that the union industry had a significant market share in the union market, which was significantly higher than the union industry's market share. the easo budget for 2014 was eur 15,664 million, of which 94% came from the union budget. interestingly, the office experienced a high turnover in 2014 with the departure of 14 staff, including 4 from senior management. although this may theoretically be linked to the current crisis, this trend is in any case not good, as it does not allow the institution to carry out its functional duties fully and efficiently. i unequivocally joined the call for the office to make its policy on the prevention and management of conflicts of interest as publicly available as possible, which was still not achieved in 2014. the same applies to the prevention of corruption, which is neither de facto nor de jure permissible in an eu institution.
in writing. ‒ the european banking authority is an independent union body tasked with ensuring an effective and appropriate level of regulation and supervision throughout the european banking sector. the authority was established in 2011 in part as a response to the financial crisis of 2008-2009. in the context of its functions, eba shall cooperate very closely with the european insurance and occupational pensions authority and the european securities and markets authority. the total budget for 2014 was eur 33,6 million, an increase of 29,39% compared to 2013. the authority shall be financed by contributions from both the union (40%) and the member states (60%). the level of implementation of the 2014 budget was 99,81%, which is 2,28% higher than the previous year. unfortunately, in 2014 the authority did not sufficiently and comprehensively inform parliament of all the details of the work carried out at that time, and i therefore unambiguously joined the call to remedy this shortcoming.
in writing. ‒ the european centre for disease prevention and control has the primary task of improving the protection of the community against infectious diseases. for example, the centre currently analyses and interprets data from the member states on 52 communicable diseases through the european monitoring system, and provides scientific advice to member states' governments and institutions. the importance of the institution has been particularly heightened by the outbreaks of ebola and zika virus in the vicinity of europe. in the case of ebola, the centre successfully responded to 117 emergency cases in 2014 in the context of urgent disease control measures. the total budget for 2014 was eur 60,486 million, an increase of 3,72% compared to 2013. 97% were financed from the union budget. the level of implementation reached 98.77%, 5.81% higher than a year earlier. one of the main achievements of 2014 - apart from the above mentioned ebola response and other profile tasks - was the development and availability of the infectious disease surveillance atlas on the centre's website, which gave wide access to this important information to institutions, the public, various organisations, etc.
in writing. ‒ the european chemicals agency (or the european chemicals agency) is responsible for promoting the safe use of chemicals. the european chemicals agency (echa) is implementing new union legislation on chemicals, thus taking care of the health of europeans, the environment, innovation and competitiveness in europe. recalling the series of votes on the acceptability of glyphosate in the member states of the union, it can be noted that echa itself is one of the primary actors in the initial [scientific] assessment of this issue. in 2014, the total budget amounted to eur 114,112 million, an increase of 6,37% compared to the previous year. the bulk of this sum comes from the various types of levies, contributions, levies, etc. each year, rather than from the union budget as such. the implementation rate in 2014 was 97,08%. it is worth noting that the agency devotes 78% of its human resources to its core functions. at the end of 2014, 479 of the 495 staff posts available had been filled and 118 contract staff and seconded national experts were employed. overall, the agency's work can be classified as of sufficient quality, although it would be desirable if the commission's communication with echa on various issues, in particular the acceptability of chemicals on the eu market, were more transparent than it is at present.
in writing. ‒ the european environment agency provides information and assessments on the state of the environment, trends in environmental development, policies and their effectiveness, as well as possible trends and challenges for the future. it is worth noting that the efta countries are 32 member states excluding the 28 eu member states plus iceland, liechtenstein, norway, switzerland and turkey. the 2014 budget amounted to eur 52,573 million, an increase of 6,7% compared to 2013. of these, 76,81% were financed from the union budget. the level of compliance reached a whopping 99.19%. given the trends in environmental developments over the past decades, including the potential impact on the economy and other areas, and the recently adopted paris declaration, the role of the eea is undoubtedly very important and will continue to be so.
in writing. ‒ the european fisheries control agency was set up to promote the implementation of common standards for control, inspection and surveillance to be carried out under the common fisheries policy. in other words, the member states' quota management, fisheries and fisheries regulations - all of these and many others - are also the responsibility of the efca, which it shares with the commission and the relevant member states' authorities. the budget for 2014 was eur 9,217 million, fully financed by the union budget. the budget implementation rate was 99.09%, 0.45% higher than a year earlier. it is to be welcomed that in 2014 the agency has committed to introducing a section on its website to introduce visitors to efca's work in all official languages of the union, as well as in galician, given efca's location in this historic region of spain. it should be noted in particular that, throughout its existence, the efca has made a significant contribution to the implementation of the cfp and is actively cooperating with the member states.
in writing. ‒ the work of the european food safety authority covers all issues that directly or indirectly affect the safety of food and feed, including animal health and welfare, plant protection and plant health, and nutrition. it should be noted that this body plays one of the most important roles in the possibility of placing various products on the eu market by issuing the relevant licences. it also includes gmos, pesticides, food additives and nanotechnology products. the budget for 2014 was eur 79,701 million, an increase of 2,11% compared to 2013. the total amount was paid from the union budget. the budget implementation rate was 99.69%, 0.86% higher than in the previous year. given that the scandals involving efsa have been occurring for more than a year, i urge the agency to pay the utmost attention to further promoting the transparency of its work and to cooperate more closely with the scientific community. although there is some parallel between these two approaches to the promotion of work in civil society, it would be desirable to intensify efforts in this direction.
in writing. ‒ the tasks of the european institute for gender equality include collecting and analysing comparable data on gender issues, developing a methodology to integrate gender equality aspects into all policy areas, promoting exchanges of good practice and facilitating dialogue between stakeholders, and increasing public awareness. the 2014 budget amounted to eur 7,34 million, 3,08% less than the previous year. the budget implementation rate was 99,04%. in particular, i would like to acknowledge the very active regular cooperation between the eige and the parliament's femm committee. the biological and philosophical 'dualism' of gender equality aside, its promotion of a qualitative solution is clearly one of the most positive real drivers of economic growth and social cohesion in the union. i therefore unreservedly wish the institute success and fruitful work in the years ahead, which will certainly contribute to a more open and progressive europe.
in writing. ‒ the european insurance and occupational pensions authority was established in 2010 to prevent risks of destabilisation in the insurance sector, to ensure financial stability and to promote a more integrated, transparent, efficient and secure financial market. the esaw thus became the 'incarnation of the institutional response' to the financial crisis of 2008-09 when the eu financial supervisory system revealed very significant weaknesses. the authority's budget for 2014 was eur 21,583 million, an increase of 15% compared to 2013. 40% was financed from the union budget. the eiopa does not have a decision-making power its task is largely to advise the commission, the council and the parliament. i therefore call for intensified efforts in this direction, by providing full information on the data used and the adapted methodology of analysis in all projects and technical opinions on a continuous basis.
in writing. ‒ the european institute of innovation and technology, which, like the european police academy, is located in the hungarian capital budapest, has the primary task of promoting innovation in the eu member states. the eit helps businesses, education and research institutions to work together to create an environment conducive to innovation and entrepreneurship. in other words, the institute's work has a clear practical element. the eit budget for 2014 was eur 233,115 million, an increase of 65,54% compared to 2013. such a major change is due to the extension of the institution's functions and the increase in the number of beneficiaries. the budget implementation rate decreased from 96,97% a year earlier to 94,13%. as the effectiveness of the institute's work depends to a large extent on the level of awareness of the general public and stakeholders, it is very welcome that the eit started operating a new website in 2014, which is much more dynamic and functional than the previous one.
in writing. ‒ the european medicines agency's main task is to protect and promote public and animal health by carrying out the evaluation and monitoring of medicines for human and veterinary use. it is important to note that the ema is directly responsible for the scientific evaluation of applications for european medicines authorisations. in other words, without an ema authorisation, no medicinal product can be legally marketed in the territory of the eu member states. in 2014, the agency's budget amounted to eur 282,474 million, an increase of 12,29% compared to 2013. only 12,53% of the ema budget was financed from the union budget. the rate of implementation was 94,32%, which was 2,44% lower than the previous year. the concerns were unequivocally joined by the fact that, without the appropriate approval of the commission or the parliament, the ema started a pilot project on adaptive licensing in march 2014, which could in fact lead to a situation where the medicinal product is licensed after it has entered the community market, which is simply not acceptable. it is clear that, both in terms of the facts and in terms of the scope of the activities, ema should ensure maximum transparency in access to clinical reports, which also applies to the provision of information on all aspects of the work carried out by the agency.
in writing. ‒ the european monitoring centre for drugs and drug addiction is an eu body tasked with collecting, analysing and disseminating factual, reliable, credible and comparable information on drugs and drug addiction, while providing europeans with a comprehensive and evidence-based picture of the drug problem. the emcdda actively collaborates with a wide range of scientists, policy-makers and practitioners. in 2014, the centre's budget amounted to eur 15,675 million, which is 3,88% less than in 2013. 93,4% were financed from the union budget. the budget implementation rate was 99,62%. drug distribution and drug addiction are problems in the european union, in all its member states, especially the less developed ones. one of the main problems is the use of drugs by children and adolescents, such as the distribution of all kinds of 'legal drugs' that can be bought even by minors. in other words, the work of the emcdda, which also covers these issues, is clearly very important and will hopefully continue to be quite effective in the years ahead.
in writing. ‒ the european maritime safety agency provides technical assistance to the union and its member states in the development and implementation of eu legislation in areas such as safety of navigation, pollution from ships and maritime safety. this also applies to preventive measures to prevent oil spills, the organisation of rescue operations and the surveillance of ships. emsa also ensures the identification and tracking of long-range ship movements. the 2014 budget amounted to eur 52,4 million, which is 9,37% less than the previous year. the level of compliance was 94,78%. from a practical point of view, it is very welcome that 20.3% of emsa's posts are allocated to administrative support and coordination, 70.8% to core activities and 6.6% to control and financial tasks. the emsa cooperates actively with other eu agencies, such as the european fisheries control agency and the european monitoring centre for drugs and drug addiction, which is located in relative geographical proximity.
in writing. ‒ the european union agency for network and information security is the eu's specialist centre of expertise in the field of cybersecurity. it helps the union and the member states to be better equipped and prepared to prevent, detect and address information security problems. enisa actively cooperates with eu agencies such as the european police college, the office of the european regulator for electronic communications, the agency for the operational management of large-scale it systems in the area of freedom, security and justice and the european aviation safety agency. this also applies to the nato cyber defence centre in tallinn, the capital of estonia. the budget for 2014 was eur 9,708 million, an increase of 0,39% compared to 2013. the implementation rate of 100% is very welcome. according to 2014 data, 68% of the agency's staff were assigned to the operational function category, 22% to the administrative support and coordination category and 10% to the neutral function category.
in writing. ‒ the european railway agency, whose functioning reform was already discussed today, has as its primary task to help maximise the integration of the european railway systems so that train traffic is safer and continues across europe without stopping at national borders. this therefore also applies very closely to the harmonisation of differences in track gauge systems. the 2014 budget amounted to eur 25,716 million, fully financed by the union budget. this amount was 0,55 % lower than a year earlier. the budget implementation rate was 97,34%, which was 0,53%, lower than in 2013. the era is located in two french cities: lille and valence. although they are located in close proximity to each other, this does, to some extent, impede the full and effective functioning of the agency. i was pleased to join in the appreciation of the fact that the agency's role in the development, testing and post-audit of the european rail traffic management system (ertms) was very productive and positive.
in writing. ‒ the european securities and markets authority is tasked with improving investor protection and promoting the stability and orderly functioning of financial markets. the authority was established in 2011 as one of the primary responses to the financial crisis of 2008-2009. the supervisory board of esma shall be composed of the heads of the relevant authorities of all 28 member states, observers from iceland, liechtenstein and norway, and the commission, as well as representatives of the european banking authority, the european insurance and occupational pensions authority and the european systemic risk college. in other words, the institution is responsible for pursuing its objectives also outside the eu, which, given its scope of activity, is clearly a logical and necessary step. in 2014, the budget amounted to eur 33,267 million, an increase of 18,02% compared to the previous year, due in large part to the additional tasks assigned. the budget implementation rate reached 98,89%, which is 5,71% higher than in 2013. it should be noted that, in accordance with parliament's decision, the commission will be invited in the white paper foreseen for the second quarter of this year and in the legislative proposal to be submitted by 2017 to propose a different financing arrangement for the annual budget of the authority with the aim of fully replacing contributions from the competent authorities of the member states with fees to be paid by market participants. this is expected to significantly enhance the independence of the institution's work.
in writing. ‒ the european education fund helps partner countries to modernise their education and training systems. in other words, this agency does not deal with education within the eu its geographical focus is on the eu enlargement countries, the eu neighbourhood policy countries, russia and the countries of central asia. in addition, the etf has a wide cooperation network with organisations worldwide, including, for example, bilateral funding agencies in the eu member states, the world bank, the organisation for economic cooperation and development and the united nations. in 2014, the budget amounted to eur 20,158 million, which is 0,07% more than in the previous year. the budget shall be financed in full by the union. the implementation rate for 2014 was 99,9%. the etf's work is undoubtedly of fundamental importance, although it is unfortunately currently very hampered by instability in the middle east and north africa region and in ukraine. it is hoped that the fund will be able to resume and/or continue its objectives in these and other partner countries in the shortest possible time.
in writing. ‒ the european agency for the operational management of large-scale it systems in the area of freedom, security and justice, set up at the end of 2012, works to enable border guards, police, customs and courts to exchange and compare information in their databases within the eu, for example on visas or asylum requests. in 2014, the budget amounted to eur 59,38 million, 3,2% less than the previous year. it shall be fully financed from the union budget. its compliance rate was 99,32%. eu-lisa shall cooperate closely with other eu agencies in the field of home affairs, such as the european agency for the management of operational cooperation at the external borders of the member states of the eu, the european police office, the european police college, the european asylum support office and the european monitoring centre for drugs and drug addiction. eu-lisa thus plays a very important supporting role in the work of these and other institutions, especially in the present context of the worsening migrant/refugee crisis.
in writing. ‒ the european agency for safety and health at work's main task is to collect, analyse and disseminate information to those (specialists, institutions, etc.) working in the field of safety and health at work. making workplaces safer, healthier and more productive, and promoting a culture of risk prevention, is eu-osha's objective. the main partners are the european foundation for the improvement of living and working conditions, the european chemicals agency, the european centre for the development of vocational training, the european institute for gender equality, the fundamental rights agency and the european education fund. the budget for 2014 was eur 17,256 million, which is 1,17% less than in 2013. of these, the union contribution was eur 14,987 million, down 4,02% on the previous year. the budget implementation rate was 98,65%. it is worth noting that the eu-osha information network is made up of contact points in more than 30 european countries, usually appointed by the government of each particular country and usually national occupational safety and health authorities.
in writing. ‒ the euratom supply agency is responsible for ensuring the regular and fair supply of nuclear fuel to users in the member states of the european union. to this end, the eaa applies a supply policy based on the principle of equal access to sources of supply. the 2014 budget was eur 104 000 , the same as the previous year. even considering that the agency has a staff of approximately 15 and that none of them is directly involved in the supply of nuclear fuel to consumers, the agency's role in the union's nuclear energy is undeniably very important, although of course only those countries where nuclear power plants or nuclear fuel suppliers are active can directly benefit from it.
in writing. ‒ the european foundation for the improvement of living and working conditions is intended to contribute to the development of social and employment policies within the union, based on research. the institution was established in 1975, making it and the european centre for the development of vocational training the two oldest institutions. eurofound's main objective is to help design and build better living and working conditions. in 2014, the budget amounted to eur 20,774 million, an increase of 0,73% compared to the previous year. the level of compliance was 99,66%. i welcome the completion of three major reports in 2014: 'psychosocial risks in europe', 'labour mobility in the eu latest trends and policy positions' and 'social cohesion and well-being in the eu'. these and a number of other studies and reports have been and continue to be used regularly in the work of the union and member states' institutions, which is to be welcomed.
in writing. ‒ the european judicial cooperation unit helps law enforcement authorities in the member states to cooperate with each other in order to combat serious forms of organised crime affecting at least two member states at the same time. in other words, the institution has an important coordinating role. the unit shall be composed of one representative from each eu country, within the college of eurojust. the budget for 2014 was eur 33,667 million, an increase of 4,04% compared to 2013. the level of implementation reached 99.82%, 0.21% higher than a year earlier. given the specific nature of the institution's work, i supported the idea of making the common rules governing lobbying in the case of eurojust as specific and harmonised as possible, although this of course applies to all union institutions and agencies at least because their work has a significant impact on all our member states, not just one or a few.
in writing. ‒ the european police office is tasked with helping member states' law enforcement agencies to combat serious transnational crime and terrorism more effectively. the european parliament has also been working closely with its foreign partners on the same lines. the budget for 2014 was eur 84,34 million, an increase of 2,2% compared to 2013. the level of compliance was 99,7%. given the fundamental role of europol in the fight against crime in the union, i unanimously supported the request that the office regularly publish its consolidated annual activity report, rather than just the general report. at least, it would provide a much clearer picture of the situation in this area in the union.
in writing. ‒ the european union agency for fundamental rights is tasked with providing independent, evidence-based recommendations in the field of fundamental rights to decision-makers at union and national level. this applies, for example, to the various forms of discrimination, access to justice, racism and xenophobia, data protection, victims' rights or children's rights. in 2014, the budget amounted to eur 21,229 million, which is 0,56% less than the previous year. approximately 99% of the fra budget was financed from the union budget. the budget implementation rate was 100% in 2014 for the second consecutive year. it is to be welcomed that the basic information on the agency is available on its website in all the official languages of the union. in addition, the fra is very active in using social media to provide europeans with important information about its activities on a regular basis. another positive element in principle is the creation in february 2014 of a dedicated webpage for children, part of the agency's website on children's rights, where they can find information about their rights in the most accessible and understandable language.
in writing. ‒ over the last few years, the role of the european agency for the management of operational cooperation at the external borders of the member states of the european union in implementing union policies has become one of the most important of all eu agencies as the migrant/refugee crisis has worsened. the tasks of frontex shall be to coordinate operational cooperation between member states in the field of external border management, to assist member states in the implementation of training of national border guards (as well as in the establishment of common training standards), to facilitate risk analysis, to develop studies appropriate to its field of competence on the current situation and its future development prospects, to assist member states with enhanced technical and operational assistance at the external borders where necessary, and to provide member states with the necessary support in organising joint return operations. the 2014 budget amounted to eur 97,945 million, an increase of 4,25% compared to the previous year. in particular, the union contribution was eur 86,81 million, which was 1,53% lower than the previous year. as you know, the structure and operational specifications of frontex have recently been substantially reformed and extended, as have the annual budget and staff. given the current circumstances, it is likely that the situation will not change much in the coming years without further much needed reforms in a number of policies and other areas.
in writing. ‒ the european gnss agency is responsible for managing the public interest in the eu's global navigation satellite system programmes the european geostationary navigation overlay systems (egnos) and galileo. the specificities of the agency's work thus overlap closely with several eu policies, which in turn means that the gsa actively cooperates with a large number of union and member state authorities. in 2014, the budget amounted to eur 25,369 million, 81,55% more than in the previous year. the level of compliance was 99,84%. it is to be welcomed that in 2014, the agency organised open days, conferences, public exhibitions and regular public events in close cooperation with the commission, with the aim of promoting its work and improving its visibility in the host member state (czech republic). it is hoped that the agency will continue to actively pursue activities in this direction in the future.
in writing. ‒ the mission of the artificial intelligence systems joint undertaking, or artemis, is to help the european union's industry to consolidate and extend its global leadership in the field of artificial intelligence technology. artemis was set up at the end of 2007 for a period of 10 years, during which its maximum union funding could reach eur 420 million. the member states' contribution must be at least 1,8 times the contribution from the union budget. although the joint undertaking has been and continues to be quite effective in achieving its creation objectives in practice throughout its existence, i join in expressing concern that throughout all these years artemis has never once taken any action with regard to the various internal control standards relating to information and financial statements. it is hoped that this shortfall will be remedied towards the end of the 10-year period.
in writing. ‒ the clean sky 2 joint undertaking, which replaced the clean sky joint undertaking for a further 10 years in 2014, is tasked with developing innovative technologies to help reduce co2 and other emissions from aircraft, as well as noise, for different types of aircraft. in the case of the first iteration, the maximum union contribution could have been up to eur 800 million, while clean sky 2 already had a contribution of eur 1,755 billion. this appropriation is intended to cover the financing of the following measures: the remaining members of the joint undertaking will thus have to invest eur 2,194 billion. these investments may be in cash or in kind. the commission shall be empowered to adopt delegated acts in accordance with article 264 concerning the establishment of a european data protection supervisor. although less than agriculture, industry, or cars, airplanes (and airplanes in particular) have a significant negative impact on the environment. the clean sky 2 joint undertaking's work is therefore of great importance and will hopefully continue to be as efficient as possible in the future.
in writing. ‒ the ecsel joint undertaking's tasks include, inter alia, fostering a strong and globally competitive electronic components and systems industry in the european union, coordinating a strategy with member states to attract private investment, and ensuring the availability of electronic components and systems in key markets. in other words, there is a deep interconnection between the scientific, economic and industrial aspects of the joint undertaking, as well as the social aspects. it is important to note that one of the objectives of the ecsel joint undertaking is to functionally replace the artemis and eniac joint undertakings. the commission shall be empowered to adopt delegated acts in accordance with article 290 concerning the conditions for the participation of third countries in the work of the european external action service. given the importance of the sector concerned for the further development of the economies of the member states of the union and of several other areas, it is hoped that ecsel will bring about ever closer cooperation not only between the eu, the associated countries and various other bodies, but also beyond, as the growth potential here is clearly very high. the same applies to the need to take more active account of the findings and recommendations of the joint undertaking's audits, which are not currently being fully implemented.
in writing. ‒ the eniac joint undertaking, like artemis and its successor ecsel, is active in the field of nanoelectronics, bringing together the commission, the eu member states, its associated countries and a number of smes, scientific institutes, universities, etc. the primary objective of eniac is therefore to develop core competencies in nanoelectronics for different applications. the maximum union contribution to this joint undertaking could potentially reach eur 450 million by the end of 2017. the member states, on the other hand, have to cover 1,8 times the amount and provide contributions in kind to cover the operational costs of the joint undertaking. the commission notes that the commission has not provided any information on the amount of the aid granted to the beneficiaries. given the scope and potential of eniac, as well as the actual benefits that this joint undertaking could bring and has already brought to the economic and technological development of the community, it should also be important to understand the amount of resources invested in promoting the activities of the joint undertaking.
in writing. ‒ the main objective of the fuel cells and hydrogen joint undertaking is to maximise its positive contribution towards the creation of a low-carbon economy in europe by 2050. reducing the cost of fuel cell production, improving electricity capacity and efficiency, reducing the use of critical or rare materials and other sub-tasks are on the list of topical actions. the joint undertaking was established in 2014 to replace the first iteration, fuel cells and hydrogen 2. the maximum contribution from the union for the periods of operation of the joint undertakings shall be eur 470 million for the yei1 and eur 665 million for the yei2. sectoral groups are also involved, both financially and in kind. i added to this appreciation the fact that the eeas has published a programme review report every year since 2011, as well as a report on the socio-economic impact of its activities.
in writing. ‒ the mission of the innovative medicines initiative 2 joint undertaking is to promote drug development by facilitating open research collaborations that have the potential to accelerate the development of the medicines needed. this important work brings together academia, the pharmaceutical and other industries, small and medium-sized enterprises, patient organisations and drug regulators. the maximum union contribution to imi over the lifetime of the imi will be eur 1 billion, while imi2 (which replaced the first in 2014) will be eur 1,638 billion. in the case of imi2, the other participants (excluding the commission) are to bear 50% of the operational costs and to contribute to the coverage of the operating costs in cash or in kind. the joint undertaking's contribution to the implementation of the programme is estimated at eur 5 million.
in writing. ‒ the iter and f4e joint undertaking, also known as the 'f4e joint undertaking', is designed to promote the progressive development of fusion energy, which could eventually lead to demonstration participation in the construction of fusion reactors. the joint undertaking was established in 2007 for a period of 35 years. initially, €6.6 billion was allocated to the first phase of construction, but this amount actually increased each year. moreover, the implementation of the phase is already delayed this year. the commission has also proposed to amend the financial rules of the f4e joint undertaking in order to take account of the new rules on the financial rules of the f4e joint undertaking. nuclear power was once thought to be the crown jewel of energy evolution. today, it is nuclear fusion. it is therefore hoped that f4e will resolve as soon as possible the financial and project implementation problems that have existed for years and will be able to make a positive contribution to the development of the european energy sector.
in writing. ‒ the sesar joint undertaking serves as the research and development platform on which the future air traffic management system of the single european sky will be built. today, sesar brings together some 3000 experts from the european union and other regions of the world. in terms of budget, the development phase of the sesar project (2008-2016) was allocated a total amount of eur 2,1 billion, to be shared equally between the union, eurocontrol and the public and private partners involved in the project. it is commendable that 348 (or 94%) of the 369 projects under the sesar programme were either in progress or completed as of the end of 2014. the quality and safety of air traffic are one of the cornerstones of the development and de facto quality of life in europe and the world, and it is therefore hoped that the sesar joint undertaking will continue to work as productively as possible.
in writing. ‒ in 2014, the european investment bank financed a total of 285 000 smes, which employed some 3.6 million people. 413 contracts for projects within the union were also signed, worth eur 69 billion, and a further 92 contracts for projects outside the community, worth eur 7.98 billion. given its contribution to the economic and other development of the union and the various partner countries, it can be conclusively concluded that the eib has one of the most positive roles in improving low levels of investment for the benefit of member states' economies. i joined the call for the eib to give the utmost attention to investments in regions with the highest unemployment rates, as well as in less developed regions, particularly in the periphery of the union. in this context, i am pleased that in 2014 a large amount of eur 19.9 billion (or 29% of total investments made during the year) was released in support of cohesion policy. another very positive aspect of the eib's work in 2014 was the financing of 84 projects in the field of environmental protection and improvement, with a total value of eur 12.6 billion. i support the view that the eib's annual reports should pay more attention to the results of completed projects, where the effectiveness of eib financing is assessed.
in writing. ‒ in warfare, unimaginable atrocities are often committed, either intentionally or unintentionally. in the second case, they may be justified at least in theory, but in the first case, not in principle. only moral hazards can deliberately endanger innocent people and children who suffer in conflict. i strongly condemn the use of civilians as human shields by parties to armed conflicts. islamic state, boko haram, hamas and other terrorist organizations often do exactly what is neither permissible nor morally justifiable. i added my deep respect for teachers, doctors and other professionals, such as humanitarian workers, for their continued courage and determination to work in conditions where the inevitable could happen at any moment. i strongly urge my colleagues and i to call on international institutions (in particular the un security council), states, ngos and any other actors with a say in ending such attacks to act in this direction. even warfare has to have its own rules, which by definition must be implemented.
in writing. ‒ public access to documents is one of the most important aspects of promoting democracy in the european union and its member states. it is a good thing that, say, during each session, documents relating to the issues dealt with during that session appear on parliament's website. the same applies to the large number of regulations, agreements, drafts and other documents in the case of the commission, the council and other community institutions. the problem is that often the information provided is either incomplete or is released too late. this creates a situation where both ordinary citizens and interested parties cannot understand in a secular way what is on the agenda and what is in principle happening in the union's administration. another problem is that the information itself may be located somewhere, yet it has become a quest to find it. it is logical that the information to which they are subject should be made publicly available, but this is often not done. i therefore unequivocally joined the call on all eu institutions to apply stricter transparency rules by making the relevant material public. the creation of a single lobby register became a very important step in this process. it is hoped that, as elsewhere, information that is not in any way classified will be made public in an open and accessible manner sooner rather than later.
in writing. ‒ in the modern era, given the demographic pattern of europe, the economic situation, migration flows and a number of other factors, in particular the increased participation of women in the labour market, the group of workers such as household workers is becoming increasingly important. caring for the elderly, children or the disabled, simple cleaning, cooking or other tasks are very important tasks that employers often cannot or do not want to do themselves. the problem is that often such employment is not declared, contracted or fairly paid. violence or extreme psychological stress may even be present. the trade unions concerned often do not exist at all. women are, of course, the most vulnerable group, especially from migrant backgrounds. in addition, this is very often the case with europeans themselves, such as poles, hungarians, latvians and other central and eastern europeans (especially ukrainians and moldovans) who go to work in this field from their own countries. the fight against undeclared employment, potential violence at work, the associated social exclusion and other problems must be a focus in the member states and also at the level of the whole community. the commission should also be able to provide information on the implementation of the new legislation, in particular on the legal basis for the new legislation.
in writing. ‒ with the rapid development of new technologies and the relatively recent beginnings of the digital age, gender equality no longer seems unattainable. the rapid expansion of internet access opens all these doors to a degree that was not possible before. to enable women to make the most of existing and potential opportunities, thereby promoting greater gender equality, it is necessary to promote the wider dissemination and availability of relevant information, to further develop the maximum general coverage of the internet and, of course, to rationalise aspects of our legislative systems that deal with these issues. the net economic effect cannot ignore the enormous potential of the full integration of both sides of humanity in the same labour market and other fields.
mr president, colleagues! there are complicated things, and there are very simple things. the issue of the nord stream ii pipeline expansion is, in my view, a very simple one. let's think about drugs and drug addiction. we all know that drug addiction is very dangerous and must be overcome. like drugs, the european union is dependent on imported gas. we already have almost 70% of imports and the trend is to increase even more. who is our biggest supplier of gas? it is the russian export monopoly gazprom. several european companies are currently considering doubling the so-called nord stream gas pipeline - nord stream ii - in this way, together with russia's gazprom. what would that do? it's like a drug addict getting injected with twice the dose. it's a matter of life and death. this is dangerous for europe. we can't go that way. fortunately, as sometimes happens with drugs, there is a cure, and that cure is the course that we have accepted, that the commission has proposed and that we have supported - the so-called energy union. the principles of the energy union make it clear that we must reduce our dependence on imports, we must harness our energy sources and we cannot allow any kind of monopoly on the european market. this single market must be created. nord stream ii is a dangerous drug. commissioner, i urge you to be brave and do everything you can to prevent this project from happening! thank you for your attention.
mr president, i would like to thank you for your time. yesterday, all of europe celebrated the day of the nazi extermination. it is a momentous event in all of human history. in my country, latvia, the day of the victory over nazism is rationally celebrated on may 8th, and, as many families do, on may 9th. just imagine, colleagues, even at this late hour, the squares of riga and other cities in latvia are full of people as people continue to celebrate the victory over nazism. europe was born after the nazi conquest. the european union project is the idea of a europe of solidarity, a europe of fraternity, a europe without borders, and so i believe that today, more than ever, we have a duty to show solidarity with the people who have lived for decades in latvia and estonia with the imperfections of latvia and estonia, and to give them the right to vote for the european parliament. thank you.
in writing. ‒ the new regulation aims to transpose the provisions of eu law on subsidies and countervailing measures concluded and codified within the world trade organisation in order to ensure the correct and transparent application of anti-subsidy rules. the regulation sets out the detailed conditions under which the existence of subsidisation is identified, the principles governing the applicability of countervailing duties which could be imposed to offset subsidies directly or indirectly granted for any manufacture, production, export or transport of products whose release for free circulation in the european union causes injury and the criteria which would be applicable in calculating the amount of the countervailable subsidies. the regulation itself does not introduce anything new in legal terms, but merely consolidates existing provisions. it was therefore logical to support the new codification.
in writing. ‒ the new regulation aims to transpose into eu law the anti-dumping provisions contained in the text of the general agreement on tariffs and trade adopted in 1994, more specifically its implementing agreement, article 6. the regulation therefore contains detailed provisions, in particular as regards the calculation of the dumping facts, the conduct of the investigation procedure, the imposition and collection of anti-dumping duties, the duration and review of anti-dumping measures, and the provision of relevant information to the public in the context of anti-dumping investigations. as in the previous vote today, this regulation does not as such bring any new legal provisions, but merely consolidates existing ones. i therefore support the new codification as an integral part of eu law.

chairman! as is well known, the cohesion policy is placing greater emphasis than ever before on an integrated and territorial approach to promote economic and territorial cohesion in the 2014-2020 programming period. as you know, two new instruments - community-led local development (clld) and integrated territorial investment (iti) - were introduced to achieve this objective. in this context, i would like to emphasise that the eip can be used at subregional level to strengthen synergies between local actors in both the public and private sectors, while the iti helps to implement an integrated territorial strategy to allow for the pooling of financial resources from different priority areas. in total, these two instruments will receive 5% of the total cohesion policy funding, or eur 16 billion, in the current programming period, and it is good that the iti will be used by 20 member states, while the erdf will only be used by 18. thank you.
in writing. ‒ regulation (ec) no 184/2005 on community statistics relating to balance of payments (bop), international trade in services (its) and foreign direct investment (fdi) is part of the eu's general rules aimed at monitoring economic developments in all member states and in the union as a whole. the qualitative compilation of such information and its subsequent regular dissemination through various sources (e.g. eurostat) is of fundamental importance for policy-makers, ordinary citizens, businesses and foreign partners alike. the amendment of this regulation seeks to achieve two main issues. first, to increase the transparency and level of detail of statistical data, especially in the case of mb, spt, and cti. secondly, by aligning the regulation itself with the treaty of lisbon, which has been in force for some time. given the frequency with which various data and information gaps are discussed, the current amendments are very important and will hopefully achieve their primary objectives as soon as possible.

in writing. ‒ the requirements laid down in the amended regulation (eu) no 575/2013 (i.e. the capital requirements regulation) apply to both credit institutions and investment firms. under this regulation, commodity dealers (or investment firms whose principal activity is the provision of investment services or activities related to financial instruments) are exempted from the large exposures requirements and from the own funds requirements. the problem and therefore the reason for the need for amendments is that the existing exemptions were set to expire at the end of 2017. given that a review of the regulation of all active investment firms had already been initiated, there were concerns that the institutions of the profile would not be able to fully transition to the new contractual reality in time and adapt accordingly. in view of the above, it was decided to extend the period of validity of the currently established exemptions, while qualitatively completing the review of the investment firm regime and preparing, adopting and applying the legislation that may result from that review. since the current amendments have at least an indirect positive impact on the stable continuation of the activities of the same energy and commodity market players at community level, it was necessary to support them.
