madam president, commissioner, colleagues, the schengen agreement is one of the most important achievements of european integration. the current migrant crisis, however, has severely damaged the possibility of schengen’s further unaltered functioning. every state has the right to promote its security by whatever legal means available, but the introduction of internal border controls within schengen takes place mostly unilaterally, without any serious assessment of the underlining systemic consequences. as a result, should the border controls ever be fully re-established, the eu economy, cooperation and development across many areas shall severely suffer. that, quite frankly, makes the estimated yearly costs of eur 5 to 18 billion a serious understatement. ensuring better protection of the external borders and assisting the member states that suffer the most is absolutely necessary. however, since one of the main reasons for reintroduction of border controls was and still is their thoroughness, revision of internal cross-border security measures is highly warranted. the only one who can fix this situation are the europeans themselves, no deals with rather untrustworthy and unpredictable neighbours will ever do that for us.
chairman! imagine that the bell rings, and two boxers come out onto the pitch. one raises both hands, the other raises one hand, because the other hand is tied behind his back. in this situation, we would all immediately cry out 'this is not fair competition! but, my friends, what do we have in the electricity market today? in the electricity market, in fact, it's precisely what's associated with hydropower. we have different regulations in different countries that apply specifically to this electricity production. the difficulty is that electricity flows freely across national borders, but competition is distorted because the rules are different. so we have different subsidies -- maybe small hydropower plants or large hydropower plants have different subsidies in different countries. we have different permit systems, we have different investment requirements, whether we need fish trails or not, and so on, but electricity flows across borders. it's like a boxer fighting with one arm tied, but the other arm is not tied. so if we want to create a fair and competitive electricity market in europe, we have to create a level playing field for everyone, we have to give the boxers both hands.

in writing. ‒ at the european parliament plenary session in strasbourg, i voted against a resolution which makes the indication of the country of origin or place of provenance mandatory for certain foodstuffs. although this is a welcome initiative in theory, i believe that the resolution also contains hidden risks or underwater stones. there is a concern that by adopting mandatory country of origin or place of provenance for certain food products, we run the risk of making the production process of these products much more expensive, thus seriously undermining the products concerned. the studies show that a large proportion of consumers would indeed support such labelling but would still be against it if it made these products significantly more expensive. i believe that to take such a step would require an in-depth study of the impact on producers and prices, which would consequently affect final consumer prices.

mr president, if one looks at the actual distribution of currencies around the world, then it is clear that an absolute majority rarely moves across native borders for the purpose of trade, investment or anything else – unless numismatics are involved. therefore, in order to engage in trade relations, national currencies have to get exchanged for something else. for the most part that includes the us dollar, the euro, the japanese yen, pound sterling, the australian dollar, the swiss franc, the chinese yuan and the russian rouble. the same situation applies to foreign currency lending: for example, according to the european central bank, in january 2016 most foreign currency loans within the eu itself were in euros, us dollars or swiss francs. households are usually the primary borrowers, with non-financial institutions coming next and financial institutions third. depending on the country, households and non-financial counterparties (nfcs) may change places. for example, most such loans in bulgaria are by nfcs, while households constitute a majority in poland. interestingly enough, in hungary and the czech republic household loans in foreign currency are virtually non-existent. at the same time in the uk, cyprus, ireland and luxembourg, a majority of foreign currency loans are taken by financial institutions. moreover, swiss francs dominate in foreign currency lending in austria, while in poland and greece their share is around half of all such loans. in other words, depending on each country, financial stability, economic growth and other factors, a choice is made in which currency it is more reasonable to take loans. a covered foreign currency loan, such as a loan denominated in a currency other than that of the borrower’s home country, for which repayment terms are prearranged through the use of a forward currency contract, means that an agreement is in place that obligates parties to exchange given quantities of currencies at a pre-specified exchange rate with a certain future date. however, not all foreign currency loans are covered, and this is precisely where an effective regulatory system is necessary in order to create an environment where, for instance, potential borrowers are fully aware of lending specificities, while banks cannot make unsubstantiated demands or change the lending conditions midway through. the principle risk of foreign currency (forex) lending is the potential volatility of the exchange rate involved. for example, since the bulgarian lev and danish crown are linked to the euro, if a bulgarian or danish national takes a loan in euros, the risk of rapidly increasing loan payments is rather low, so one of the only things a person would have to worry about is the stability of their income. if, however, a russian or ukrainian national decided to take a loan in a foreign currency – for example in us dollars or euros – a couple of years ago, the situation may have turned out differently, as is precisely the case now due to several currencies’ depreciation, including the russian rouble and ukrainian hryvnia, over the last two years. of course, there may be serious issues arising from having a common currency, due to financial crises or internal financial instability taking place in one or more states employing the currency, such as greece for example, where the question of the reinstitution of the greek drachma was seriously considered some time ago. for the most part, however, having a common currency is quite beneficial. in the case of foreign currency loans, this is quite true if the common currency has a stable exchange rate in relation to the one in which the loan was made. a couple of months ago, questions for oral answers were submitted to the commission on the matter of foreign currency loans, or more precisely retrospective conversion of foreign currency loans into local currency loans. the issue mostly concerns eu member states outside the eurozone. the main point of such actions is that many people often buy foreign currency denominated cars or have personal loans, thereby putting hundreds of thousands of households at risk of financial problems in case the exchange rate rapidly changes. even more so, quite a few people also have mortgage loans converted into national currencies in addition to their foreign currency debt. conversions therefore may be necessary for the sake of ensuring national financial stability. one of the main matters at hand is that the above—mentioned retrospective conversions take place at non—market exchange rates, thereby raising the question of them being in line with the general principles of eu law, such as freedom of capital movement and freedom of establishment. as a result, these actions may, for example, contribute to damaging investors’ confidence in the internal market. furthermore, the european court of justice stated two years ago that certain foreign currency transactions relating to loan agreements are not yet covered by eu legislation. since is the commission who is responsible for ensuring that eu law is correctly applied, it is absolutely necessary for it to clearly make its position known on the matter of legal compatibility between the practice of retrospective conversion of foreign—denominated loans and the obligations of member states to communicate to the commission any draft regulation not related to the transposition of adopted eu law or international law. in addition, should the above—mentioned ecj statement indeed be true, it is also highly necessary to extend the respective eu legislative coverage to loan agreements currently omitted.
mr president, i would like to thank you for your time. i'm not going to let you down. when i was a kid -- i was looking around the room -- when we were kids, buying a product meant going to the store, taking out money, giving money back, getting the product, going back home. that's how we all used to shop. of course, the world has changed a lot since then, thanks to technological advances. now, very often -- some of us even more often -- we buy a product not by physically going to a store, which may be close to us, but we either sit down at a computer, take out a tablet, or even use our cell phone and buy a product using the internet. but the problem we have is that even though we think we're right there, picking up and buying something, very often the place where we're buying it is not just next to us, it's not even in our country, it's somewhere else in europe. and the difficulty that we know is that even though technology allows us to do this process -- you could say -- effortlessly, effortlessly, there are certain laws or national regulations that actually discriminate against the buyer based on the country in which they are at the time of purchase. thus, the same product may have different selling prices, including different shipping prices, even though the distance does not justify this. this is what i very much welcome - the commission's commitment to eliminate so-called geo-blocking or discrimination, because if we want this single market, the digital market must also be single, so that it is like going to the next store and buying. it's no longer about the weight of the country we're in and the weight of the product we're buying.
mr president, i would like to thank you for your time. i'm not going to let you down. if we want to reduce poverty in europe, the right remedy is to create new jobs. to create jobs, we need investment. so we have to think about the investment climate if we want to reduce poverty. if we want to help the poor who are unable to participate in the labour market, we must use the social assistance systems of our country, of each member state, and these systems can always be improved and developed. but if we want to reduce energy costs, then the drug is competition. if there is a market with competition in any area - whether it is the gas or electricity market - prices will fall. we have a solution to this, ladies and gentlemen, and we have been implementing it for some time now, under the commission's leadership, in specific legislation. it is to develop, to create a european energy union, whose main objective is to develop a single market for both gas and electricity, because when we develop this market and create the necessary interconnections, and improve or reduce regulation so that trade can also take place across national borders, this competition will be created, which will result in an improvement in the quality of supply, a reduction in import dependency and, most importantly, a reduction in the price of electricity and gas for all consumers.

mr president, i would like to thank you for your time. the transatlantic relationship, or the relationship between the european union and the united states, and the eu and canada, is the most active, and arguably the most financial, economic relationship in the world. for example, the data flow between the eu and the us is about 15 terabits per second, more than any other region in the world combined. in the context of the motion for a resolution, it should also be noted that in 2014, imports of digital services from the european union to the united states accounted for 54% of total bilateral trade in services. in other words, the security of transatlantic data flows should be at the heart of eu-us relations, and this is particularly true of the ttip negotiations. but overall, of course, i support this document and i think it is very necessary for the european union.
mr president, i would like to thank you for your time. the reform of the energy system currently being implemented at union level is generally geared towards making it more decentralised, energy efficient, flexible and largely based on non-exhaustible energy sources. and one of the primary goals for us, therefore, would be to provide our citizens with stable, accessible, efficient, sustainable energy. that's number one. the changes to be implemented also mean the possibility for people to produce their own energy as needed, especially in sparsely populated areas, and the freedom to choose the appropriate service providers. finally, in my view, this should actually lead to the abolition of energy monopolies, which in some member states are still far from being implemented. and of course, i support that.
mr president, i would like to thank you for your time. of the european population, 64.6 million women and 57.6 million men are living in poverty. this means that there are not just more women in absolute numbers. they are much more, i would say, predisposed to poverty and social exclusion than men. this applies to virtually all social groups, all ages, all family structures, etc. in this context, it is also worth mentioning the regrettable fact that women find it much more difficult to achieve both their personal and professional lives at the same time, for various reasons. and i agree with the call on the commission to develop as soon as possible a comprehensive, global legislative initiative aimed at directly addressing the needs of mothers and fathers for different types of leave. stereotypically or not, the role of the dominant provider must be paralleled by the role of the full-fledged parent.
in writing. ‒ poverty is a problem that must be addressed by improving the socioeconomic situation in the member states and in the european union as a whole. however, in voting on this particular report, i abstained because i believe that the report included requirements that restrict the member states' right to decide on these important issues themselves. every member state of the european union faces the problem of poverty, but the situation is specific to each member state and i believe that this problem cannot be solved by a 'one size fits all' set of measures. this requirement is contrary to my views and is too specific to be dealt with in the same way across the european union. i believe that each member state should have the right to retain national competence in dealing with this problem.
mr president, i would like to thank you for your time. it is worth noting that for more than 20 years, the reform of the common market has given rise to a single market in which tariff barriers have been successfully removed. unfortunately, non-tariff barriers continue to have a significant impact on trade and the free movement of goods. while it is perfectly understandable that there will always be some bureaucratic burdens, the reduction of bureaucracy, which is one of the main objectives, must also be achieved in the digital market. and the quality of crossborder services is worth noting, since our member states still have very different systems in this respect. this often results in a situation where, say, a recorded letter sent physically by post sometimes arrives faster than one sent electronically.
in writing. ‒ the european union is one of the largest and richest markets in the world, but there are areas that are unfortunately not yet fully unified, and member states have very different practices on a number of issues. i am in favour of the single market in the european union, but i abstained in the final vote on this report because i think it contains too many details and requirements which could be interpreted too broadly. this would in reality directly distort the single market. one of the most visible examples is the principle of 'equal pay for equal work at the same place of work' embedded in the report. in my view, such a principle is very dangerous and would effectively eliminate any competition. maintaining competition in the european union's single market is crucial, as competition is one of the driving forces behind growth.
in writing. ‒ the single market has already been experiencing a high level of unemployment this year, with 22 million europeans of economically active age in absolute terms last year. while much has been achieved in recent years, in particular thanks to the measures taken to overcome the effects of the financial crisis, the real potential of the single market has not yet been realised. the need to work more actively towards their fuller implementation is therefore quite logical and important. reducing bureaucracy is one of the main objectives to be achieved. the abolition or qualitative modernisation of legislation that is genuinely redundant or obsolete must become the cornerstone of this task, which applies both at the transnational and national levels of government for example, in the regulation of vat policy. the role of smes and the further digitisation and innovation of the economy are also important objectives, as is the fight against counterfeiting and the protection of consumer interests, which in the modern era is particularly relevant in the digital environment (e.g. online shopping). the need to improve the quality of crossborder services is also of particular importance, since our member states still have very different systems in this respect. it is also necessary to facilitate cross-border remote work and to deal with unjustified geo-blocking, for example when a youtube video is available in one member state but no longer available in another. in a genuine single market, this is neither acceptable nor logical.
chairman! first of all, i would like to thank the commission for what the commission is doing in terms of aids prevention, and we are doing a lot here in the european union. we have the best medicine in europe, the most advanced technology. we know how we can win this battle. but sadly, the figures show that the highest percentage of those who are sick is among young people. those are terrible numbers. i can tell you this when i talk about my country, latvia, because the biggest percentage increase here is because young people use drugs - first legal drugs, so-called spaisus, and then they use drugs intravenously, which only increases the percentage of aids patients. of course, thanks to the world health organization, for example, in latvia, three great centres have been opened in riga, liepāja and daugavpils, but unfortunately these preventive measures are not available to people in rural areas. and yet, it should be noted that the situation in my country and in the countries of eastern europe is better than it was in the early 1990s after the collapse of the ussr. and yet, speaking globally, it is clear that 90 percent of the aids patients, including children, are in sub-saharan africa (south africa, namibia, botswana), and it should be noted that we must fight against so-called religious cults when sex with an innocent youth is considered [a misconduct] which is also the cause of the spread of aids.
mr president, as many colleagues mentioned, today around 1.5 billion people in the world are living in poverty with severe deprivation in health, education and living standards. most of them are women. policy coherence for development is an important part of the 2030 agenda and should indeed be made an integral part of the eu’s foreign policy, as well as a legally binding set of principles for our member states to follow. we know that the eu is a major – and of course the largest – provider of humanitarian financial expert assistance to the developing countries, but the current migration crisis, and many more like it in other regions, have severely worsened the already drastic situation for hundreds of millions of people. environmental issues such as desertification only make things worse. the intensification of assistance towards guaranteeing food security and the development of the private sector in developing countries, as well as introducing binding rules, will help us.
on behalf of the s&d group. – mr president, the belarusian nuclear power station was first envisaged backed in the 1980s. however, due to the chernobyl disaster in 1986 these plans were halted until less than a decade ago. construction began in 2013 and should cost around usd 10 billion. it must be pointed out that the reactor employed, a water-water energetic reactor, is the same as is currently planned for use in the hanhikivi nuclear power plant in finland. the fears of the international community, especially lithuania, are understandable. belarus has never had a nuclear power plant on its territory, thus raising fears of inability to operate it properly. therefore, in order to alleviate these concerns, belarus should allow for a series of necessary stress tests performed under the auspices of the international atomic energy agency, as well as a comprehensive environmental impact assessment, particularly on the matter of drinking-water sources shared with lithuania. with eu sanctions lifted, relations between the eu and belarus are once again moving forward. i believe, therefore, that a solution to the current issues around the ostrovets nuclear power plant will be found soon, since, in the field of nuclear energy, there can be no other way but multilateral cooperation between all parties involved, directly or indirectly, be that belarus, lithuania, the eu, the russian federation, whose specialists are building the plant, or any other international institution concerned. i want to add that nuclear energy is also environmentally friendly, and with proper maintenance, nuclear plants can last for decades on end.
madam president, for millennia rural areas have acted as the main providers of food and numerous other agriculture-related resources. they also accounted for the major proportion of population and demographic growth. over the years, however, with the advent of the industrial revolution, cities started to attract more and more people from across the country, thereby eventually leading to depopulation of rural areas. nowadays in most eu member states, rural dwellers account for much less than half of the population. whilst these tendencies may have a positive effect on job creation, education, housing or decreasing transportation costs due to usually permanent resettlement from their native places, rural areas essentially lose their future in every sense. it also harms productivity, especially in agriculture and traditional craftsmanship. moreover, whole cultural strata become endangered. central and particularly eastern european member states are the ones suffering most from this trend, even more so after 2004.
in writing. ‒ the conclusion of the world health organisation's general convention on tobacco control was endorsed on behalf of the community by council decision 2004/513/ec. the protocol to combat illicit trade in tobacco products was signed on 20 december 2013. the aim of today's vote was to approve the protocol at union level, subject to certain exceptions. the protocol is an important contribution to international efforts to eradicate illicit trade in tobacco products in all its forms and thus to combat the avoidance of excise duties and customs duties. given the importance of the problem and the need to promote public health protection, including in the latgale region of the republic of latvia, where this phenomenon is quite widespread, it was logical to vote in favour.
in writing. ‒ the purpose of this vote was to support the adoption of a new version of the text of the agreement of the united nations economic commission for europe on the approval of uniform technical prescriptions for wheeled vehicles, equipment and parts. the agreement itself was concluded in 1958, when the industry in question was entering what was perhaps its most active phase of development in the post-war years. the amendments to the revised version 3 will allow for more active promotion of international regulatory cooperation, thus promoting a more transparent, efficient and competitive economic environment, while developing and ensuring a higher level of protection in the field of health and safety. it is worth noting that the existing system and its modernisation efforts also help to reduce the cost of design, production and certification, as well as to avoid administrative duplication of procedures when it comes to bilateral trade agreements in the field under consideration. given the importance of the wheeled vehicle industry to the union's economy, the environment and the health and well-being of its citizens, as well as to several other aspects of our daily lives, it was logical and also necessary to vote in favour.
in writing. ‒ the aim of the vote was to support the conclusion of an additional protocol to the trade agreement between the eu and colombia and peru, to take account of the accession of croatia to the union a few years ago. in other words, given the specific nature of the approval of these additional protocols and the absence of any other amendments, it was logical to vote in favour. i would add that it is almost three years since croatia joined the european union, but there are still many agreements which have not been amended in the light of the community's territorial changes. it is hoped that they will all be adequately modernised in the near future to enable croatia to be fully involved in the functioning of the matters covered by these agreements.
in writing. ‒ this vote concerned a proposal for a council regulation to enable the 18 member states of the european union to establish a platform for closer cooperation in the field of international matrimonial property relations. the purpose of enhanced cooperation shall be to develop judicial cooperation in civil matters with cross-border implications, based on the principle of mutual recognition of judgments, and to ensure consistency between the rules applicable in the member states in relation to conflicts of law in those matters. given the increasing number of international couples registered in the union each year, such a legal framework based on common principles is essential. it is, however, of fundamental importance that nationals of the 10 member states who chose not to participate in that partnership should not find themselves in a legally restricted position in the case of further registration of such a type of couple or in the case of an existing couple.
in writing. ‒ like another vote today, this one concerned the protocol to the who general convention on illicit trade in tobacco products. unlike the first vote, this one was specifically about judicial cooperation in criminal matters, i.e. the community's area of freedom, security and justice. the first vote covered all other forms of cooperation. as noted earlier, this protocol is an important contribution to international efforts to eradicate illicit trade in tobacco products, thus contributing to the fight against the evasion of excise duties and customs duties. it was therefore logical, in view of the positive aspects of such closer cooperation, to vote for the conclusion of the said protocol.
in writing. ‒ as is known, following the recent financial crisis, directive 2014/65/eu and regulation (eu) 600/2014 were adopted, which extended the scope of application to securities markets, investment intermediaries and trading venues. the new rules are therefore intended to strengthen and replace the current but entirely obsolete directive 2004/39/ec. given the structural complexity of the new legislative package, especially in terms of further implementation, a period of 30 months was consistently envisaged for its implementation between its adoption and its entry into force, which is somewhat different from the normal period of 18 to 24 months. in other words, according to today's vote, the new rules will enter into force on 2 january 2017 instead of 3 july this year. as the primary objective of the markets in financial instruments directive is to promote the stability and transparency of eu financial markets and to prevent the emergence of potential crises, it was logical to vote in favour of the commission's amended proposal.
in writing. ‒ this vote amended three regulations (on markets in financial instruments no 600/2014, on market abuse no 596/2014 and on improving securities settlement in the european union and for central securities depositories no 909/2014). the amendments initially included new [future] dates by which the new legislative acts would enter into force. in the light of the comments and comments made in this and the previous report, i agree that a longer period of time is indeed necessary for the entry into force of the updated regulation in order to allow all the parties concerned to fully adapt to the new legislation. therefore, recalling in particular the potential consequences of market abuse and the examples that are fresh in my mind in europe and other parts of the world, i voted in favour.
in writing. ‒ the european court of auditors acts as an independent external auditor, regularly auditing the management of the union budget by the european commission and consistently providing an overall view of the existing financial situation together with recommendations for improvement. although it has no legal authority, the role of the court of auditors in financial control is undeniable. this means that the european court of auditors, with 28 members one from each eu member state , is staffed by the most qualified financial professionals, with a long record of service in their home country, and that its primary task is in fact to monitor the union's finances in a way that safeguards the interests of our citizens, that is, taxpayers . having considered and analyzed mr. shajius' candidacy and having given the utmost consideration to his professional experience and a number of other factors, i have voted in favour.
in writing. ‒ promoting sustainable development is one of the main policy areas of the european union, which is feasible at virtually all levels of its functioning. so, development policy coherence, which includes things like migration policy, trade and finance, private sector development, food security, climate change, security and a number of other things, is very important. i therefore welcome the adoption of the interim report. since effective development policy coherence is not really possible without efforts to correlate with external realities and thus with external partners, it is very welcome that the role of the union delegations in other parts of the world has increased considerably, especially in terms of information. given the importance of development policy for the union's internal and external actions, and the fact that the eu is the world's largest financial donor to developing countries, i support making development policy coherence a legally binding aspect of the policies pursued by the eu and its member states.
in writing. ‒ as you know, the new alliance for food security and nutrition (nafsn) in africa was launched in 2012 under the auspices of the g8 with the aim of enhancing the efficiency of private investment in agriculture and thus improving food security and nutrition in black africa. in particular, the european union is responsible for coordinating the efforts being implemented in côte d'ivoire and malawi. the general idea of nafsn is to implement an agricultural development model in african conditions that includes monoculture, mechanization, biotechnology, reliance on fertilisers, long distribution routes and the cultivation of exported products. the eradication of widespread family poverty is of particular importance in this context, as family farmers and small farms provide more than 60% of employment in this region. in order to achieve the implementation of such a model, particular attention is paid to policy aspects to be implemented such as governance and accountability, regulation of accountability, promotion of sustainable family farming, access to land and security of property rights, regulation of seed material, promotion of gender equality and also financing of investments in african agriculture. africa is considered to have a high development potential, particularly in the agricultural sector. it would therefore be essential to provide the necessary support to the countries of the continent to enable them to pursue these fundamental development efforts on their own, already relying on their own strengths and those of their regional partners. the main threat to the naps is, and will probably continue to be, pollution itself. hopefully, this problem will be solved soon.
in writing. ‒ international accounting standards are a fundamental part of the eu's financial services regulatory and supervisory framework. since 2005, eu listed companies have had to prepare their consolidated financial statements in accordance with these standards. the well-known problem, however, is that these rules have for the most part been in place for more than a decade and thus survived the recent financial crisis , but the much-needed wide-ranging changes in legislation to make these standards more effective have not yet been initiated. the current system of accounting standards is too complex and i agree that it needs to be rationalised, with particular attention to informing users which includes investors, companies and ordinary community residents . it is noteworthy that the full or partial implementation of international accounting standards has taken place in 116 jurisdictions, which makes streamlining efforts very difficult. the introduction of common or at least closely related international accounting standards is a good thing, but not in itself, but as an effective tool to prevent the same financial crises. of course, this is only one of the possible means of combating the factors that caused the crisis, which must be closely coordinated with other means, firstly, the respective efforts of each particular national government.
in writing. ‒ peace support operations, as well as civilian missions, have long been an integral part of the european union's policy towards african countries, especially during the last decade. i therefore agree that it is precisely the combination of diplomacy and security and development efforts that is the most effective option for the eu policy in question. it is important to note that given the limitations of the participation of eu military forces (e.g. in campaigns against the same terrorists), it is of paramount importance to cooperate and coordinate fully with the legitimate governments of the countries of the region and the african union in order to effectively combat these hostile elements. the real strength of the european union, however, is not in peace support operations as such, but in its other efforts to prevent conflict, such as the exchange of expertise, the implementation of reforms, humanitarian aid, etc. of course, the effectiveness of the eu and its partners' action in the waters of the horn of africa should not be overlooked, and we hope that the same will soon be done in the waters of the gulf of guinea, in cooperation with the regional actors. it is also important to note that the vast majority of our member states are also members of nato. consequently, in developing eu cooperation with the un and the african union, the nato factor, without whose indirect support virtually no eu peace support operation abroad is possible, must also be taken into account in a purely military sense (especially in the provision of equipment).
in writing. ‒ i support the ep resolution calling on the european commission to propose a framework regulation at eu level against unfair trading practices in the food supply chain to ensure fair income for farmers and a wide choice for consumers. the law should ensure fair and transparent relations between food producers, suppliers and distributors. more needs to be done to improve the relationship between suppliers and supermarkets, particularly in terms of reducing the so-called fear factor. farmers and smes are particularly vulnerable to unfair trading practices late payments, limited market access, sudden contract breaks, etc. the results of voluntary and self-regulatory systems have so far been 'limited' by the lack of adequate implementation options, the under-representation of farmers, conflicts of interest and the failure of dispute resolution mechanisms to address supplier risk factors.
in writing. ‒ unfair trading practices in themselves have a very negative impact on the relevant aspects of the european union's operations, including, of course, the interests of consumers and also the capacity of smaller market players to develop effectively and participate in the functioning of the market. such practices in the food supply sector, on the other hand, have the potential to endanger the health of the population, which is unacceptable in itself. some 15 years ago in my country - latvia - almost any moderately large city could find a local or regional food company, most of which had only a few or one store. while there is, of course, such a thing as free competition and different market conditions, nowadays the absolute majority of food in latvia is sold in supermarkets or markets of various sizes. without offending anyone in any way, i simply want to emphasise that smaller market players are always the weakest and most vulnerable, especially in the context of unfair trading practices. regulations aimed at combating unfair trading practices operate more or less effectively in individual member states, but there is no single system at union level. i therefore joined the call to the commission to initiate the development of such a system as soon as possible. among other benefits, it could also make a significant contribution to the development of the single market by involving smaller players more actively in the market, under conditions that would maximise the quality of the fight against such unfair practices.
in writing. a number of statistical data and market analysis indicate that unfair trading practices have increased in recent years and are particularly prevalent at certain stages of the supply chain. at least 80% of producers and 96% of the food supply chain have acknowledged being affected by unfair trading. unfair trading practices are particularly harmful to small food producers, including farmers, who are often subject to disproportionate and unworkable contractual terms imposed by large companies. in addition, attention must also be paid to the so-called fear factor, where the weaker party often does not take any legal action and accepts the unfair practice despite the damage, for fear that the stronger party will terminate the trade relationship. it is estimated that suppliers, usually farmers, lose between €30 and €40 billion a year in the eu as a result of unfair trading practices. the ability to obtain differently favourable conditions in a trading relationship is a perfectly natural and widespread phenomenon, but the abuse of a stronger market position is already considered abnormal relations between traders and often leads to the development of unfair trading practices. methods based on voluntary refusal to engage in unfair practices contribute to reducing this problem, but are not sufficient to solve it completely, and consequently consistent measures are needed to eliminate unfair trading practices completely and to guarantee the proper functioning of the market and fair and transparent relations between all actors in the food supply chain.
in writing. ‒ sustainable agricultural development is, in fact, a necessity for the successful existence of any state (except perhaps the vatican). the development of new technologies, investment in innovation, mutual cooperation, exchange of experience, promotion of organic farming and much more form a set of issues that must also be clearly promoted within the eu. of course, particular attention should be paid to the policy on the use of genetically modified organisms in order to avoid any distortion of the market and any risk to the interests and health of consumers. the same applies to the use of chemicals. agree on the need to promote the development of more active agricultural gene banks, the protection and spread of indigenous species, and the promotion of seedlings with a view to making agriculture more profitable. from a governance perspective, it is essential to create a regulatory environment more favourable to innovation and to ensure that union rules do not act as a barrier to innovation. in parallel, more active support for small farmers and businesses needs to be provided at different levels. the same applies to the reduction of administrative burden. it is worth noting that under the framework programme 'outlook 2020' some eur 80 billion is available for research and innovation. a significant proportion of these resources should go directly to the development of the agricultural sector. the key objective is also to provide the necessary skills and to attract knowledge and people who would like to work in the sector.
in writing. ‒ with the world's population demand for food growing much faster than the area of arable land (by only 4.3% by 2050), fostering innovation in agriculture is not simply a necessity but a matter of survival. in parallel with innovation, the competitiveness of eu farmers in relation to other world-class players must be improved. labour productivity, the development of information and communication technologies, the dissemination of the necessary information and data, the promotion of precision agriculture (including drones with the aim of exploiting every centimetre of arable land), and innovation in livestock farming are the aspects of the future management of european agriculture that need to be promoted most actively. in parallel, maximum attention should be paid to issues such as biodiversity conservation, food waste reduction, soil degradation levelling, climate change and efforts to promote cross-sectoral synergies. within the eu, agriculture provides jobs for 25 million people, and these are the only people who work directly with the land. there are even more workers in related industries. in other words, the effective development of agriculture in the member states of the union is not only a matter of economic importance but also a social one, the answer to which is at least partly to be found in the policy of encouraging rational innovation.
the european commission is returning to proven methods, as it did in previous migration crises from morocco to the spanish coast, from libya to the italian coast 15 years ago. or, to pay for migrants to stay outside the european union. ms mogherini also mentioned that the narrative had changed, that the migration crisis was not just a european union issue but a global one. that's right! i think it's also right that instead of aid to north africa, we're giving investment. but the european union's own narrative on migration has also changed significantly. from what we have heard until very recently, all migrants must be guaranteed a place in the european union. that they will all work in the european union economy and even bring unemployed young people from the european union into employment, and that the main - the most important - thing is to distribute all asylum seekers among the member states. and so, many of these same countries are coming together. looking ahead, the main risk remains. criminal activity will not drive out this lucrative and secure business of migration. and it's possible that it's going to move to these camps and projects in north africa or the middle east.
in writing. ‒ at the european parliament plenary session in strasbourg, i voted to set up a committee of inquiry to look into possible irregularities in the so-called 'panama papers' scandal. i believe that we must be firm and uncompromising in the fight against tax evasion and money laundering schemes. the panama scandal proves that this fight must be global. i believe that there is a need for greater transparency with regard to offshore zones and companies incorporated therein. this would help to combat money laundering and tax evasion. the european parliament expresses by this decision its clear willingness to act to put an end to such practices.
in writing. ‒ in the light of recent scandals, firstly luxleaks and the 'panama papers', i fully agree that it is clearly time to set up a committee of inquiry within the european parliament, whose members will examine in detail both the specifics of the aforementioned scandal and any other possible irregularities and administrative errors in the application of union law in relation to the legalization of illicitly acquired funds, tax evasion and tax avoidance. i believe that this committee of inquiry will also be a great complement to the existing taxe2 committee, allowing much more effective consideration of issues that are often similar. it is hoped that the committee will produce a report within the next 12 months which will enable a much wider range of measures to be properly implemented in relation to these types of illegal transactions and offences.
in writing. ‒ the eu-palau agreement on the short-stay visa waiver provides for visa-free travel for eu citizens and palau citizens travelling to the territory of the other contracting party for a maximum of 90 days in any 180-day period. the visa waiver applies to all categories of travellers (holders of ordinary, diplomatic, service/official and special passports) travelling for any purpose other than gainful employment. relations between the eu and palau have been strengthened since the second accession in 2000 to the african, caribbean and pacific group of states. palau is a highly developed democratic republic with an absolutely predominant (86%) service sector in the economy. in the context of the agreement, it is worth noting that the trust in palau visa applicants is high and the rejection rate of visa applications is low. there were no illegal immigrants or asylum seekers from palau in the european union in the same year 2014. the commission's proposal for a regulation on the establishment of a european agency for the operational management of large-scale it systems is based on the principle of subsidiarity.
in writing. ‒ the eu-kingdom of tonga agreement on the short-stay visa waiver provides for visa-free travel for eu citizens and citizens of the kingdom of tonga travelling to the territory of the other contracting party for a maximum of 90 days in any 180-day period. the visa waiver applies to all categories of travellers (holders of ordinary, diplomatic, service/official and special passports) travelling for any purpose other than gainful employment. the kingdom of tonga's economy is based on 60% of the services sector and about 20% of agriculture, especially crab farming and tuna fishing. it is worth noting that, from a geographical point of view, the kingdom of tonga has significant capacity for wind power generation. since the democratic elections in 2010, relations between the eu and the kingdom of tonga have experienced a gradual improvement. the kingdom of tonga does not pose any threat to the european union in the area of illegal migration or in the area of security and public order. the primary migration flows are oriented towards australia, new zealand and the usa. in 2014, the eu recorded the arrest of five tongan nationals staying illegally, issued five entry refusals at the union border and, finally, 10 return decisions. in view of the above and the prospects for the further development of relations with both the kingdom of tonga and the other countries of the region, it was logical to vote in favour.
in writing. ‒ the eu-colombia agreement on the short-stay visa waiver provides for visa-free travel for eu citizens and colombian citizens travelling to the territory of the other contracting party for a maximum of 90 days in any 180-day period. the visa waiver applies to all categories of travellers (holders of ordinary, diplomatic, service/official and special passports) travelling for any purpose other than gainful employment. the agreement reached can be seen as a positive stimulus for further development of relations between the two sides in the economic, cultural and political fields, as well as - which is of fundamental importance - as a factor that will help to combat organised crime more effectively. it is worth noting that the eu is colombia's second largest trading partner, while the number of tourists from both sides is growing rapidly every year. the commission has also received a request from the united kingdom to provide additional information on the status of the third-country national concerned. the agreement also includes a joint declaration on cooperation in the field of illegal migration. in view of the above and the future prospects for the development of eu-colombia relations, particularly in the field of security and the fight against organised crime, it was only logical to vote in favour.
in writing. ‒ under the it agreement, each of its parties (currently 82) abolishes customs duties on all it products covered by it (computers, telephones, as well as raw materials, components and equipment for the production of it goods) and undertakes to set them at zero under the most-favoured-nation regime. last year, an additional list was agreed, including 201 it products. the trade value of these products is approximately usd 1,3 trillion, corresponding to approximately 10% of global trade. in the case of the european union, the eu exports eur 189 billion of products from this additional list, and this figure is expected to increase significantly after the reduction of customs duties. in view of the above and the other benefits that the union could derive from the expansion of trade in information technology products, it was only logical to vote in favour.
in writing. ‒ alpha-pyrolidinopentiophenone, or simply α-pvp, is a synthetic stimulant of the cathinone class developed in the 1960s and still often marketed as a 'designer drug'. this can cause a condition called ' panic delirium', which includes hyperstimulation, paranoia and also hallucinations. while in some countries this substance is controlled or even partially legal, in eu member states such as estonia, finland, france, germany, greece, hungary, ireland, latvia, lithuania, poland, romania, slovenia, sweden, the united kingdom and the czech republic it is completely banned. today's vote was aimed at applying control measures to the distribution of α-pvp in the union. although the abovementioned member states already have much stricter rules in this respect, it was logical to support the draft council decision to provide for uniform rules throughout the union.
in writing. ‒ the international convention on hazardous and noxious substances was concluded in 1996. the ratification of the 2010 protocol under discussion today would, on the other hand, allow the principles of the 1982 un convention on the law of the sea, which focus on the implementation of liability and compensation for damage caused by spills of such substances in world waters, to be fully reflected. it is a pity that this ratification is only now taking place. it is true that there has not been a major environmental and economic recovery in europe since the prestige oil tanker disaster off the coast of galicia in 2002, but given the fundamental importance of the issue, it certainly should have been achieved much earlier. in other words, i voted unanimously to ratify the protocol. i very much hope that this protocol will enter into force as soon as possible in the four years that have elapsed since its ratification at ep level.
in writing. ‒ unlike another vote today on the ratification of the 2010 protocol to the international convention on dangerous and noxious substances, this vote was specifically about the aspects of judicial cooperation in civil matters. active transnational cooperation is essential to tackle such issues, as pollution often occurs in several countries and requires effective cooperation. in view of the importance of the matter referred to above and considered, i voted unanimously in favour.

in writing. ‒ the european union's single market is one of the greatest benefits of the european union and the schengen area. each eu member state benefits from the functioning of the single market, but it is important that its functioning is based on fair and uniform principles, including fair competition in the market. in this respect, the eu's tax policy and the member states' good practices in this regard are of great importance. there are 28 different national tax policies in the european union, with their inherent differences, and it is important that the eu member states continue to maintain their current exclusive national competence in tax law and enforcement. however, ways must be found and more coordinated eu legislation must be developed to reduce the possibility for member states to exploit uncertainties and to prevent these differences in national legislation from distorting competition and the functioning of the single market, which is why i voted in favour of this proposal in the ep plenary.
in writing. ‒ i believe the fight against tax evasion and aggressive tax planning have to be high on the political agenda. i agree that better standards need to be established and certain loopholes need to be closed so as to make sure that corporate taxpayers operating businesses in several countries do not take advantage of disparities and shortcomings in order to reduce their tax bills. nevertheless, i could not support the parliament report ‘rules against tax avoidance practices that directly affect the functioning of the internal market’ and, going along with the ecr group’s position, abstained in the vote. this is because the report seeks to establish a number of rules and practices that may go against its very aim, as well as risk distorting the smooth functioning of the single market. there is also the question of proportionality and the respect of the member states’ sovereignty. to summarise, i believe that the report in its current form, albeit containing some useful measures, is not up to the task of effectively tackling the problem of tax avoidance.

in writing. ‒ at the european parliament plenary session in strasbourg, i supported a european parliament resolution calling for the withdrawal of the commission's decision to allow the cultivation of genetically modified maize in the eu. the european commission should come up with better proposals for authorisation procedures and, through studies, promote more efficient and fairer taxes on genetically modified products and authorisations to place such products on the market. the current proposal does not address the concerns often raised about the potential health and environmental harm of gmos. the impact of products containing gmos on humans and the environment is not yet sufficiently understood and there is too much uncertainty about gm products.
in writing. ‒ this objection relates to the commission's wish to authorise the placing on the market of genetically modified maize bt11 × mir162 × mir604 × ga21 developed by syngenta france sas as a food, food ingredient and feed ingredient. one of the reasons why the european parliament voted against was that the maize mon-øøø21-9 described in that application produces the protein mepsps, which confers resistance to glyphosate herbicides, and that on 20 march 2015 glyphosate was classified as a possible human carcinogen by the international agency for research on cancer. in view of the above, and also of the fact that the commission proposal is not in line with union law, in particular regulation (ec) no 1829/2003 of the parliament and of the council, the amendments to which, following the commission proposal, parliament refused to approve in october 2015, it was not possible to vote on the commission proposal again, and therefore i voted for the ep's rejection decision.
in writing. i have always advocated that latvia and the european union as a whole should be free of genetically modified organisms (gmos). we cannot allow the health of our citizens to be jeopardised in the name of economic interests. it is regrettable that this is the only time that the european commission, contrary to the member states, has given the green light to the placing on the market of new gmo products, using the powers conferred on it by law. gm maize is resistant to glyphosate, which is classified as a possible human carcinogen by the international agency for research on cancer, which specialises in cancer research. this time, the ec not only ignored national concerns about the harmfulness of gmos, but also ignored studies by the world health organization agency on glyphosate as a possible carcinogen. in the light of the studies carried out by the world health organisation agency on glyphosate and the negative attitude of the latvian public towards gmos, i voted to repeal the commission decision authorising the placing on the market of this gmo maize. furthermore, i believe that the existing legislative procedure should be amended to give greater decision-making powers to both the european parliament and the member states when authorising the placing on the market of new gmo food and feed products, thus ensuring more effective democratic control and respect for the public interest.
in writing. ‒ at the european parliament plenary session in strasbourg, i supported the european parliament resolution calling for the withdrawal of the commission's implementing decision concerning the placing on the market of genetically modified carnations (dianthus caryophyllus l., line shd-27531-4). the european commission should come forward with better proposals for authorisation procedures and, through studies, promote more efficient and fairer rates for genetically modified products and authorisations to place such products on the market. the current proposal does not address the concerns often raised about the potential health and environmental harm of gmos. the impact of products containing gmos on humans and the environment is not yet sufficiently understood and there is too much uncertainty about gm products.
in writing. ‒ in accordance with directive 2001/18/ec, the release of genetically modified organisms into the environment is not permitted without a comprehensive analysis of the potential consequences. the problem in this case is that the genetically modified iron involved in the vote has the surb (also) gene, which confers tolerance to sulfonylureas. sulphonylureas, on the other hand, are a widely used second choice for the treatment of type 2 diabetes and their use is associated with, among other things, a higher risk of cardiovascular problems compared to other diabetes medicines. since the creation of a market for sulphonylurea-resistant plants would promote the use of these diabetes medicines as herbicides worldwide, it is simply not acceptable to place on the market this type of gmo which would lead to the use of the medicines for other purposes not related to public health and would potentially have a very detrimental impact on the world's ecosystems, in particular drinking water and biodiversity.
in writing. it is regrettable that this is the only time that the european commission, contrary to the member states, has given the green light to a new gmo product, using its legislative powers. although the commission's authorisation covers only the import, distribution and retail of gmo grapes for purely decorative purposes, there are serious concerns that the placing of gmo grapes on the market will not only endanger the environment and human health, but also have a detrimental impact on biodiversity and chemically contaminate drinking water. it is recognised that, although gmos would not have enhanced survival properties if released into the environment by seeds, pollen or rooted plants, they would still retain resistance to certain herbicides which are highly toxic to plants and animals even at very low doses. the use of such herbicides can have a corrosive effect on the productivity of other crops and on the structure of the natural plant community and the wild food chain. it is also not possible to exclude the spread of gm plants in the environment, for example in gardens, by grazing or composting. in view of the potential risks to the environment and human health and the negative attitude of the latvian public towards gmos, i voted for the repeal of the commission decision authorising the placing on the market of these gmos. furthermore, i believe that the existing legislative procedure should be amended to give greater decision-making powers to both the european parliament and the states when granting authorisations for the placing on the market of new gmo food and feed products, thus ensuring more effective democratic control and respect for the public interest.

on behalf of the s&d group. – mr president, in 2009 the lisbon treaty created the european parliament we know today, although already since 2006 it had been progressively included in more and more negotiations and consultations on numerous important matters, such as the second schengen information system for example. the point of retelling these public and well-known facts was simply to point out one rather simple matter: even though over the last decades all of the union’s institutions have evolved and gained new powers and responsibilities, parliament has undoubtedly evolved the most. today it is by right amongst the top four eu institutions in terms of power, the others being the commission, the council of ministers and the european council. in fact, in a majority of cases when important decisions are concerned, parliament has to agree to their implementation, not to mention that quite a few of them may get amended in the process as well. in this instance, it specifically applies to the conclusion of international agreements, something that the lisbon treaty has brought to the table. moreover, not only does the parliament in this respect possess the power of the consent procedure, it also has to be provided with comprehensive information on the process leading to the conclusion of an agreement in question, meaning that, even though parliament can ask for specific information when such a need arises, it should be given to it of the other involved institutions’ own accord. in other words, i fully support the implementation of additional and the reinforcement of the currently existing measures aimed at a fully functional operation of the system, whose goal is to inform parliament on the negotiation process of international agreements. it is, of course, fully understandable that, depending on each and every particular case, information may not be fully available for various reasons. however, the points mentioned in the debated oral question should indeed be a bare minimum. the third country in question, for example, the type of agreement discussed, the stage of the procedure and the relevant documents pertaining to the negotiations. parliament does not legally have the power of legislative initiative, something that sets it far apart from the national parliaments in our member states. but then again, the european union itself is not a federation or a single state, and parliament is essentially still a consultative assembly. however, it is a consultative assembly with some very advanced powers unlike those of any other similar institutional body around the world, including the ability to ask the commission to draft the necessary legislation. so, in this respect, parliament even has a de facto power of legislative initiative. committee and delegation hearings and non-binding resolutions have an indirect, albeit quite powerful, influence on the union’s foreign policy. for example: approval of overseas development grants, eu enlargement etc., not to mention the non-binding quotes on new eu treaties, even without possessing the respective veto powers. all of this makes the parliament an actor to be taken into utmost consideration. to put the overall idea a bit more simply, thanks to the enormous legitimacy gained through european elections directly from the eu citizens themselves, and due to the long-term constant expansions of various powers through the eu treaties, parliament has the right to be constantly kept in the loop of essentially every eu-level and international development that concerns the european union, which undoubtedly also includes information provision concerning negotiations on international agreements involving the eu.
