madam president, this week we will vote on the fotyga report which accuses russia of engaging in propaganda against the west. but where is the condemnation of anti-russian propaganda? one recent example: on 11 november 2016 my country, latvia, celebrated bear slayer’s day, which commemorates the victory of latvian independence fighters in 1919 over the troops of german-backed warlord pavel bermont-avalov. on 14 november the financial times europe published an article saying that bear slayer’s day honoured latvian freedom fighters who secured independence against russian forces. this year, us troops were in riga for the commemoration — part of nato’s attempt to reassure the baltic states in the face of a ‘renewed threat from the big bear to the east’ (end of quote). but bermont-avalov did not represent any russian government, neither that of imperial russia nor that of soviet russia. his idea was to create a pro-german baltic duchy. the blatant russophobic propaganda based on false historical facts must be punished, a serious manifestation of racism and xenophobia. we must not allow historic misrepresentation to stand in the way of reconciliation.
madam president, i would like to thank you for your time. you guys! all over europe we have a problem or a challenge with so-called officers. offshore is a way for individuals to hide their income. whether to launder illicit funds, to finance terrorism, or simply to avoid paying honest taxes. we have adopted a law - a european law - a directive which will create a so-called beneficial ownership register, which will show who the individuals behind any company are throughout the european union. it is a very serious instrument to help fight against this illicit money. but, colleagues, we must also remember that when we fight money laundering, when we fight the financing of terrorism and when we fight tax evasion, we must always weigh in on what we do to promote this fight, but we must not unnecessarily infringe on the right to privacy of individuals. there is no written rule about where to draw the line, but it is important to realize that we must always weigh the matter in the balance. because we have to make illegal activity even more difficult at the same time, we have to make it so that it's not easy to avoid paying taxes, but we can't go into the second trap, which is to simply ignore the interests of, let's say, the 99 percent of the population who are really honest and do everything the right way. thank you for your attention.

madam president, i would like to praise vice—president mogherini for the great work done by the small team east stratcom in the external action service. i would also like to praise ms fotyga for her excellent report. i believe that the very small amount of money spent on the work of the team is some of the most efficiently used in the eu budget. however, the strategic communication efforts remain seriously underfunded and understaffed. countering the hybrid information operations is critical for preserving our democracies, and indeed our union. i therefore call on vice—president mogherini immediately and significantly to increase the resources allocated to this task, particularly to the task of countering the billions of propaganda dollars spent by putin’s agencies.
madam president, i would like to thank you for your time. my fellow citizens! so far, i think the european union has an unwavering moral position that propaganda can be fought with facts and objectivity. when i saw this report, i knew i was wrong. this is what real propaganda looks like! one report linked the terrorist organization to a particular country as a brilliant propaganda device. the main objective is to make it clear that russia and daesh are one and the same. another propaganda tactic is to make it seem that the problem is confined to one country. except for daesh and russia, no other examples are mentioned, although the title of the report refers to third parties. does this mean that no one in the world uses propaganda anymore? i doubt that very much. therefore, the report cannot be described as objective and comprehensive. we are being offered a low-quality propaganda product which, if adopted, will discredit the european union. and if parliament votes in favour, then in the next report among those who use propaganda, we can safely mention the european union.
thank you madam president. in my opinion, all red lines are crossed in the texts under discussion here. the enormous number of resolutions adopted by this parliament critical of russia and the russian people has remained in my memory. but a resolution so offensive in both form and content is a new development for the european parliament. the very principle of freedom of expression and information is challenged in this resolution. russia is accused of – and i quote – ‘aggressively employing a wide range of tools and instruments, such as think tanks and special foundations [...], special authorities [...] multilingual tv stations [...]’ – end of quote. but every state has its think tanks, special foundations, special authorities dealing with compatriots living abroad, and its tv stations. it is impossible to understand what happens if you watch only channels like cnn, bbc, euronews, tdf and france24. for example, the coverage by those channels of events in aleppo and mosul is based on evident double standards, so the russia today channel effectively contributes to the principle of media pluralism.

in writing. ‒ over 400 000 europeans die prematurely every year due to the bad quality of the air they breathe. this is why the european parliament passed a new law to reduce the ceiling of dangerous pollutants allowed in eu member states. i backed the new law because it will save lives and also public money: national governments pay between eur 300 and eur 900 billion every year in health-related costs.
in writing. ‒ it is important to establish global standards and principles for the prudential regulation of banks. banks need to be well capitalised in order to support the real economy, reduce systemic risk and avoid any repeat of the enormous bailouts witnessed during the crisis.
in writing. ‒ european countries have to decide whether european countries need to create common army or not. the lisbon treaty creates the possibility to take some steps in this direction. i support steps to eliminate duplication, overcapacity in defence and security and barriers to defence procurement. but i am against imposing an obligation on member states to spend 2% of gdp for defence.
in writing. ‒ russia has been engaged in anti-western propaganda for some time. this is most evident in the russian border states that were formerly part of the soviet union, including latvia. this propaganda is nothing more than social disinformation designed to divide society. i believe that the european parliament's resolution is a step in the right direction to combat this propaganda, which is why i supported it in the plenary. the first step in successfully fighting propaganda is to be aware that such an information war is being waged and to inform the public about it. the european union must take appropriate steps to counter the kremlin's heavy artillery of disinformation, but people themselves must assess the information, its content and its objectivity.
in writing. ‒ parliament voted against a low-quality propaganda product that discredits the european union. one report linked the terrorist organization to a particular country as a brilliant propaganda device. the main objective is clear: to show that russia and daesh are one and the same. so far, i think the european union has an unwavering moral position that propaganda can be fought with facts and objectivity. when parliament voted on this report, i realised that i was wrong.
on behalf of the ppe group - the president! you guys! imagine that our goal is not just to run a marathon, but to run a marathon in under three hours. if we want to achieve this goal, there are certain things that we simply won't do: we won't drink beer every day, we'll stop smoking. we know we can't eat just frozen dumplings. if you want to achieve your goal, you have to be consistent in your actions and you have to stick to those actions, otherwise you will not achieve your goal. in the european union, we have set ourselves the goal of creating a so-called energy union - like running a marathon. the energy union has several objectives at once: to reduce import dependency, to reduce co2 emissions and to increase competition between energy suppliers to ensure that consumers have the lowest possible prices. so it is absolutely incomprehensible that three things are happening or not happening on the commission's part that are actually delaying this energy union. the first is what happens to the peace? will there be a peace treaty with gazprom or not? gazprom has long abused its dominant position by making eastern europe pay much higher prices for gas. we read in the press that a peace agreement is possible, where he doesn't have to pay anything at all. what's that? how does that compare to our goals? the second is this nord stream 2 pipeline. the european commission, all the time you said: 'well, it has to be relevant, it has to be relevant...' he's not fit for purpose! clear and bright, black and white, it must be said! and third, the expansion of the opel gas pipeline for gazprom. all three of these things are working against our goals of achieving the energy union. it's like a marathon runner smoking every day instead of training.

in writing. ‒ at the european parliament plenary session in strasbourg, i voted against the resolution on eu-turkey relations, because the main message or call of this resolution is to freeze the accession negotiations with turkey. i believe that such a decision is shortsighted, given turkey's role in stopping the uncontrolled flow of migrants. although there are still human rights violations in turkey, i believe that this step was not the right one from a strategic point of view. the european union and turkey have an agreement that turkey will help stop and control the flow of illegal refugees, but the european union will speed up turkey's accession talks, and i therefore believe that this resolution does not send the right signal and incentive to the turkish side to implement this agreement.
in writing. ‒ since the attempted coup the turkish authorities have arrested ten members of the turkish grand national assembly belonging to the opposition party hdp and some 150 journalists; 2 386 judges and prosecutors and 40 000 other people have been detained, of whom more than 31 000 remain under arrest; 129 000 public employees either remain suspended (66 000) or have been dismissed (63 000), according to the commission’s turkey 2016 report. but we are very polite and careful. why? because erdoğan can open the doors and allow millions of migrants to go to the eu, but the eu still cannot adopt a common position about migration policy.

in writing. ‒ the current vat system, in particular as it is applied by large corporations to cross-border transactions, is vulnerable to fraud. according to a commission study, mtic fraud alone is responsible for a vat revenue loss of approximately eur 45-53 billion annually. the estimated 'vat gap' amounts to around eur 170 billion annually, and better digital technologies are becoming available to help close this gap. administrative cooperation among member states in fighting vat fraud is still not efficient when it comes to coping with intra-eu vat evasion and fraud mechanisms, or to managing cross-border transactions or trading. i support the call on the commission to carry out more monitoring visits to member states, selected on a risk basis, when assessing administrative cooperation agreements.

my dear colleagues! i'm not going to let you down. one of the objectives of sustainable development is to ensure that all people have access to affordable, reliable, sustainable and modern energy services. african countries are currently facing huge energy supply constraints that are severely hampering their development. the continent of africa is developing rapidly and is experiencing a demographic explosion. together with weak education and health systems, the underdeveloped energy supply creates a vicious circle of poverty from which it is very difficult for africans to escape. colleagues, even a relatively small investment in energy supply can bring enormous economic benefits and significantly improve the living and working conditions of many people on the african continent. it's not just charity on the european side. sustainable development in africa will help mitigate the migration crisis and create new opportunities for our businesses in the future. the european union's energy development initiative aims to provide an additional 500 million people with access to sustainable energy by 2030. half a billion people! and will provide eur 3.5 billion in energy development assistance from 2014 to 2020. we have a goal and money. it is therefore important that the 30 programmes envisaged work accurately and efficiently. here, parliament, i mean the european parliament and the commission, must work together to prevent problems and coordinate our efforts to achieve our objectives. it is therefore important for members to get the commission's views and to learn in good time about the difficulties of implementing programmes in the future.

on behalf of the ppe group - the president! commissioner, please! this initiative by the european commission is perhaps the biggest policy initiative of the last decade. its importance cannot be underestimated. what exactly has driven our energy policy so far over the last 15 or even 20 years? it's been climate policy. the goal of reducing harmful emissions of co2 or climate change is what we do as human beings. and because of this policy, we've subsidized renewable energy, and these technologies have evolved to the point that we now have wind turbines, solar turbines, and so on, that we have a viable alternative to fossil fuels. with this initiative, we will in fact be adding to our policy arsenal by introducing another principle that could be peacefully interpreted as price competition. now we're also going to think about how to make sure that the consumer and the industry have the lowest prices possible, and the answer to that, and the way we're going to do that, is to introduce more competition, by allowing the market to determine what capacity there should be in the space. we know that subsidies have been necessary up to now, but now on the eve of the day we have to look at where and how we can maybe step back from that and let the market take over what the subsidies have started because the market has matured for it.

in writing. ‒ the european union sees far beyond its borders, but often fails to see what is happening at home. what are the reasons for this shortsightedness? i think there are two: the inability to see and the unwillingness to see. the eu's lack of visibility was revealed this year when it emerged that there were no eu institutions that could independently and professionally identify problems in hungary and poland. before joining the eu, candidate countries had to meet the copenhagen criteria. once a country has joined the eu, it turns out that no one looks at whether it still meets these criteria. the eu treaties state that the member states share common values, including human rights. if we don't even care about what's happening in the eu, it means we're overreacting and human rights are not our shared values. latvia has a large population of former soviet citizens who live in collective status and have not been able to vote in parliamentary, european parliament or local elections for a quarter of a century. if such a situation were to arise in a non-eu country, the european parliament would certainly adopt a damning resolution. because it's an eu member state, there's a silence. because the eu has an unwritten rule: you won't see my problems, and i won't see yours, but we'll show our principledness in our external actions. this arch needs to be repaired. let's not talk about activities for now, but at least practice spotting problems.

in writing. ‒ europeans and politicians still see europe as the centre of the world, which can dictate its rules to other countries and regions of the world. that is no longer the case, and practitioners who encounter the supernatural on a daily basis can attest to that. the number of instruments of influence for european foreign policy is very limited. we still have strengths in technology, education, an inclusive internal market, influence in international organisations, and the ability to finance development projects. but here too, europe is losing its influence with each step. how quickly europe loses its position to other great powers and where the new balance will eventually be will depend on how well european countries manage to overcome their differences and establish a common, truly functioning european foreign policy. this is the main question we must ask ourselves in this report too. the other questions, i think, are secondary.
in writing. europe is currently facing unprecedented external and internal challenges, including interstate conflict, terrorism, hybrid threats, cyber and energy insecurity, organised crime and climate change. the citizens of the european union (eu) are exposed to threats of terrorism, mass flows of refugees and disinformation campaigns designed to divide society. all of this directly affects the well-being of our citizens, which is why we need a foreign policy that is able to defend our interests and values. i supported the resolution, which calls, among other things, for eu member states to build mutual trust and the ability to speak with one voice in response to developments in foreign policy. the eu can only be a strong global partner on a par with other major powers if all member states are united and act together in pursuing a strong eu foreign and security policy. latvia is directly experiencing the fact that russia continues to ignore the sovereignty of its neighbours and openly challenges the peace and security order in europe and the world. the kremlin's official propaganda portrays the west as an enemy and actively seeks to undermine european unity. i believe that sanctions have been and are an effective foreign policy tool and should therefore be maintained as long as russia continues to violate international law. i support the view that the eu's foreign policy should be based on three pillars: diplomacy, development and, of course, defence. in particular, it must be made clear to the russian government in a clear and unambiguous manner that its actions in ukraine and its air maneuvers in the baltic states will not go unnoticed.
in writing. ‒ the european union does not currently have a genuine safety net to curb market volatility. noting that farmers will be increasingly exposed to price volatility, the european parliament deemed it necessary to adopt a more incisive and coherent policy, with targeted instruments at eu and national level. the european parliament has underlined the importance of maintaining decoupled direct aids under the current cap, together with the single area payment scheme, which constitute compensation for public services and a vital component in securing the income of farmers and providing them with a degree of financial stability.

mr president, the customs union is a cornerstone of the european union and is essential for the proper functioning of the single market. on 1 may 2016, traders in the european union could see the largest change to european customs procedures in the past 20 years with the introduction of the union customs code. the new legislation aims to facilitate legitimate rate, increase safety and security and rationalise, harmonise and standardise the application of custom controls and decision-making processes across europe. under the union custom code, all communications between custom authorities and businesses will have to be electronic, with the goal of creating a completely paperless custom environment across the eu. it is important to have an independent impact assessment to ensure that the regulatory framework of the eu customs policy is effective, proportionate and fit for purpose, both for member states and for trade operators. the european commission has to step up efforts to create more uniform custom electronic requirements and risk assessment programmes at eu level in order to ensure that the arrival, transit and exit of goods in and out of the eu is registered as effectively as possible. and of course, dear colleagues, merry christmas and happy new year.
